The windowed swapchain path is gated by `aqua-verify/tests/window_memory_budget.rs` (`RENDERER_WINDOWED`: 140 MiB private over the bare process, 4 MiB framework heap, 1 MiB steady growth, 64 MiB local video memory; 2560x1440 swapchain, editor workload, 600 presents; measured on the RX 9070 XT, debug test build: 107 MiB private, 0.0 MiB steady growth, 48.7 MiB video memory, frame CPU p99 1.4 ms). The whole-application gate is `tools/compare-editors.sh`, which fails the run when the settled private bytes exceed 142 MiB or dedicated GPU memory exceeds 64 MiB and prints the GPUI comparison. Measure with a uniquely named executable per run (`win-drive.ps1` does): per-thread context-switch counters are keyed by process name, and a shared name mixes in unrelated processes (idle Aqua read 2600 switches/s that way, 9.5/s once isolated). Transients are aliased and sized to effect bounds (the planner sizes layer transients to their bounds, record 14.10, so a 200x40 px group costs 64 KB instead of 29 MiB; the D3D12 executor consumes that sizing, but no end-to-end windowed measurement exists); the atlas is an 8-bit texture grown in pages with eviction. The adapter responds to video memory budget changes and frees transient heaps when minimized.

Implemented residency (`aqua-media`, verified against `aqua-gpu-ref` on WARP and a hardware adapter): one `Atlas<K>` per texel format (8-bit mask for glyphs and single-colour paths, RGBA8 for images, SVG and gradient paths). Shared pages grow 256, 512, 1024 up to a page cap; items larger than a quarter of the maximum edge get a dedicated exact-size page. Allocation is shelf packing without a CPU mirror. When a budget or the page cap is hit, the least recently used page that holds nothing used in the current frame is reset (shared) or released (dedicated), so a page can never be overwritten while an earlier draw of the same frame still reads it; if every page is in use the atlas grows past budget and counts it. `trim_idle` releases pages idle for N frames (minimize, memory pressure), and the D3D12 side frees the matching `ID3D12Resource` behind the frame fence. Large uploads are split into row bands and streamed under a per-frame byte budget. Paths and SVG are rasterized on the CPU (tiny-skia, resvg) into the atlas, not rendered by a GPU path pipeline; Slug-style rendering for scaled text and animated paths is not built. After device recovery the host must call `Residency::clear` between frames (it drops every page, pending update, streaming queue and retained vector source, and bumps `Residency::generation`), and reset any `PageTable`; the next frame re-creates and re-uploads whatever it draws, so a recovered device never sees a release or write for a page it does not have. Glyphs go through `Residency::glyph_any`, which keeps the 8-bit mask and the RGBA color-glyph atlases apart.

Pure crates get an exact heap gate: `aqua_verify::heap::CountingAllocator` counts allocations, reallocations and bytes per thread plus live and peak bytes process-wide, and `HeapGate` fails a test on any excess. Gates in force (`aqua-verify/tests/heap_gates.rs`): an idle `Runtime::build_frame` performs zero heap events at 100, 1000 and 4000 rows and shapes, measures and emits nothing; an idle `RenderSide::step` with 10 and 5000 scene items performs zero heap events and requests no wake; `emit_scene_diff` of an unchanged 2000-item scene allocates nothing; a cold mount shapes each text run exactly once. The earlier `aqua` perf_scene failure (12 allocations per idle frame) does not reproduce on the extracted runtime.

Whole-host heap budget (decided in round 5, status note): the 4 MiB framework heap figure above is the renderer-side budget and stays the gate for `RENDERER_OFFSCREEN` and `RENDERER_WINDOWED`. The full host in `aqua-verify/tests/host_window.rs` (400-row document at 2560x1440, release) measured a 13.2 MiB framework heap, which includes the UI tree, layout, shaped text runs and the scene, and was gated by `HOST_WINDOWED` at 16 MiB with the other fields equal to `RENDERER_WINDOWED` (round 6 measured 9.5 MiB after the interaction-store fix and tightened the gate to 12 MiB, section 11.4) (140 MiB private, 1 MiB steady growth, 64 MiB video memory). This is a deliberate loosening of the original application-heap allowance (about 4 MiB) to a measured figure plus slack, not a claim that the 4 MiB target is met by the host; tightening it needs a per-subsystem heap breakdown, which does not exist.

**Windowed framework heap decision (round 5, 2026-11-04; superseded by 12 MiB in round 6, section 11.4):** 16 MiB for the full host, 4 MiB for the bare renderer. The 4 MiB figure counts only renderer-side state (`RENDERER_OFFSCREEN`, `RENDERER_WINDOWED`). The full host also holds the document, scene, layout, fonts and shaped text, measured at 13.2 MiB for the 400-row document, so `HOST_WINDOWED` (`aqua-verify/src/budget.rs`, gated by `tests/host_window.rs`) is the renderer budget with a 16 MiB framework heap and everything else unchanged. 4 MiB is rejected for the host because it is below what the document and fonts alone occupy; 16 MiB leaves about 20% headroom over the measurement.

**Editor comparison, release builds, 3 runs, 600 scrolls, 12 s idle (round 5, `tools/compare-editors.sh`, medians; verdict in `shots/compare/verdict.txt`):**

| Metric | Aqua Editor port | GPUI Editor |
|---|---|---|
| Scroll UI-thread latency p50 / p90 / p99 / max (ms) | 0.12 / 6.22 / 29.6 / 137 | 0.06 / 0.18 / 0.68 / 1.90 |
| Scroll samples over 16.7 ms / over 50 ms (of 600) | 5 to 12 / 2 | 0 / 0 |
| Idle CPU (% of one core) | 11 to 12.3 | 5.0 |
| Idle context switches per second | 417 | not collected |
| Idle UI-thread latency p99 (ms) | 0.45 | 0.57 |
| CPU per scroll input (ms) | 5.42 | 2.63 |
| Private MiB, settled | 152 | 115 |
| Working set MiB | 146 | 118 |
| GPU dedicated MiB | 91.5 | 53.6 |
| Threads | 26 | 36 |
| Aqua frame CPU avg / worst (ms) | 0.07 / 2.54 | n/a |

The scroll cadence is now a recorded gate: in release builds the report fails when scroll p50, p99 or the count over 16.7 ms is worse than GPUI by more than 10% plus a 0.5 ms (p-values) or 1 sample slack, and it writes the verdict next to the logs; debug builds record the numbers but do not fail on scroll. Current verdict: the 142 MiB private gate, the 64 MiB GPU gate, scroll p99, scroll samples over 16.7 ms, idle CPU and settled private memory all FAIL against GPUI; scroll p50, probe timeouts, idle latency and idle context switches pass. The framework-level frame cost is 0.07 ms average, so the stalls are in the Editor port's application work or wake sources, not the renderer.

### 11.2 Editor memory breakdown and caps (round 5, 2026-11-04)

Measured on the RX 9070 XT, release `editor-aqua.exe` under `win-app` (window client 1898x1125 physical, 2 BGRA8 swapchain buffers), settled, `AQUA_MEMORY_REPORT=1`. The Editor has two reachable states depending on whether the Forge answers: the "Choose a Forge" picker (Forge down) and the landing screen with sidebar and composer (Forge up). The compare harness measures whichever it gets, which is why its numbers flip between runs.

| Metric | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Landing, private MiB | 136.8 to 139.5 | 136 to 138 (gate 142: passes) |
| Landing, GPU dedicated MiB (perf counter) | 80.5 | 77.7 release; 75.7 on the debug probe build with the image reduction below (final release not re-measured: the Forge went down) |
| Picker, private MiB | 114.4 to 115 | 113.6 to 114.7 |
| Picker, GPU dedicated MiB | 66.9 (64.9 on some runs) | 66.1 |
| Before first `Present` (device and window, nothing presented), private / GPU MiB | 72.3 / 63 | unchanged (driver floor) |

GPU dedicated, landing screen (perf counter 77.7 MiB, of which DXGI local usage is 48.2 MiB):

| Allocation | MiB | Controlled by |
|---|---|---|
| Swapchain, 2 x 1898x1125 BGRA8 | 17.1 | window size, flip model minimum of two buffers |
| Transient heap (backdrop blur and glass layers) | 6.4 | `aqua-gpu-d3d12::scene::transients`, planner bounds |
| Atlas, RGBA8 pages | 6.9 before, 5.2 after | `aqua-media`, see below |
| Upload ring and scene upload | 1.2 | `aqua-gpu-d3d12` |
| Driver memory DXGI reports but Aqua did not create | 15.8 to 16.5 (14.6 measured on the bare 640x400 test window; 0.1 on WARP) | vendor |
| Driver memory the perf counter reports and DXGI does not | 29.5 (77.7 minus 48.2) | vendor |

The atlas holds 19 icon vectors (22x22) on one page, five dedicated pages for the composer tab-card glass paths (998x188, 1018x253 twice, 1016x251 twice: 5.0 MiB, one RGBA page per path) and two dedicated 460x460 pages for full-resolution avatars drawn at about 20 px (1.7 MiB). The vendor floor plus the swapchain is 29.5 + 14.6 + 17.1 = 61.2 MiB, so the 64 MiB gate leaves 2.8 MiB for transients, atlas and upload rings together. With the Editor's glass design the gate is not reachable from the caches: the transient heap (6.4) and the per-path RGBA rasters (5.0) are the two remaining items above the floor, and neither belongs to a cache. Neither is solved in this round; the gate stays at 64 MiB and is recorded as failing for the landing screen (77.7) and, by 2.1 MiB, for the picker (66.1).

Rust heap, landing screen, release: 25.8 MiB allocated, 32.7 MiB committed (boot 5.5, picker 20.5). Accounted components from `AQUA_MEMORY_REPORT` (`aqua-lowering-heap`): Slug outline cache 0.04, Slug store CPU mirror 0.05 and GPU texture 0.13, retained vector sources 0.02, retained decoded images 1.7 (two 460x460 avatars), residency streaming queue 0, measure cache under 0.4 (4096 entries per generation, two generations, 24 bytes of key and value each). A sampling allocator (`probe-allocator` feature, debug build; `aqua-heap-sites` in the report) attributes the live allocations of 4 KiB and more: UI tree 0.9, transport ring 0.45, UI arena 0.4, slotmaps 0.3, Slug store 0.1, scene 0.05, fonts database 0.03, the rest below that. Allocations under 4 KiB are 8.3 MiB (4.2 up to 256 bytes, 4.0 up to 4 KiB), those from 4 KiB to 1 MiB 17 MiB in the probe build, which includes the probe's own symbolizer cache; the debug build is not a faithful size, so only the ranking is trusted. The heap is dominated by many small allocations from the UI tree, layout and shaped text rather than by any cache, and no single subsystem exceeds 1 MiB at 4 KiB granularity. Private bytes at the landing screen add up as: pre-present floor 72.3 (device 43, process and runtime about 22, heap about 7), first `Present` +43, heap growth to the landing screen about +20, other +2.

Caps added this round:

- The shared pages of the RGBA atlas open at 256 and never exceed 512 (masks keep 1024); anything wider than 256 gets an exact-size dedicated page. 20 icons now cost 0.25 MiB instead of 1 MiB, and the 4 MiB 1024x1024 shared page that held two items can no longer appear. Budgets: RGBA atlas 24 MiB (was 32), 8-bit atlas 8 MiB (was 16; with Slug text the mask atlas is empty in the Editor).
- Images drawn much smaller than their source upload a box-filtered copy (`aqua_media::reduce`: level k when source >> k is still at least the draw size, at most 8 levels) keyed by `(layer, level)`; the full-resolution copy is uploaded only when something draws it large. `Lowering::release_image` drops every level. The 460x460 avatars drop from 0.85 MiB each to 0.03 MiB on the GPU.
- The CPU Slug outline cache has a 4 MiB budget (`SlugCache::with_budget`); when a lookup would exceed it, the cache is cleared and refills. The GPU Slug store keeps its own 4 MiB budget.
- The layout measure cache (`aqua-layout::measure`) is two generations of at most 4096 entries; entries touched in the previous generation are promoted, the rest drop on the next rotation. The host now clears it on memory pressure and when a window is occluded (`Runtime::request_trim`, `request_trim_all`).
- Trim policy: idle atlas pages are released after 600 rendered frames without use (was 1800), still only on a quiet frame every 300 rendered frames. Idle windows render no frames, so the old figure meant pages from a finished animation lived until the next long animation. Round 6 adds the wall-clock idle trim that covers exactly that case (section 11.4).
- The Editor image budget (`ImageAssetBudget::editor()`): decoded 32 MiB (was 64), pending 16 MiB (was 32), GPU 24 MiB (was 32).

Tests: `aqua-layout` (five measure-cache tests: bound, promotion, expiry, clear), `aqua-media` (`reduce`, small first page, shared pages at most 512), `aqua` (`images_drawn_small_upload_a_reduced_copy_and_release_every_level`), `aqua-text` (`outline_cache_stays_within_its_budget`, runs on Windows, passes). Pure ones also run on the Linux build host.

Measurement caveat: the Editor under `win-app` is placed at the bottom of the z-order and Aqua stops presenting an occluded window, so some runs stay in the pre-present state (72 MiB private, 63 MiB GPU). A run counts only when private is above 100 MiB.

### 11.3 GPU dedicated memory below the 64 MiB gate (round 6, 2026-11-04)

Measured with `AQUA_MEMORY_REPORT=1` and the `win-app` `gpumem` probe (perf counter `\GPU Process Memory(pid)\Dedicated Usage`) on the RX 9070 XT, landing screen of the Editor port, window client 1898x1125 physical, debug-profile port build (the allocations below do not depend on the profile), `AQUA_RENDER_WHEN_COVERED=1`. After 600 wheel steps over the sidebar the figure did not move (63.9 before, 63.9 after, two runs; 63.7 in an earlier build); the thread view with a large thread needs the benchmark fixture and was not measured.

| Step | GPU dedicated MiB | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Round 5 landing | 77.7 | breakdown in 11.2 |
| Composer glass without RGBA atlas pages (5 path pages and the avatars gone from the atlas) | 71.0 | atlas resident 6.9 to 0.8 MiB |
| Backdrop blur without snapshot and full-resolution output (transient heap 6.4 to 1.2 MiB) | 66.0 | A/B with the old planner path in the same build: 71.0 against 66.0 (the 3 level threshold was measured last: 63.9 with transient heap 1.2 MiB) |
| `Core` shader-visible descriptor heap 69,632 to 2,112 descriptors | 63.7 | `AQUA_SRV_STATIC=4096` alone gave 64.1 (-1.9 MiB); the production renderer never uses this heap, only the HAL tests do |

What was changed, in cause order:

1. The five glass paths (`ui/tab_card.rs` of the Editor port) were `GpuiPath` fills and strokes rasterized by resvg into one RGBA page each (5.0 MiB). They are now `GpuiBackground` and `GpuiBorder` items with per-corner radii: analytic quads and gradient quads in the existing quad pass, no raster at all. The shapes are an approximation of the old silhouette (tab, body, well, strip as rounded boxes); the Editor's UI fidelity is not a goal.
2. The planner no longer snapshots the target for a backdrop blur and no longer allocates a full-resolution output for deeper blurs. `BlurDown` may read the surface (`validate`: the surface may be read by a copy or by a blur down-sample, nothing else), so the first pyramid level samples the target directly. For `HALF_RESOLUTION_LEVELS` (3) levels or more the last up-sample pass is dropped and the composite image pass samples the half-resolution level bilinearly (`BlurOutput::texels` is the level's extent); the pyramid's offsets are scaled by `HALF_RESOLUTION_OFFSET_SCALE` (1.03) to give back the width the dropped tent contributed. Peak transient bytes per blurred region fall from about 10.7 to about 2.7 bytes per pixel (RGBA16F: snapshot 1.0, level 1 0.25, output 1.0 before). The two constants are evidence based, from `aqua-gpu-ref/tests/blur.rs` (`blur_width_grows_smoothly_and_monotonically_with_the_radius`, impulse width in pixels, largest step per 0.4 radius: limit 0.9): the previous planner 0.818; dropping the pass from 3 levels without compensation 0.948 (fails), because the 3 level blur ended 0.3 px narrower at radius 13.6 (11.12 against 11.41); with the 1.03 scale 0.839 (radius 13.6: 11.47, radius 8: identical to the previous planner at 7.6757, radius 16: 15.36 against 15.30). The Editor's glass blurs mix 3 and 4 level pyramids, which is why the threshold is 3 and not 4: with 4 the Editor held a 4.4 MiB transient heap, with 3 it holds 1.2 MiB. Below 3 levels the full-resolution path stays; mixed depths whose two pyramids differ in resolution mix at full resolution.
3. The `Core` HAL owned a 65,536 + 4,096 descriptor shader-visible heap on the Bindless tier that only tests allocate from; it is now 64 + 2,048 on every tier. The `Core` upload ring starts at 64 KiB instead of 1 MiB (system memory, not in the perf counter; the HAL tests grow it on demand).
4. The host now calls the backend's budget and visibility paths. `RenderLink::set_hidden` (raised from `WindowEvent::Occluded`) sets `RenderStats::hidden` and wakes the service; the slot calls `FrameTarget::visibility` before its next frame, which marks the window hidden in the shared hub and calls `WindowBackend::set_visibility(Hidden)` when every window sharing the device is hidden (transients and upload rings are released immediately, `evict_idle_resources` is returned) and `Visible` when any is shown. A true `evict_idle_resources`, from a hidden device or from `service_budget`, requests `TrimHandle::request`, so the next lowered frame runs `TrimRequest::Everything` and drops idle atlas pages, glyphs and Slug records on the render thread, where the residency lives. `service_budget` runs in the existing maintenance hook (every `MAINTENANCE_FRAMES` frames) and applies `trim_for(pressure, visibility)`. Tested in `tests/render_service.rs` (`hiding_and_showing_a_window_reaches_its_target_once_per_change`) and, for the backend half, `aqua-gpu-d3d12/tests/window/memory.rs`. The hidden path was not observed in a real occluded window: `win-app` windows are not covered by anything.

Round 8 re-measured the floor and the meters (11.5): the 61.1 MiB D3D12 floor stands on the gated performance counter, the in-process DXGI meter reads 28.9 MiB for both APIs, and the 50 MiB target is not reachable on D3D12. The 22.9 MiB D3D11 figure above is the counter before the swapchain buffers are charged; the settled figure is 31.4 MiB.

### 11.5 GPU dedicated memory: two meters, the D3D12 floor, the ledger and the D3D11 evidence (round 7, 2026-11-04; redone in round 8, 2026-11-05)

Method. `compare-editors.sh` phase `memdiag` (release port on the bench fixture, `bench-large` open, 600 wheel steps 16 ms apart; `AQUA_STATS_OVERRIDE=memory` keeps `AQUA_MEMORY_REPORT=1`, so the host prints `aqua-gpu-memory` once per second next to the `gpumem` probe). A temporary print in `Transients::ensure` listed every transient texture of every frame that changed the plan.

Cause found. Before: 72.4 MiB after startup and 73.2 to 77.2 MiB after 600 scrolls (a 9.3 to 11.3 MiB transient heap, 8.9 MiB of it one 916 x 812..1065 RGBA16F texture of 5.9 to 7.8 MiB). That texture was the layer of a `PushOpacity(1.0)` group: the UI tree emits an identity opacity group around every node that owns a render-side opacity track (`forced.opacity`, `aqua-ui/src/tree.rs`), and `aqua-graph` isolated every non-foldable group into a layer even at opacity 1. Fix in `aqua-graph/src/paint.rs`: a group whose combined opacity is 1 draws its children directly (CSS semantics: no stacking context at opacity 1; backdrop-reading content inside such a group now reads the real backdrop). Second finding, same file: a rounded clip allocated a layer the size of its children's bounds clipped to the clip rectangle (the same pane size) because only four corner squares need the mask. Rounded clips whose bounded rectangle exceeds 65,536 px now draw their children through three non-overlapping straight bands with ordinary clips and through four corner layers of at most `ceil(radius)` squared; the band edges lie on whole pixels, so the analytic clip coverage of neighbouring bands is exactly complementary (no seam). Tests: `aqua-graph/tests/planner.rs` (`opaque_groups_draw_directly_without_a_layer`, `large_rounded_clips_layer_only_their_corners`), `aqua-gpu-ref/tests/effects.rs` (`large_rounded_clip_has_no_seams_and_rounds_every_corner`); `aqua-gpu-d3d12` conformance 6/6, effects 3/3 and transients 5/5 on Windows after the change. The 75 percent area cap in `aqua/src/render/lower.rs` (`ROUNDED_CLIP_AREA_CAP`) was removed in round 8.

After (same fixture, same machine, RX 9070 XT, release): 64.7 MiB after startup, 64.9 MiB after 600 scrolls; transient heap 1.2 MiB (blur pyramids only), no change in the heap over the scroll. GPUI on the same fixture: 49.1 and 51.1 MiB.

Two meters measure "GPU dedicated memory" and they disagree by a constant that decides the question (round 8).

| Meter | What it reads | D3D12 bare probe | D3D11 bare probe |
|---|---|---|---|
| DXGI `IDXGIAdapter3::QueryVideoMemoryInfo(LOCAL).CurrentUsage`, in process (`MemoryBreakdown::local_usage`, the `d3dprobe` stages) | allocations the process made through the API | 28.9 MiB | 28.75 MiB |
| Performance counter `\GPU Process Memory(pid_*)\Dedicated Usage` summed over adapters (`win-drive.ps1` `gpumem`, gated by `compare-editors.sh`) | everything the kernel scheduler charges to the process, including driver-internal allocations | 61.2 MiB | 31.4 MiB |

Same probe, same 1898 x 1125 two-buffer `FLIP_DISCARD` BGRA8 swapchain, RX 9070 XT, the counter sampled externally every 150 ms while the probe pauses between stages. Counter by stage, D3D12: `D3D12CreateDevice` plus a queue 44.1 (DXGI says 11.85 at that point), swapchain 61.1, first `Present` 61.2. D3D11: device 14.4, swapchain 22.9, settled 31.4 (the buffers are charged lazily). So the APIs are identical on the DXGI meter and 29.8 MiB apart on the gated meter: the D3D12 user-mode driver allocates about 32 MiB of default-heap memory when the device is created that DXGI never reports and that no application call changes (feature level 11_0 or 12_2: same figure). The round 7 statement "the floor is 28.9 MiB, identical for D3D11 and D3D12" is true only on the DXGI meter; the 61.1 MiB D3D12 floor of 11.3 is correct on the meter the scoreboard uses. The round 7 and round 8 claim that Aqua sits 36 MiB above the floor compared the counter (64.9) against the DXGI floor (28.9); against the same meter Aqua sits 3.5 to 5 MiB above the floor.

Only default-heap resources are charged to the counter. A 16 MiB committed buffer adds 16.0 MiB in `DEFAULT` and 0.0 in `UPLOAD` or `READBACK` (private bytes move instead). So atlas staging, scene upload buffers, the frame upload ring and the timestamp readback cost no dedicated memory; swapchain buffers, persistent textures, the transient heap, pipelines and descriptor heaps do.

The ledger (`aqua-gpu-d3d12::ledger`, round 8). Every creation site holds a `ledger::Token` next to its resource: swapchain buffers per surface, the frame upload ring and its graveyard, command allocators, CPU and shader-visible descriptor heaps, per-slot scene upload buffers and SRV heaps, the timestamp query heap and readback, persistent textures by format, atlas staging, the transient heap including retired heaps, pipeline states. Dropping the resource drops the token. Per label the ledger keeps live count and bytes, peak count and bytes, and the number created. `WindowBackend::ledger()` returns the snapshot; under `AQUA_MEMORY_REPORT=1` the host prints it as `aqua-gpu-ledger` next to `aqua-gpu-memory` once per second (`compare-editors.sh` phase `memdiag`). Tests: `tests/ledger.rs` (pure) and `tests/window/memory.rs` (swapchain, transient heap and upload ring against `MemoryBreakdown`, WARP and the RX 9070 XT). The AMD tracker warm-up swapchain shows as `composition_swapchain_buffer` with a peak of 2 x 16 KiB; an A/B in the same test (160 warm-up presents on and off) leaves the counter at 61.9 MiB both ways, so the warm-up costs 0.0 MiB of dedicated memory.

Ledger of the Editor on the fixture (release, `bench-large` open, after 600 scrolls; counter 66.2 MiB, DXGI local 32.9 MiB):

| Allocation | MiB | Charged to the counter | Sized by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Swapchain, 2 buffers (17.1 at the peak, while a resized pair is alive) | 16.3 | yes | window size, fixed |
| Slug curve store, RGBA32F 8192 x 8 (`MIN_STORE_ROWS` 8; 2.0 while a grown page is retired) | 1.0 | yes | `aqua-text::slug` |
| Colour atlas pages, 2 | 1.25 | yes | `aqua-media` page policy |
| Transient heap (blur pyramids of the glass paths, 19 x 64 KiB, includes the 25 percent growth slack) | 1.19 | yes | `scene::transients`, `aqua-graph::paint` |
| Pipeline states (11 with the pipeline-cache hint, 22 without) | API does not report | yes: counter 44.2 for `Core` alone, 44.9 for `WindowBackend::new` (22 states), about 0.7 | `scene::pipeline` |
| Atlas staging | 1.0 | no (upload heap) | `scene::persistent` |
| Scene upload buffers, 3 slots | 0.75 | no | `scene::renderer` |
| Shader-visible, SRV and RTV heaps, timestamps, frame ring | 0.14 | mostly no | `hal` |
| D3D12 device, driver-internal (invisible to DXGI) | 32.3 | yes | vendor, fixed |
| Device, queue, driver swapchain state (the part DXGI reports) | 11.9 | yes | vendor, fixed |

Reference points on the counter: bare probe 61.2, a bare `WindowBackend` with the Editor-size swapchain and a three-quad scene 61.9, the Editor 64.7 to 66.2 (the spread across runs is other agents' concurrent edits to the port and the text stack: the Slug store and the colour pages were resized between runs; the ledger totals are identical before and after the 600 scrolls). GPUI on the same fixture: 49.1 to 51.1 MiB, which is its D3D11 floor 31.4 plus about 19 MiB of its own textures.

Verdict for the 50 MiB target: not reachable on D3D12 on this driver. The floor on the gated meter is 61.2 MiB; deleting every Aqua-controlled allocation (all 3.5 to 5 MiB) would still leave the Editor 11 MiB above the 50 MiB line and 10 MiB above GPUI. The 64 MiB section 11 gate passes with 0 to 1.3 MiB of margin (64.7, 65.9 and 66.2 in three builds of the same code with different concurrent port edits; the 66.2 build fails it). What is left to cut in code Aqua owns is the transient heap's growth slack (0.25 MiB) and a smaller pipeline set (0.3 MiB at most); the Slug store (1.0 MiB, `MIN_STORE_ROWS`) and the colour atlas pages (1.25 MiB) are owned by `aqua-text` and `aqua-media` and together could give about 1.5 MiB. None of it changes the verdict. A D3D11 backend (floor 31.4 MiB, Editor expected at about 35 to 37 MiB plus its own textures, i.e. about 50 MiB, which ties GPUI) would; it is the user's decision (14.4).

Cost of a D3D11 adapter (nothing is built):

| Quantity | D3D12 (now) | D3D11 (measured floor, estimate for Aqua) |
|---|---|---|
| Device + swapchain floor, counter | 61.2 MiB | 31.4 MiB |
| Device + swapchain floor, DXGI | 28.9 MiB | 28.75 MiB |
| Aqua on top | 3.5 to 5 MiB | about 4 MiB (atlas 1.25, glass transients 1.2 to 2.5 because D3D11 has no placed aliasing, misc.) |
| Private bytes floor | 88.9 MiB | 29.8 MiB |
| Device creation, first present | 186 ms, 256 ms | 44 ms, 112 ms |

What it costs. (1) Code: a second `GpuBackend` next to `aqua-gpu-d3d12`, about 8,300 lines of Rust in `hal`, `scene` and `window` there, of which device selection, swapchain, pacing, damage, mirror and recovery are largely reusable in shape; command recording, barriers, descriptor management, transient placement and timestamps are D3D12 specific. (2) Tiers: D3D11 has one tier, `Baseline` without the other three axes; `conformance` and `tiers` run one variant. (3) Shaders: `aqua-shaders` compiles with `dxc` to DXIL; D3D11 needs DXBC (`fxc`, SM 5.0), so `build.rs` gains a second profile and runtime `CustomPassSpec` HLSL goes through `D3DCompile`. (4) Oracle: nothing in `aqua-gpu-ref` changes; the adapter must pass the same 133 cases and the differential fuzz. (5) Policy: 14.4 stays Windows only; the host would select D3D11 for the memory profile. (6) Risks: flip-model swapchains with a waitable object and `Present1` damage rects exist on D3D11, DirectComposition and WARP work, device removal uses the same DXGI events, GPU timestamps exist, and there is no pipeline cache blob, so shader creation is paid at startup.

### 11.4 Heap and private bytes, attribution and trim (round 6, 2026-11-04)

Method. `aqua::mem_probe` (feature `probe-allocator`, forwarded by the port as `heap-probe`) is a global allocator that stores a 16 byte header with every block (call-stack hash, size) and aggregates live bytes and cumulative allocation counts per call stack in a fixed table; it never symbolizes in process. `AQUA_HEAP_CHAINS_FILE=<path>` makes the host write the chains (module-relative return addresses) once per second, and `tools/heap-attribution.sh EXE CHAINS [TOP]` resolves them offline with `llvm-addr2line` into three tables: by leaf crate, by owner (first workspace frame) and by leaf site. The profile is `[profile.heapprobe]` of the port (release, no LTO, line tables for the workspace crates only, symbols kept). `AQUA_MEMORY_REPORT=1` additionally prints, once per second, the per-heap allocated and committed bytes (`aqua-heaps`) and a `VirtualQuery` walk of the private address space (`aqua-address-space`). `win-app` placed windows at the bottom of the z-order, so every run below sets `AQUA_RENDER_WHEN_COVERED=1`. The workload is the private bench fixture (`tools/compare-editors.sh fixture`, own data directory and port) with the Editor port opened on `bench-large`, release builds, three 600-wheel bursts 16 ms apart with 3 s of rest between them.

Where the private bytes go (release port, bench thread open, 150 to 160 MiB private; `aqua-address-space` plus the probe build):

| Component | MiB | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Driver system memory, write-combined private regions (`PAGE_WRITECOMBINE`) | 47.7 on the Choose-a-Forge picker, 62.8 with the thread open | `VirtualQuery`: one 30 to 31 MiB region plus 5 to 9 regions; Aqua's own upload, atlas staging and transient allocations are under 3 MiB (`MemoryBreakdown`) |
| Process heap, allocated (default heap) | 41.7 to 43 | `HeapSummary` |
| Rust live heap inside it | 24.8 (26.5 with the probe's headers); 33.5 before the fixes below | probe chains |
| C and COM allocations of other DLLs in the same heap | about 17 | allocated minus Rust live |
| Heap committed but free (fragmentation slack) | 13 to 17, growing 3 to 4 MiB over the first scroll burst, flat afterwards | committed minus allocated; `HeapCompact` returns about 1 MiB of it |
| Second private heap (not Aqua's, created by a system component) | 12.8 | `GetProcessHeaps` |
| Everything else (stacks, images, module data) | about 12 | remainder |

So the Rust heap is a quarter of the private bytes, the driver is two fifths, and the GPUI Editor's 115 MiB with the same driver means the budget for everything Aqua controls to match it is about 32 MiB, against about 70 now (Rust 25, slack 15, DLL heaps 17). The target of 100 MiB private is not reachable from the Rust heap alone: it would need the driver's write-combined memory to fall by 15 MiB or more as well (it grows by 15 MiB between the picker and the thread view; this is pipeline and command state in the user-mode driver, listed under GPU memory in 11.3).

Findings and fixes, each with its measurement:

- `UiTree` retained a `RetainedInteraction` entry per node, including clones of the node's `LayoutStyle` and `Paint` and three optional hover/focus/pressed `Style`s (about 3.5 KiB per entry in a hash table that doubles), although only nodes with interaction styles ever read them. Probe chains: the interaction table was 28.6 MiB of 82 MiB for 8000 nodes, and 8.1 MiB of 33.5 MiB Rust heap in the real thread view. The entry now keeps only state, semantics and colours; the base style and the style set live in a boxed `InteractionBase` that exists only for nodes bound with `ElementBinding::InteractionStyle` (`Bind { styled }`). Mount cost per node (box and text row of the heap gate): 27.2 KiB before, 13.5 KiB after; box only 12.2 to 5.3 KiB. Thread view Rust live heap 33.5 to 26.5 MiB (probe), hash tables 8.9 to 2.2 MiB. Host window gate: framework heap 13.37 to 9.55 MiB, mount stage 8.31 to 5.01 MiB. Test: `aqua-verify/tests/retained_heap.rs` bounds the retained heap per mounted row (20 KiB mount, 96 KiB first frame).
- `ShapedParagraph` kept `Vec` growth slack for clusters and runs; they are shrunk after shaping (-6% per shaped character, 172 to 162 KiB per 800 character row). Shaped text costs about 200 bytes per character (`Cluster` 56, `RunGlyph` 16, `LaidGlyph` about 140 bytes with 20 bytes per optional colour). Compacting `LaidGlyph` (a shared per-span style table, `u32` offsets) was measured against the thread view and rejected for now: text is 2.6 MiB of the 25 MiB Rust live heap there because only realized rows are shaped, so it would save about 1 MiB for an API change across `aqua`, `aqua-runtime` and the port.
- Allocation churn per scroll burst (cumulative allocation counts from the chains, 600 wheel steps minus the idle baseline): 1.74 M before, 1.39 M after. Causes found and fixed in the port: `Conversation::observe_visible_rows` rebuilt a `HashSet<RowKey>` of the mounted rows every frame, `advance_disclosure_frame` built row keys for every row every frame even with no disclosure change, `observe_viewport` rebuilt a `HashMap<RowKey, u64>` and re-keyed every row twice per frame, the root view cloned the whole `rows` vector per viewport event to compare it afterwards, and each painted row cloned its source text (`RowSnapshot::source`, `ProjectedProof::source`) and its row key twice per painted frame. They now use a per-`Conversation` `rows_epoch` (bumped only when `rows` or a row version changes), a keys cache in the transcript observer keyed by that epoch, `Rc<str>` for the message source, and a precomputed paint key. `Footprints::of_scene` allocated one `Vec` of group ids per scene item per frame (353 k allocations per burst, 25% of what was left); the chain is now an `Arc<[NodeId]>` rebuilt only when the group stack changes, and the entry map is pre-sized.
- Heap fragmentation: the process heap's committed-but-free bytes grow with scrolling and `HeapCompact` cannot reclaim them (low-fragmentation heap blocks interleave with long-lived ones). Two allocator replacements were measured in the same fixture and rejected: mimalloc as the Editor's global allocator raised private bytes by 76 to 80 MiB (238 to 255 against 162 to 170) with the same working set, with and without `MIMALLOC_ARENA_EAGER_COMMIT=0`, `MIMALLOC_EAGER_COMMIT=0` and `MIMALLOC_PURGE_DELAY=0`; the segment heap (`<heapType>SegmentHeap</heapType>` in the application manifest) changed nothing (private 156 to 173 against 157 to 173, working set 5 to 10 MiB higher). Per-frame bump arenas for the UI tree and layout were not built: the churn above came from a handful of sites, now removed, and the retained heap, not the transient one, dominates.
- Wall-clock idle trim. `TrimInput` carries `now`; `TrimPolicy::idle_deadline` is the time of the last real frame plus `IDLE_TRIM_AFTER` (2 s) until one trim consumes it (a requested trim after the deadline consumes it, an earlier one does not). The render service folds that deadline into the slot's wait, so an idle process takes exactly one extra wake per idle episode, raises the existing trim flag, and the forced maintenance frame trims the atlas and Slug stores (`TrimRequest::Everything`), then `mem_probe::return_memory_to_os` runs `HeapCompact`, `HeapOptimizeResources` and empties the working set. Measured cost of the pass on the render thread: 16 to 21 ms once per idle episode (compaction 2 ms). Measured effect (three runs each, after a 3 s rest): private 0 to -7 MiB, not reliably (atlas and Slug stores and driver-side staging released; the process heap does not shrink), working set 170 to 175 MiB down to 0.8 to 4 MiB at rest and 50 MiB after a scroll burst (pages are touched on demand); scroll p50 and p99 after the trim are equal to the runs without the working-set step (3.1 to 3.4 ms and 20 to 27 ms), with one 63 ms and one 162 ms outlier in six post-trim bursts that earlier baseline runs also show on this shared machine. Private bytes are not reduced by the working-set step; it is reported separately for that reason.
- `HOST_WINDOWED` framework heap: 16 MiB (round 5) to 12 MiB. The 400-row document of `host_window` measures 9.5 MiB (mount 5.0, first present 4.3, scrolling +0.1), so the original 4 MiB application heap is not reachable by the host (the first present alone costs 4.3 MiB of lowering, scene and ring state); 12 MiB leaves 25% headroom.

End-to-end A/B (release builds of the port, same fixture, 3 runs each, medians): scroll p50 4.8 to 3.2 ms, p90 21 to 6.1 ms, p99 36 to 24 ms (these also include the composer-rebuild and key-latency work of other agents landed since the baseline executable was built); private at boot 158 to 151 MiB, after three bursts and 20 s idle 164 to 154 MiB; working set at rest 175 to 4 MiB. Still failing: private is 150 to 160 MiB against GPUI's 115, and not at or below 100 MiB; growth across bursts is 3 to 4 MiB on the first burst (heap slack) and about 0 after.

### 11.6 Round 7: private bytes, attribution before and after (2026-11-05)

Method. Same fixture and method as 11.4 (`bench-large`, release port, `tools/heap-attribution.sh`, heapprobe build). Round 7 adds three things to the probe: live block counts per call stack (`blocks=` in the chains file, the table now reports live blocks instead of cumulative allocations), a `HeapWalk` classification of the default heap (`aqua-heapwalk` line: busy, uncommitted and free blocks by size class) and `private_mib` on every `aqua-startup stage=` line (`AQUA_PROFILE=1`), which attributes private bytes to device, renderer, pipeline and first-present stages. The machine was loaded by other builds during the work (load average up to 21), which moves the scroll position and so the realized row count; the numbers below that are not comparable run to run are marked.

Rust live heap by owner, MiB (heapprobe, bench-large open, after the first scroll bursts):

| Owner | Start of round 7 | End of round 7 | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| Decoded preview image (`decode_preview_still`, 1 block) | 3.3 | 0.0 | `ImageAssets::retire` and `retire_idle` (below) |
| Bundled fonts (`ShellResources::with_text`, 14 blocks) | 0.65 | 0.0 | fonts are registered as `&'static [u8]` (`load_font_static`), no copy |
| `UiTree` node arena (`Arena<NodeRecord>`, 1568 B per node) | 3.1 (one 2064 slot block) | 1.5 to 2.5 (32 slot chunks, 960 to 1664 slots) | chunked `aqua-core::arena::Arena` |
| Taffy (`NodeData` per node, `LeafCtx` side table) | 3.4 plus 0.87 | 2.5 plus 0.05 | `LeafCtx.text` is `Option<Box<LeafText>>` (200 B to 8 B per side-table entry) |
| Text shaping and layout | 2.1 | 2.1 to 3.0 (follows the realized rows) | unchanged |
| Slug store and cache | 1.7 | 0.8 to 1.8 (follows the glyph set drawn) | unchanged |
| Port conversation view and port other | 6.7 | 6.2 to 6.7 | unchanged (port side, not addressed this round) |
| syntect syntaxes | 1.5 | 1.5 | unchanged |
| Render link rings (4096 slot command and event rings) | 1.1 | 1.1 | unchanged |
| Total, probe `live_total` | 27.1 | 21.3 (same tree size) to 24.3 (larger realized window) | -5.8 to -2.8 |

Release private bytes at `mem_open` and after two 600 wheel bursts plus rest (`mem_settle`), fixture thread open, same machine and exe protocol: start of round 7 152.5 and 156.1 (one run; round 6 recorded 150 to 160). End of round 7, five runs: open 149.4 to 151.5, settle 147.6 to 149.0. So about 7 MiB less after the bursts, and the target of 112 MiB is not met (33 to 37 MiB short). GPU dedicated moved from 72.7 to 65.7 to 66.2 MiB in the same runs because of the parallel round 7 GPU work, not because of the changes here.

Why 112 MiB is out of reach on D3D12, measured (`aqua-verify/tests/window_memory_budget.rs`, now with a write-combined and all-heaps column per stage, RX 9070 XT, a renderer with no UI, no fonts and no text):

| Stage | Private MiB | Write-combined | All heaps |
|---|---|---|---|
| process | 11.2 | 0 | 0.3 |
| device and renderer (`WindowBackend::new`, includes the AMD frame-tracker warm-up) | 103.4 (+92.2) | 45.5 | 26.3 |
| swapchain and uploads | 103.6 | 45.5 | 26.5 |
| first present | 108.5 | 49.1 | 27.5 |
| after warm-up and 600 steady frames | 110.5 | 51.1 | 27.5 |

The bare renderer is already at 110.5 MiB of the 112 MiB target. Inside the app (`aqua-startup stage=... private_mib`, release): process 11.4; `D3D12CreateDevice` +52.9 (vendor); queue, descriptor heaps, rings and command list +4.0 (Aqua); loading 11 pipelines from the library +8.7 (about 0.8 MiB of driver memory per pipeline); first present with window, swapchain and application +55.6 of which about 40 is the driver; the AMD tracker warm-up +6.1. Of the 149 MiB, the write-combined driver regions are 51 to 60 (three allocations of about 30, 20 and 11 MiB that no Aqua call controls), the foreign allocations in the default heap are 16.3 (13 of them appear while the device and swapchain are created), the second private heap is 12.8 (a system component), and Aqua's Rust heap is 21 to 24. A D3D11 adapter (private floor 29.8 against 88.9 MiB for D3D12, 11.3) is the only route to 112 MiB private and remains the user's decision (14.4). Heap fragmentation, measured by `HeapWalk` on the default heap: 47.7 k busy blocks (37.0 MiB), 7.8 MiB free in 23 k blocks (0.5 MiB at 64 B or less, 1.0 at 512 B or less, 1.8 at 4 KiB or less, 2.3 at 64 KiB or less, 2.2 in 8 blocks above 64 KiB) and 2.7 MiB uncommitted: there is no large reclaimable hole, so per-frame arenas for the tree, layout and text would save at most the 2 MiB in large free blocks and are not built (peak 30.3 against live 27.1 MiB in the probe says the same).

Findings and fixes, each with its measurement:

- Parallel pipeline warm-up costs private bytes. The pipeline warm-up (round 7 cold start work) ran four threads. Each OS thread that calls the AMD driver gets a private heap of 3.5 MiB allocated and 5.3 MiB committed that stays after the thread exits: heaps #7 to #9 at 0.0/0.5 MiB with one lane against 3.5/5.3 each with four. Same exe, same cache, `AQUA_WARM_LANES` 1, 2, 3, 4: private after the bursts 164.9 and 162.6 (two runs), 173.3, 179.6, 184.0 MiB; `warm` stage 104 to 125, 59, 58, 38 ms; first present 661 to 751, 533, 677, 525 ms (noisy). Each extra thread costs about 8.5 MiB for about 30 to 40 ms. `WARM_LANES` is 1 (the calling thread is lane 0, a lane count above 1 spawns the others); the knob stays as `AQUA_WARM_LANES`. This trades 60 to 140 ms of cold start for 18 to 21 MiB; if the cold start work needs the time it must find it without new driver-calling threads.
- Half of the warmed pipelines are never used. `Pipelines` now records which pipelines `get` served; the usage set is stored with the library in `pipelines.cache` (`AQH1` frame: length, names, library; an unframed file is a bare library and still loads) as the union of earlier sessions, and the next start warms `Quad` plus the used set. Editor thread view, 22 pipelines warmed before: `Glyph/10`, `Glyph/91`, `Slug/10`, `Layer/10`, `Layer/91`, `Copy/10`, `Copy/91`, `BlurDown/91`, `BlurUp/91`, `ColorMatrix/10`, `ColorMatrix/91` unused (11). With the hint: loaded 5 at start instead of 11, `warm` 83 ms against 124 to 129, private at that stage 77.4 to 77.6 against 81.8, and 1 to 2 MiB less after the bursts; a pipeline needed later is loaded from the library on first use and counted in `late`. The host now also runs the maintenance hook (library save) when the idle trim fires, so a short session persists its usage set. `tests/pipeline_cache.rs::a_second_session_warms_only_the_pipelines_the_first_one_used`, `tests/scene/hint.rs`. The cache file is shared by every Aqua process of the user: an older executable that writes a bare library replaces the framed file and the hint restarts from empty (everything warmed once).
- Decoded images. A decoded still image above 256 KiB is dropped (`ImageAssets::retire`) as soon as `ManagedUiApp` has handed it to the render thread, and also when it has gone unused for 2 s (`retire_idle`: not visible, not resident in any window); the encoded bytes stay in the slot, `ImageHandle::status` keeps reporting `Ready { width, height }`, `decoded_bytes` keeps the logical size, and a visible image that is no longer resident is decoded again on a worker (`revive`). The fixture thread's image was never visible, so before this it held 3.3 MiB for the whole session; probe: `image-decode` 3378 KiB to 3 KiB. Cost: an image that scrolls out and back is decoded again (10 to 30 ms on a worker) instead of re-uploaded from memory. `ManagedUiApp` image and lease tests 11 of 11, `retire_tests` 3 of 3.
- Node storage. `Arena` keeps slots in chunks of 32 instead of one doubling `Vec`: no 2x slack, no multi-MiB copy on growth (the 3.1 MiB block held 2064 slots for 1004 live nodes). Measured sizes per node (`size_of`): `NodeRecord` 1568 B (`NodeKind` 584: `TextContent` 584, `BoxContent` 504; `Paint` 504; `LayoutStyle` 200; `NodeSemantics` 152), taffy `Style` 560 + `Cache` 408 + two `Layout` 92 = about 1.15 KiB in taffy's slot map, which doubles (2.5 MiB block for 1004 nodes) and cannot be changed without forking taffy. `NodeRecord`, `Paint` and `TextContent` were not reshaped: every cut needs a public field type to change (`BoxContent { paint }` is built literally in about 25 call sites in tests and the port, `NodeKind::Text` has 262 uses), the saving at 1000 to 1700 nodes is at most 1.6 MiB, and the next step is recorded instead: put `gpui_text_shimmer`, `text_color_map`, `gpui_text_color_map` and `gpui_quad_pulse` (about 250 B of the 504) behind `Option<Box<_>>`, and give a box node's `Paint` to `NodeRecord::paint` only (a box currently carries two, `BoxContent::paint` and the unused `NodeRecord::paint`, 1 KiB).
- Not changed because measured small: the render link rings (0.4 MiB could go with 512 slot rings, with a backlog path that exists), the tokio channel block per markdown presenter (0.2 MiB), the reactive scope table (0.45 MiB), syntect (1.5 MiB, port side).

### 11.7 Round 8: private bytes, heap and working set (2026-11-05)

Method. Release port on the `bench-large` fixture (`tools/compare-editors.sh` phase `memdiag`, a private fixture root and port, `AQUA_MEMORY_REPORT=1`, 600 wheel steps, 5 s rest), paired runs of the round 7 exe and the round 8 exe, four pairs, machine load below 8. Attribution from a heapprobe build (`tools/heap-attribution.sh`, `AQUA_HEAP_CHAINS_FILE`; `AQUA_STATS_OVERRIDE=on` is required for the report, `memory` mode does not print it). Per-row figures from `aqua-verify/tests/retained_heap.rs` (deterministic counting allocator, 2000 rows).

Where the 142 MiB goes (private_committed 140.3 MiB, release, address-space walk): default process heap 47 MiB committed (37 allocated; Rust live about 20, the rest is driver and runtime allocations that grow by 13 MiB at first submit and present), a second driver heap 12.7, write-combined driver regions 50.4 (30.2 + 15.9 + 4.3, none of it Aqua buffers: Aqua's upload heaps are 2.5 MiB), driver heaps 8, everything else (stacks, images, tables) about 20. The Rust live heap is a flat profile with no owner above 3 MiB: node arena 2.9, taffy node slab 2.55 (one block), shaped paragraphs and layouts 2.6, slug store 0.8 plus outlines 0.8, port conversation view 2.8, hash tables 1.5, syntect 0.9 (two copies), link rings 0.45.

Changes and their measurements:

| Change | Evidence |
|---|---|
| `NodeRecord.paint` is `Option<Box<Paint>>` (`paint_slot`, read through `NodeRecord::paint()`), allocated only for a non-default paint on a non-box node; box nodes keep their paint in `BoxContent` and no longer carry a second 504 B default | `retained_heap` mount cost per box+text row 13.5 KiB (11.4) to 12.3 KiB |
| `Cluster` stores `u32` offsets and indices behind accessors, 48 B to 28 B per character, in the shape cache and every retained shaped paragraph | `retained_heap` first frame 88.6 KiB to 79.7 KiB per row (the gate is 96); shape cache 1.2 MiB for 277 paragraphs |
| One `SyntaxSet` per process (`LazyLock`), shared by the main-thread engine and the stored-markdown worker | two 0.6 MiB deserialized copies in the probe chains |
| Render link rings 4096 to 512 slots (the backlog path already handles a full ring) | 0.45 MiB, probe chains |
| taffy built with `flexbox`, `grid`, `content_size` only (no `block_layout`, `float_layout`, `calc`, `detailed_layout_info`, `flexbox_balance`; none are used) | taffy `Style` 552 B, `Cache` 408 B, `Layout` 92 B in the reduced build; `aqua-layout`, `aqua-ui`, `aqua-text` suites pass |

Result, four paired release runs: private after 600 scrolls 141.5 MiB median (141 to 145) before, 137.5 MiB (137 to 139) after, so -4 MiB. Default heap allocated 36.0 to 30.9 MiB, committed 46.5 to 40.9 MiB. Scroll probe latency during the runs was unchanged (p50 1.3 to 1.5 ms, p99 28 to 37 ms in both, instrumentation on). The target of 125 MiB is not met; 12.5 MiB remain.

What is left, quantified. The D3D12 API floor of a bare probe is 88.9 MiB private (11.2 process, 52.9 device, 55.6 including the first present with a window). Aqua is 48.6 MiB above it: Rust live heap about 18 MiB (down from 20), heap committed-but-free about 10 MiB, driver heap growth that follows UI activity (the second driver heap grows 4.0 to 12.7 MiB between boot and the thread view) about 12 MiB, the rest threads and tables. The 125 MiB target would need a further 12.5 MiB: all of the Rust live heap cut by two thirds, or the D3D11 adapter (private floor 29.8 MiB, section 14.4), which is the user's decision and was not built.

Not done, with the reason. Lowering the transcript overscan from 1600 to 800 px cuts about 3 MiB more (alternating runs with the intermediate exe: 141 and 150 at 1600 px against 138 and 139 at 800 px, 138 at 600 px; heap committed 46.3 to 40.7 MiB at 600 px), but its scroll cost could not be judged on this machine (load 15 to 22 during those runs) and the scroll owner set the figure, so it stays. `LaidGlyph` is 140 B per glyph and dominates the first-frame text cost (about 60 of the 80 KiB per row of the heap gate): narrowing its four `usize` byte offsets and sharing the per-glyph colours would save about a third of that, a change through every `aqua-text` consumer that this round did not make. Idle shrinking of rows after a scroll burst was rejected on evidence: freed rows leave holes in the default heap (allocated fell 3 MiB, committed 0.9 MiB in the same comparison), so only a smaller peak lowers private bytes.

### 11.8 Round 10: node records, taffy storage and the realized window (2026-11-05)

Method. Release port on the `bench-large` fixture through `tools/compare-editors.sh` (`AQUA_COMPARE_PHASES=memdiag`, then `main`), `AQUA_MEMORY_REPORT=1` prints `aqua-tree-footprint` once per second (`UiTree::footprint`: live nodes, arena slots, taffy nodes, table capacities), heapprobe chains from `tools/heap-attribution.sh`. Baseline exe: the round 9 end release build (16:55); after exe: the same tree with the changes below. Three memdiag runs per variant, one to two main runs per variant (the second baseline main run was still queued behind other scoreboards when this was written).

Measured before touching anything (`size_of`): `NodeRecord` 1072 B, `NodeKind` 584 B (`TextContent` 584, `BoxContent` 504 = one inline `Paint`), `NodeSemantics` 152 B (`CollectionSemantics` 96 B inline), taffy `Style` 552 B, `Cache` 408 B, two `Layout` 92 B. The fixture thread holds 974 nodes at first paint and 1000 to 1100 afterwards (arena high-water 1130 to 1476 slots); the earlier "5.2 MiB taffy vector" is the same doubling `Vec` as the arena had: 2048 slots of 1.2 KiB, a single 2.5 MiB block for about 1000 live nodes, and the growth copy leaves 1.3 + 0.6 + 0.3 MiB of freed blocks behind in the heap.

| Change | Evidence |
|---|---|
| `NodeKind::Text(Box<TextContent>)` (built through `NodeKind::text`), `BoxContent` holds `Option<Box<Paint>>` behind `paint()` and `set_paint()`, `NodeSemantics.collection` is `Option<Box<CollectionSemantics>>` | `NodeRecord` 1072 B to 464 B, `NodeKind` 584 B to 64 B, `NodeSemantics` 152 B to 64 B (`size_of` test run); `retained_heap` mount 12.2 KiB per box+text row, first frame 79.7 KiB |
| taffy is vendored (`vendor/taffy`, MIT, taffy 0.15.1, excluded from the workspace members) with the `slotmap` dependency replaced by a chunked slab (`src/tree/slab.rs`: chunks of 32 slots, generation-checked keys, a free list, a secondary table for node contexts); nothing else in taffy changed | taffy block 2533 KiB for 2048 slots in one allocation (heapprobe chains of the pre-change exe) against chunks of 38 KiB; `aqua-layout/tests/taffy_storage.rs` (200 children across chunk boundaries, removed keys never alias a replacement, detach and reattach), `aqua-layout`, `aqua-ui`, `aqua-runtime`, `aqua-text` suites pass |
| `SlugStore` mirror grows in 8192 texel steps (`reserve_exact`) instead of doubling | 768 KiB block; `slug_store` tests pass |
| `UiTree::footprint` and the `aqua-tree-footprint` report line | evidence tool for the numbers in this section |
| Transcript realized window (port): settled overscan 1600 px to 800 px (`AQUA_OVERSCAN_PX` still overrides) | nodes 1070 to 876-985 at rest; memdiag private 117, 117, 118 MiB against 120, 119, 120 MiB at 1600 px; main phase scroll p50 and p99 unchanged (see below) |

Result, memdiag (release, 600 wheel steps, three runs each): private after the scroll 131, 131, 131 MiB (baseline), 120, 119, 120 MiB (after), 117, 117, 118 MiB (after, 800 px overscan); default heap allocated 70.7 MiB to 62.4 MiB (60.4 MiB at 800 px), committed 81.6 to 72.4 MiB (69.6 MiB). Main phase (no memory report), private after 600 scrolls and settle: 132.3 MiB baseline (one run), 122.6 and 119.8 MiB after, 121.2 and 117.9 MiB at 800 px; scroll p50 0.098 to 0.146 ms, p99 0.606 to 0.764 ms, max 0.81 to 1.70 ms and zero frames over 4 ms in every run (baseline p50 0.145, p99 0.634, max 1.40); first content 166 to 192 ms against 207 ms. Caveat: the two exes also differ by the changes other agents landed in the same hour (the D3D11 pool, the text caches, the runtime profiling spans), so only the structure sizes, the taffy block and the retained-heap gate are attributable to this change; the process-level difference (11 MiB at 1600 px) is an upper bound, and the Rust share that this change explains by measurement is about 3 MiB (records 0.6, taffy block and growth holes 2, Slug mirror 0.1 to 0.2, 800 px overscan 2.7 more). The three-run memdiag p90 (0.58 to 0.61 ms against 0.40 to 0.42 ms baseline) is not reproduced in the main phase (0.45 to 0.50 against 0.40); a 2000-node relayout costs 0.36 ms (`compute_layout`, one dirty row, release test), so the slab lookup cannot account for it.

Heapprobe (`tools/heap-attribution.sh`, release-like profile with the probe allocator, memdiag fixture run): Rust live heap 15.19 MiB with the shrunken records and the doubling taffy block (1018 nodes, 1304 arena slots) against 12.93 MiB with the chunked slab and 800 px overscan (825 nodes, 1037 slots); owner `UiTree` 3274 KiB to 1888 KiB, taffy storage 2672 KiB (one 2533 KiB block) to 1317 KiB (103 chunk allocations), `Arena<NodeRecord>` 650 KiB to 488 KiB, `ProcessHeap` allocated 65.0 to 63.7 MiB in the probe build. The records alone were measured by `size_of` (the 1072 B records would have held about 1.4 MiB for the same slots).

Why not the 6 MiB of the plan from per-node cuts alone: at 1000 to 1500 nodes every 1 KiB per node is 1 to 1.5 MiB. Records and taffy together were about 3.3 MiB (arena 1.3 MiB at 1072 B, taffy 2.5 MiB plus growth holes); the fixture's node count, not the node size, bounds what is left: taffy `Style` (552 B) and `Cache` (408 B) are still in each node and cannot shrink without replacing taffy's storage trait impl by one that derives the style from `LayoutStyle`, which would add a conversion to every layout access. Viewport-first open already exists (landing overscan 320 px, then 1600, now 800): 61 nodes in the first frame, 974 at first content, no node above the settled window.

Round 11 (per-node taffy memory). Decision: taffy stays vendored in `vendor/taffy`, not a `[patch]` dependency, because the change edits `Style` and `Cache` themselves and the chunked slab is already local; a patch crate would carry the same diff. `Style` moved its ten grid fields into `Option<Box<GridStyle>>` (`Style::grid_mut()` allocates on first use; the accessor traits read defaults when it is `None`), and `Cache` keeps its measure entries in a `Vec` that grows two at a time up to the original nine instead of a fixed array (same eviction order). `size_of`: `Style` 552 B to 256 B, `Cache` 408 B to 144 B, so 560 B less per taffy node. `aqua-verify/tests/retained_heap.rs` (deterministic counting allocator, 2000 box+text rows, debug build): mount 12.2 KiB to 10.6 KiB per row, first frame 79.7 KiB to 78.1 KiB per row. At the fixture's 1000 resident nodes that is about 0.55 MiB of Rust heap, not the 3 MiB aimed at: the remaining per-node weight is the two 92 B `Layout`s, the 464 B `NodeRecord` and the text and glyph state, which this round did not touch. Not done: the three-run memdiag p90 bisection (0.58 to 0.61 against 0.40 to 0.42 ms) and a Windows A/B, because both build slots stayed occupied by port release builds for over an hour; the earlier analysis (a 2000-node relayout costs 0.36 ms, p90 not reproduced in the main phase) stands.

## 12. Verification

- **Conformance suite:** the same `FramePlan`s run on every `GpuBackend`; output is diffed against `aqua-gpu-ref` with per-channel tolerances. Every variant of the D3D11 adapter (the default: WARP, RX 9070 XT, integrated GPU, float and BGRA8 targets, 9 variants, zero skips) and of the stashed D3D12 adapter runs it.
- **Differential fuzzing** of fast paths against reference implementations from one scene generator.
- **Replay determinism:** `Recorder<Runtime>` logs of `RuntimeInput` (the `Machine` input type, including ticks, frames, shell events, clipboard, IME and accessibility attach) replay into a fresh `Runtime` to identical outputs at every step and identical scene and lowered-plan hashes. Covered over `Runtime`, `RenderSide`, the transport lowering and the full `aqua` host composition (`aqua-verify/tests/host_full.rs`: `Aqua` + `UiApp` + `RenderLink` on the Reference, WARP, discrete and integrated adapters; identical settled frames across two replays, D3D12 within 1/255 of the Reference host).
- **Host replay:** `aqua-verify/tests/host_replay.rs` records a scripted wheel and click session on the live host (`Aqua` + `UiApp::record_session` + render thread, Reference and WARP backends) and replays the recorded `RuntimeInput` log three ways: into a fresh `Runtime` (identical `RuntimeOutput` at every step, identical scene hash), into a fresh `Runtime` plus `RenderSide` plus lowering plus backend (identical final frame hash to the live host's last presented frame), and through `Timeline` (seeking back and forward lands on the scene of the replayed prefix; a paused timeline freezes machine time). Two independent live sessions agree on the final scene and frame.
- **Exact counters:** allocations, instances, glyphs, layout nodes, passes per scene.
- **Text oracle:** DirectWrite pixel diffs (section 8).
- **Motion checks:** no one-frame jumps, no unmount above zero opacity, no motion on first mount, settle within declared bounds.

### 12.1 Reference semantics (`aqua-gpu-ref`)

`aqua-gpu-ref` defines what every `FramePlan` op means. Backends match it within the tolerances below. Pixel output is premultiplied sRGB-encoded RGBA (the GPUI colour model, 6.8), quantized to 8 bits per channel with round-to-nearest.

| Op | Reference definition |
|---|---|
| Frame start | The surface holds `plan.clear` (premultiplied). A plan that never writes the surface therefore yields the clear colour. |
| `LoadOp` | `Clear(c)` fills the whole target before the pass; `Load` and `DontCare` keep the contents, a never-written transient is transparent. |
| Quad | Signed-distance coverage `saturate(0.5 - d)` for the rounded rect, the same for the clip rect, border blended over the fill with `saturate(0.5 - inner)`, then `x opacity`. |
| Gradient | `t` is the projection of the pixel centre on `start -> end` (absolute pixel space), mapped through the two stop offsets and clamped. Colours interpolate in Cartesian Oklab (straight alpha), then convert to linear sRGB, clamp, encode and premultiply. Backends may dither by at most half an 8-bit step. |
| Shadow | Exact Gaussian-blurred rounded rect: Gaussian-weighted integral over y of the closed-form erf x-extent of the rounded rect at that row. Spread grows the rect and the corner radius. `sigma < 0.05` is a hard signed-distance edge. |
| Glyph | Hard pixel-centre inclusion in `bounds`, nearest texel of the R8 atlas inside `texels`, toned by `plan.text_tone` against the straight encoded luminance of the colour, multiplied by clip coverage and opacity. Slug glyphs use the same tone. |
| Image | Quad coverage of `bounds` and `radius` times the coverage of `clip` rounded by `clip_radius` (ignored without a clip), bilinear sampling clamped to the `texels` sub-rectangle. Persistent RGBA8 data is premultiplied sRGB-encoded. `Blend::Over` composites `src * k` over the target; `Blend::Replace` yields `src * k + dst * (1 - k)` (premultiplied lerp), with `k = coverage * opacity`. Backdrop blur composites with `Replace`, so a translucent backdrop is replaced, not stacked. |
| `LayerOver` | `src * opacity` composited over the target. |
| Blur down / up | Dual Kawase. Down: 4x centre plus four diagonal taps at `0.5 * offset * (source/dest ratio, at least 1)`, divided by 8. Up: four axis taps at `offset`, four diagonal taps at `offset / 2` with weight 2, divided by 12. All taps are bilinear and clamped to the pass region. |
| Copy | Texel copy between `source_origin` and `dest_origin` of `size`. |
| ColorMatrix | Per pixel: unpremultiply (alpha 0 gives rgb 0), `out = clamp(M * [r g b a 1])` with the 4x5 row-major matrix applied to straight color, premultiply. Source and target extents must match. Implemented by the safe twin `aqua_kernels::color_matrix::reference`. |
| Custom | The reference runs a CPU kernel registered under `CustomPassSpec::name` (`Kernels::register_custom`); the standard set holds `aqua.invert`. A name with no kernel is `Unsupported`. Pass reads are inputs, the single write is the output; the HLSL contract is in section 14.9. |
| Compute | The reference runs a CPU kernel registered under the `ShaderId::Custom` id (`Kernels::register_compute`); the standard set holds id 1, a row box blur over 64-wide tiles (`groups = [ceil(w/64), h, 1]`, `params[0]` is the tap radius), so a partial dispatch leaves the rest of the target untouched. |
| Feature gating | A backend that reports `Features::color_matrix`, `compute` or `custom_passes` false is not asked to run plans that need them: the suite and the contract checks skip those cases (`conformance::requirements`) and report `SKIP: not offered by the backend`. Every other op is mandatory. |

### 12.2 Conformance suite

- `aqua_gpu_ref::conformance::run_suite(reference, backend, config)` runs hand-written cases (every op, clips, nested and sibling groups, degenerate geometry, odd extents) plus seeded fuzz scenes from one generator, then the backend contract checks (unknown surface, extent mismatch, invalid plan, determinism, surface reuse without residue, `recover`).
- The case list covers rounded clips (fractional, nested, under an outer clip, inside groups), bounded layers away from the origin and past the surface edge, backdrop replace on translucent surfaces, a blur-radius sweep across every octave boundary, color matrix, custom and compute passes, and explicit clear plans.
- Each pixel is classified `Interior`, `Soft` (shadow and blur footprints) or `Edge` (within `edge_band` pixels of any shape or clip boundary) and diffed per channel against the limit of its class. Defaults: interior 4, soft 8, edge 24 of 255 (measured on a Radeon RX 9070 XT and WARP: worst 1, 4 and 4).
- `tolerate_unsupported` turns `Unsupported` errors into reported skips. It was added for the wgpu adapter, which is deleted; no remaining backend uses it, and the D3D12 adapter must pass every case it is required to offer (`Features` gating, section 12.1).
- The D3D12 adapter runs the suite on every adapter preference through `aqua-verify/tests/d3d12_conformance.rs`, built with `aqua-win-build -p aqua-verify --test d3d12_conformance` and executed on Windows.
- The enforcement tests of section 4 live in `aqua-verify` (`tests/enforcement.rs`): `cargo metadata` purity over the Linux and Windows target graphs, `forbid(unsafe_code)`, and a source lint for clocks, threads, filesystem and stdio in pure crates. The lint ignores comments, strings and `#[cfg(test)]` items.

### 12.3 Verification harness and results (2026-11-04)

Everything lives in `crates/aqua-verify`; Linux runs the pure parts, Windows runs everything that touches Direct3D (`aqua-win-build -p aqua-verify --test NAME`, then run the `.exe`).

- **Exact counters and heap gates:** `src/heap.rs` (`CountingAllocator`, `Counts`, `HeapGate`, `measure`), `src/wake.rs` (`WakeTally` over `RenderWake`), `tests/heap_gates.rs`. Passing on Linux.
- **Frame-time distribution:** `src/stats.rs` (`Distribution` with p50, p90, p99, p99.9, max; `jank` counts frames over a budget and the longest run). `FrameReport.gpu_time` is now populated by the D3D12 adapter (timestamps), so the memory test reports both.
- **Private memory and VRAM budgets:** `src/budget.rs` (`MemoryBudget`, `RENDERER_OFFSCREEN`), `src/process.rs` (private bytes, working set, DXGI local usage), `tests/memory_budget.rs`.
- **Differential fuzzing across tiers:** `src/differential.rs` plans each fuzz scene under four planner variants (baseline, bindless, 4 KiB heap alignment, 256-texel texture limit); `tests/differential.rs` requires the oracle to render all four bit-identically (160 scenes, passing), and `tests/d3d12_differential.rs` runs 120 scenes x 4 variants on WARP and the RX 9070 XT across the Baseline, Bindless and Bindless+enhanced-barrier tiers against the oracle, tiers against each other (interior 1, soft 2, edge 2) and hardware against WARP.
- **Motion jump sweep:** `tests/motion_sweep.rs` retargets springs and tweens at random times, then requires the presented value to be independent of the sampling rate (30, 60, 144, 240 Hz against a 1440 Hz reference), no one-step jump in a 1 kHz trace, and exact settling on the target. Passing.
- **Replay determinism over the real runtime:** `tests/replay.rs` records seeded sessions of 800 inputs (resize, pointer, wheel, keys, text, IME, clipboard, accessibility attach, ticks, frames) through `Recorder<Runtime>`, replays them into fresh runtimes and requires identical outputs at every step, identical scene hashes and identical lowered-plan hashes (`aqua_transport::lower_items` over the render-side scene). It found and closed a real nondeterminism: `Runtime::reconcile` freed scene slots in `HashMap` iteration order, so replayed sessions reused slots in a different order; stale keys are now removed in `scene_order` order.
- **Runtime motion sweep:** `tests/motion_runtime.rs` drives the real runtime and render side with random wheel schedules: the presented content position is independent of the presentation rate (60, 144, 240 Hz against 1440 Hz), second differences stay under 0.25 px (no one-frame jump at wheel events or between frames), the glide settles exactly, popup fades are monotone, start at the declared value, end at exactly 1 and leave no track, group or opacity after a close, and first mount starts no motion.
- **Host-path counters:** `tests/host_counters.rs` runs the runtime step, render-side step and wake tallies together: an idle cycle is 0 heap events, 0 wakes and 0 presents on both sides; a wheel notch requests exactly `presented - 1` render-side `NextFrame` wakes and no app-side wake, then returns to 0; the render side allocates about 5 times per glide frame (gate 6). `tests/d3d12_host_counters.rs` gates the D3D12 adapter at 0 heap events per steady frame on hardware and WARP (it measured 20 per frame, identical every frame, before the adapter's per-frame allocations were removed).

- **Full-host verification (2026-11-04):** `tests/host_full.rs` drives the `aqua` facade (`Aqua`, `UiApp`, `RenderLink`, `RenderService`, `Lowering`, `GpuFrames`) headless over `RefBackend` and `OffscreenBackend` on WARP, the discrete and the integrated adapter, with an editor-like document (sidebar, 400 banded text rows): replay determinism of settled frames, host pixels against the Reference host (worst 1/255, 0 pixels over 24), scroll-glide jump detection from presented frames, and idle counters (0 presents, 0 render-thread waits, 0 app-thread heap events across `draw_ready` and `drain_render_events`; `Aqua::pump_inbox` allocates once per wake). `tests/host_window.rs` runs the same document through a real HWND on `WindowBackend` at 2560x1440: the `HOST_WINDOWED` budget (`RENDERER_WINDOWED` with a 16 MiB framework heap, measured 13.2 MiB for the document, fonts and scene), steady-state growth, input-to-scene latency (release gate: p99 4 ms and no input over 16.7 ms; measured p99 1.7 ms), and every hardware adapter presenting natively or across adapters (RX 9070 XT drives the monitor, the integrated GPU presents across adapters). `tests/d3d12_directwrite.rs` renders glyph runs on all four tier combinations of WARP, the discrete and the integrated adapter and compares them with the DirectWrite oracle (ink ratio and error) and with each other (at most 2/255). `tests/d3d12_differential.rs` now defaults to 400 fuzz scenes (`AQUA_DIFFERENTIAL_FUZZ` overrides; the corpus test requires exactly the requested number of scenes) and includes the integrated adapter. At 400 scenes, scene 234 fails identically on every adapter, tier and planner variant (edge 25 of 24): its first command, a hard-edged shadow (`sigma` 0.008, negative spread), differs from the oracle by 31 in the soft band when rendered alone. `tests/window_memory_budget.rs` adds the integrated adapter and, in release builds, a frame-CPU gate (p99 4 ms, no frame over 16.7 ms). `tools/compare-editors.sh` defaults to the release profile, builds both editors with `tools/compare-editors.sh build` (`AQUA_COMPARE_TARGETS` selects) and exits non-zero when a memory gate or a smoothness comparison against the GPUI editor fails.
- **Oracle speed:** shadow integration builds one profile per pixel row (Gaussian weights and silhouette widths), merges the constant-width taps and skips taps saturated beyond 6 sigma (erf is exactly 0 or 1 there). A 1440p frame with a sigma-40 shadow went from 48 s to about 1 s in release; `aqua-gpu-ref/tests/analytic.rs` checks it against a dense independent integration.
- **Windows clippy:** `tools/win-clippy.sh -p CRATE --tests` (also `aqua-win-clippy`) runs `cargo clippy --no-deps -D warnings` for `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` in its own target directory, behind the same two build slots.
- **Hot-path profile:** `tests/profile.rs` (`--ignored`, release).
- **Editor comparison:** `tools/compare-editors.sh` drives `win-app` (window-targeted, posted messages only) against the GPUI Editor and the Aqua Editor and prints one table; the new `win-drive.ps1` steps `cpu`, `gpumem`, `wakes`, `lat` and `scrolllat` sample process CPU time, private and working-set memory, dedicated GPU memory, thread context switches per second, and the round-trip latency of `SendMessageTimeout(WM_NULL)` while idle and while a wheel burst is posted (a UI-thread stall distribution that is comparable between the two apps).

- **Scoreboard gate (round 7, section 14.X):** `tools/compare-editors.sh` is the single gate. `tools/compare-editors.sh build` builds the fixture Forge, the port and the GPUI release editor; `tools/compare-editors.sh` runs three interleaved runs of the phases main, open, stream and keys (target order alternates per run), plus the aqua-only diagnostics wakesrc and spikes, and writes `scoreboard.txt`, `verdict.txt` and `manifest.txt` to `AQUA_COMPARE_OUT` (default `shots/compare`; the committed joint scoreboards live in `docs/scoreboard/`). `verdict.txt` has one PASS or FAIL line per gated row: the port must beat GPUI by at least 3% or tie at zero, plus completion, no focus theft, the section 11 memory limits and a quiet machine; the last line is OVERALL and the exit status follows it.

Results on the RX 9070 XT, WARP and this machine:

| Measurement | Result |
|---|---|
| Idle `Runtime::build_frame`, 100 to 4000 rows | 0 heap events, 0 runs shaped, 0 layout nodes measured |
| Idle `RenderSide::step`, 10 to 5000 items | 0 heap events, 0 wakes requested over 120 steps |
| Editor workload at 2560x1440 (16 passes, 4155 instances), RX 9070 XT, release | CPU submit p50 0.12 ms, p99 0.51 ms, max 0.84 ms; GPU p50 0.091 ms, p99 0.19 ms, max 0.27 ms; 0 of 600 frames over 16.7 ms |
| Same workload on WARP (software) | CPU p50 16.0 ms, p99 18.0 ms; 95 of 600 over 16.7 ms |
| Private memory, RX 9070 XT (added over bare process) | device and renderer +52.6 MiB (of which bare device 43), surface +15.0, first frame +4.4, warm-up +4.3, steady state +0.0; total 76 MiB; VRAM 31.6 MiB; renderer heap 0.36 MiB |
| Private memory, WARP | 37 MiB total; +3.8 MiB growth over 600 steady frames (WARP's own caches, not Aqua) |
| Differential, oracle against all planner variants | 160 scenes x 3 variants bit-identical |
| Differential, D3D12 tiers against each other and hardware against WARP | agree within interior 1, soft 2, edge 2 (WARP versus hardware default tolerance) |
| Differential, D3D12 against the oracle | 5 of 5 pass (re-run in round 4, `d3d12_differential`, 46 s): WARP and the RX 9070 XT across Baseline, Bindless and Bindless+enhanced barriers and all four planner variants, tiers against each other, hardware against WARP. The round 2 and 3 failure (72 of 480 runs per tier, from rounded clips, backdrop replace and continuous blur) is closed |
| Conformance, D3D12 against the oracle | 3 of 3 pass (re-run in round 4, `d3d12_conformance`): RX 9070 XT, the minimum-power adapter and WARP; 130 passing hand, fuzz and contract checks per adapter, no skips |
| Tiers | 6 of 6 pass (re-run in round 4, `tiers`): float on every tier combination on the RX 9070 XT, the integrated adapter and WARP, plus BGRA8 on the top tier on hardware and WARP; the default backend reaches the best tier the adapter offers |
| Motion sweep | 40 schedules x 4 rates, 60 jump traces, 40 settle traces: pass |
| GPUI Editor (debug build, 3 runs, median) | idle CPU 7.2% of one core, 5071 thread context switches per second, private 74 MiB, working set 77.5 MiB, dedicated GPU 53.6 MiB, 36 threads; UI-thread stall during a 600-wheel burst p50 0.06 ms, p99 2.4 ms, max 2.9 ms, none over 16.7 ms; 4.2 ms CPU per wheel message |
| Aqua Editor, same script | round 3 (not re-measured in round 4): the port launches on the native stack; scroll frames cost 60 to 90 ms (up to 139 ms) mostly in editor-side app work, 148 MB working set on the landing screen and 176 MB in the thread view, against the GPUI Editor's none over 16.7 ms; release `collections` showed 140.5 MiB private and 64.2 MiB GPU dedicated. The earlier wgpu-stack startup failure no longer applies |

Driver overhead included in the budget: the device floor (43 MiB) and the first-present cost (40 MiB) are measured on the RX 9070 XT; both are vendor-fixed. The GPUI figures come from a debug build; a release GPUI build was not available and the user's running Editor was not touched, so the 25 MB figure in the old budget could not be re-measured.

Round 6 status of what was listed here: replay over the full host composition is covered by `aqua-verify/tests/host_replay.rs`; a presence and layout-edge sweep over the real host runs in `aqua/tests/host_motion.rs` (offscreen D3D12) and `aqua/tests/host_swapchain.rs` (real swapchain window, pixels read back through `PrintWindow`); the DirectWrite oracle runs on every D3D12 tier (`d3d12_directwrite`, round 4); the `debug_layer` flake is root-caused (14.17); windowed (swapchain) memory is gated. Integrated-GPU memory was not measured. Not verifiable under the no-global-input rule: real touch and pen, a cross-process OLE file drop (section 12.4).

Round 7 gate stability (verification area). Every change below cites the failure it removes.

- **Timestamp labels:** the per-pass timestamp labels (`scene/timestamps.rs`) already reuse preallocated vectors; `aqua-gpu-d3d12/tests/heap.rs` measured 0 allocations and 0 bytes per steady frame on all 56 combinations (WARP and the RX 9070 XT, Baseline and Bindless+enhanced barriers, 14 cases) three times in debug and twice in release. `d3d12_host_counters` and `heap_gates` also pass. The gates were not loosened.
- **`host_full` scroll glide (99 frames per notch on WARP):** the glide is time based, so the frame count is the glide duration divided by max(render interval, frame cost). The test attached the render link with a 2 ms interval, so a backend that rendered faster than the readback produced up to 99 frames and a slower one 20. The test now uses a 16.667 ms interval (one 60 Hz refresh); all four backends then present 19 frames per notch in release and debug. The step list now starts from the frame before the wheel, so the 77 px total is complete when the first glide frame already moved.
- **Refresh pacing (`warp_vsync`, `hardware_vsync`):** reproduced by starving the machine with 24 busy loops (4 of 6 runs failed with 1.5x to 6x the refresh interval). The measurement thread and WARP's worker threads were simply not scheduled. The tests now raise the process to the high priority class and the thread to time critical (`tests/support::prioritized`); the same stress then passes 5 of 5 on WARP and hardware (mean 6.04 to 6.11 ms against a 6.061 ms refresh).
- **`render_service` arrival, repaint, trim, share:** a viewport alone is a frame, so when the render thread picked up the viewport before the scene the tests saw one extra present (14 against 13, 5 against 4). Reproduced under 32 busy loops (13 test failures over 30 runs). The tests now wait for the viewport frame before scripting the scenario and count presents from that baseline; 40 of 40 runs pass under the same stress.
- **Memory budget:** the hardware run was once 99.6 MiB against the 96 MiB limit, entirely in the device and renderer stage (+75.7 MiB against +48.7 MiB in 13 other runs), right after other agents rebuilt the renderer. The offscreen backend compiles its pipelines without the on-disk pipeline library, so a driver shader cache miss compiles inside the process. The test now runs one priming child process first so the budget is measured against a warm driver cache. This is a hypothesis fitting the evidence (cold compile leaves about 27 MiB of driver memory), not a proof; a first launch on a machine that never ran the shaders is likely to pay it once.
- **Probe rows:** `heap_gates` now runs every runtime and render-side gate with a probe sink installed, adds `an_active_probe_sink_adds_no_allocations_to_idle_frames` (the sink must have seen spans and counts) and `the_profile_recorder_allocates_nothing_per_event_or_per_frame` (200 frames of every phase span nested, with counts, `begin` and `finish`). `tests/probe_heap.rs` compares steady `GpuFrames` presents on WARP without and with the sink: 72 heap events either way, so the probe adds 0. The 72 events per forced host present are the lowering path, not the probe.
