- **Idle wake gate:** `tools/idle-gate.sh` (phase `idlegate` of `compare-editors.sh`, step `wakelines` of `win-drive.ps1`) opens the fixture thread, idles 8 s, then counts new `aqua-wakes:` lines in the app's stderr over 10 s and fails unless the count is 0. `AQUA_COMPARE_GATE_CONTROL=1` scrolls right before the window and requires lines to appear, proving the gate can see wakes.
- **Facade list:** `pump_sent_messages` was added to the declared root items of `aqua-shell-win32` (it is the shell side of the core checkpoint hook).
- **Linux-only tests:** `banned`, `enforcement`, `lint_scanner` and `kernels` read the source tree and run on Linux only; their Windows executables fail by design and are excluded from Windows sweeps.

### 12.4 Shell, TSF and UIA hardware gaps (round 4)

Verified without global input, on Windows:

- **Touch and pen translation:** `aqua-shell-win32/tests/pointer.rs` (pure, also runs on Linux) covers concurrent contacts with distinct ids, `UP` winning over a stale `INCONTACT`, eraser plus barrel, tip swaps emitting releases before presses, lifting with several buttons held, cancel, id offset against the mouse contact and position passthrough. It found a real defect: release events after the first reported the original button mask instead of the cumulative one, so `translate` now folds the mask transition by transition.
- **In-process drop:** `tests/windows.rs` calls the registered `IDropTarget` directly with real shell data objects: one file in logical coordinates, a non-file object (`DROPEFFECT_NONE`, no file events, a single `Leave`), and enter followed by leave with no drop.
- **TSF key eating:** `aqua-ime-tsf/tests/tsf.rs` drives `Keystrokes::pre_translate` against a test double of `ITfKeystrokeMgr` (`Keystrokes::with_manager`) that eats one virtual key: eaten keys run test then deliver for both phases, other keys and `WM_CHAR` pass through, and nothing is asked of the service while the IME is disabled. A plain TSF client cannot advertise a key event sink (`AdviseKeyEventSink` returns `E_INVALIDARG` for a thread-manager client id that is not a registered text service), and registering a real text service would change the user's input profile, so the double replaces the keystroke manager rather than the text service.
- **Toast, local time:** `tests/toast.rs` shows, replaces and withdraws a real toast under a test AppUserModelID and rejects invalid content before the native API; `tests/local_time.rs` checks the locale formatting of `clock` and `short_date`, including invalid parts.
- **ShellExecute:** the URL, path and reveal targets are validated by `tests/open.rs`. `OpenTarget::execute` is not called by tests because it launches the associated application and can take foreground focus.

Added in round 10, all in process and without global input:

- **WM_POINTER through the real window procedure:** `aqua_shell_win32::pointer_source` is a seam (`PointerSource`, `Reading`, `set_pointer_source`) that supplies what `GetPointerType`, `GetPointerInfo` and `GetPointerPenInfo` would return for a pointer id; with no source installed the window procedure reads the system. `tests/pointer_window.rs` installs a digitizer double and sends real `WM_POINTERDOWN/UPDATE/UP/CAPTURECHANGED/LEAVE` messages to a shell window: touch down, move and up in logical coordinates (screen position through `ScreenToClient` and the window scale), two simultaneous contacts with independent ids, pen barrel as `Secondary` and eraser tip as `Extra(5)`, cancel by lost capture and by `POINTER_FLAG_CANCELED`, `PointerLeft` only for a held contact, and unknown ids producing no input. What stays unverified is the system's own pointer plumbing (`InjectSyntheticPointerInput` would deliver to whatever window is under the point on the user's desktop, which is global input).
- **TSF through a test text service:** `aqua-ime-tsf/tests/text_service.rs` implements `ITfTextInputProcessor` and `ITfCompositionSink` in the test, activates the service on a real thread manager and drives composition through the real `ITfContextComposition`: a clause conversion with caret moves inside the marked text (byte cursors for multi-byte kana), composition after astral text (UTF-16 to UTF-8 cursor conversion), a composition over a selection followed by a second one (one `Start` and one `Commit` each), app-side `CancelComposition` (no commit) and `CommitComposition`. The service is not registered in the user's input profile (that would change the profile); a key event sink cannot be advised without registration, so key eating stays covered by the keystroke manager double above.
- **UIA through a UIA client:** `aqua-a11y-uia/tests/uia_windows.rs` adds the control-view hierarchy (parent, first, next and last child, `FindAll` children), enabled and keyboard-focusable state, `SetFocus` arriving as `A11yAction::Focus`, and text ranges that move by character over multi-byte text (`h`, `é`, `o`), clamp at the end and `Select` as a byte selection (1, 3), next to the invoke, toggle, value, range-value and text pattern tests.
- **Low-memory trim:** `aqua::render::pressure::watch_notification` injects a real kernel event in place of the OS low-memory resource notification; `aqua/tests/pressure.rs` covers the rising-edge and poll-interval logic and `aqua/tests/host_trim.rs` drives the host trim through the injected event. The OS raising that notification itself cannot be provoked without exhausting the machine.

Not verifiable under the no-global-input rule (documented, not claimed): physical touch and pen digitizer input reaching the window through the system's own `WM_POINTER` plumbing (the translation and the window procedure are covered through the `PointerSource` seam), a cross-process OLE drag from Explorer, an installed third-party text service (an IME composing and eating keys end to end; composition itself is covered by the in-test text service), toast activation by clicking, `ShellExecute` launching a handler, and the clipboard round trip with another process. Each has its pure or in-process part tested above.

## 13. Migration order

Each step keeps the app running. Status as of the round 4 audit (2026-11-04); results are from reports except where section 14 says re-run.

| Step | Work | Status | Evidence and what remains |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Remove `aqua-macros` and the `ui!` references | done | no such crate and no `ui!` or `aqua-macros` mention in any source or manifest (grep, round 6) |
| 2 | `aqua-protocol` and `aqua-runtime`; time as input | done | `Runtime: Machine` over `RuntimeInput`/`RuntimeOutput`, replay and divergence tests pass (record 14.9); the host steps it in `native::looping`. The `Application` trait stays as the Editor port's compatibility layer (section 9.2) |
| 3 | Wrap wgpu as a temporary backend; split `aqua-render` into `aqua-scene`, `aqua-graph` and the adapter | done, then reverted | `aqua-scene` and `aqua-graph` stay; `aqua-render` and `aqua-gpu-wgpu` are deleted (step 11) |
| 4 | `aqua-gpu-ref` and the conformance suite | done | 167 cases (119 base plus 48 fuzz) and 11 contract checks; the oracle renders all planner variants identically; D3D12 matches it on WARP and both hardware adapters (section 12.3); the wgpu adapter it was first compared with is deleted |
| 5 | Analytic tracks and fixed-size channels | done for popups and scroll glide | `aqua-motion` `Track`, `TrackSet`, `AppMotion`; author-driven `MotionController` values sample app side by decision (10.6); popups, scroll glide, declarative animate and presence run on render-side tracks; the taffy SlotMap panic is fixed (record 14.M) |
| 6 | GPU adapter passes conformance on all tiers, then delete the wgpu adapter | done for D3D11 (default) and D3D12 (stashed) | D3D11 is the default since 2026-11-05 (6.7): conformance 9 variants with zero skips, differential, debug layer clean, 0 steady allocations, window tests 58 of 58, all against `aqua-gpu-ref`. D3D12 conformance, differential, tiers and `debug_layer` passed in the last clean sweep and the adapter stays selectable (`AQUA_GPU=d3d12`) but gets no more investment; fence barriers are not available in the bindings and stay off (14.A); `aqua-gpu-wgpu` is deleted |
| 7 | `aqua-shell-win32`, `aqua-ime-tsf`, `aqua-a11y-uia`; delete winit | done except unverifiable inputs | the three adapters are wired into `native::looping::Host`; `winit`, `accesskit_winit` and `aqua-platform` are gone from the tree and the lockfile (`aqua-verify` `banned`); unverified without global input: real touch/pen (`WM_POINTER*`) and a cross-process file drop; Mica pixels are verified on the backend (14.B) and on a host window (14.N, `tests/host_material.rs`) |
| 8 | Render side on its own thread with `aqua-transport`; split `aqua-motion` | done | `spawn_render_thread_with`, `GpuRenderHost`, the inspector socket and the trim policy exist; the host lowers display items itself (`render/lower.rs`) rather than through `GeometryLowering`, which still ignores text, images and vectors; one render service thread for all windows over one shared device (14.D, 14.N) |
| 9 | Replace cosmic-text and glyphon with HarfRust, skrifa and the DirectWrite oracle | done | `aqua-text`, `aqua-fonts-system`, `aqua-oracle-dwrite` pass on Windows; strikes (EBDT, CBDT, sbix), fallback index, residency and the prewarm worker are done; `glyphon` and `cosmic-text` are gone; Slug curve rendering is built for scaled, rotated and large text with an LRU store (14.O, 14.T); animated vector paths and icons use the CPU-rasterized atlas by decision (14.O) |
| 10 | Declarative motion, presence and the debug-mode read check | done and wired into `aqua-runtime` | animate/presence in `aqua-ui` and `aqua-style`, read check in `aqua-reactive`; `aqua-runtime/tests/declarative_motion.rs` drives fill, text colour, translate, edge, row presence and popup presence through `RenderSide`; keyed slide, snapshot transitions and text raster mode are in 10.6; `aqua/tests/host_motion.rs` drives presence slide, edge animation, popup slide and a snapshot transition through the full host loop on D3D12 (WARP and RX 9070 XT); quad pulse and shimmer are per-item render-thread cycles (14.N); `aqua-motion/tests/cycle.rs`, `aqua/tests/render/animation.rs` and `aqua-verify/tests/host_animation.rs` verify them |
| 11 | Delete the legacy stack (`aqua-render`, `aqua-gpu-wgpu`, `aqua-platform`; `wgpu`, `winit`, `glyphon`, `cosmic-text`, `accesskit_winit`) | done | the three crates are deleted; no manifest, lockfile package or source path names the banned crates (`aqua-verify` `banned`, 4 of 4); the host and the Editor port compile against the native stack |

Latest joint scoreboard (`docs/scoreboard/round-9-end`, release, fixture, 3 interleaved runs, median, RX 9070 XT, quiet machine at 6.5 to 6.8% system CPU): OVERALL FAIL, 13 gated rows FAIL (no scoreboard was run in round 10). The port beats GPUI on: every ETW keystroke row by about 10x (p50 1.76 vs 17.60 ms), scroll p90 (0.31 vs 1.31), p99 (0.46 vs 2.66) and max (0.75 vs 3.26 ms), cold start to first content (168 vs 210 ms) and to thread painted (386 vs 485), open large thread (190 vs 221 ms), streaming CPU (8.5 vs 12.2%), streaming p99 (0.37 vs 1.83 ms), resize p99 and max (2.81 and 3.01 vs 3.97 and 4.00 ms), idle CPU and idle context switches (0.13 vs 0.20 per second). Gated rows still FAIL: scroll p50 (0.09 vs 0.05 ms), CPU per scroll input (9.48 vs 3.90 ms; the metric spans the idle latency probe and the screenshot, so it is mis-scoped), resize p50 (1.94 vs 1.63), streaming p50 (0.07 vs 0.05), streaming frames over 4 ms (1 vs 1), idle busiest thread (0.10 vs 0.10 per second, the Forge heartbeat; a tie only passes at 0), private MiB after startup and after 600 scrolls (122.1 and 125.0 vs 115.3 and 118.7), GPU dedicated MiB (49.9 and 51.9 vs 49.1 and 51.1). The section 11 limits pass (125.0 of 142 MiB private, 51.9 of 64 MiB GPU dedicated). The working set rows are flattered by a trimmed working set. The visual gate found no clear renderer defect but is a weak comparison (scroll offsets differ, no pair has text behind the blur); the rail hover menu (turn navigator card: rows lack ellipsis, glass card spans to the window edge) is wrong in the port and release-blocking, and emoji and blur over text are unverified. The AMD idle spin on D3D12 is fixed (`settle_amd_frame_tracker`, `aqua-gpu-d3d12/src/window/backend.rs`); D3D11 shows no spin and the tracker settle was not ported (6.7) |

The end state requires steps 6, 7, 11 and 12. Steps 1 to 11 are done in the senses stated above; step 12 is the only step that blocks the end state.

### 13.1 Deviations record (round 6, refreshed in round 8)

Re-run on Windows in round 6: the list at the end of the table in section 14. Nothing in this record is taken from an earlier round without saying so.

1. Step 12 is the only open step. Editor scroll smoothness, idle wakes and memory are measured against the GPUI Editor with `tools/compare-editors.sh` on the fixture; the latest joint scoreboard (`docs/scoreboard/round-9-end`) is OVERALL FAIL with 13 gated rows failing (table row 12 has the numbers). Open gaps: scroll p50 0.09 vs 0.05 ms, CPU per scroll input 9.48 vs 3.90 ms (mis-scoped metric), resize p50 1.94 vs 1.63 ms, streaming p50 0.07 vs 0.05 ms and frames over 4 ms tied at 1, idle busiest thread tied at 0.10 per second, private bytes 122.1 to 125.0 vs 115.3 to 118.7 MiB, GPU dedicated 49.9 to 51.9 vs 49.1 to 51.1 MiB. The D3D11 decision is made (14.4); the port runs the D3D11 default and `cargo clippy -p editor-aqua` is clean under the port's lint set (14.U).
2. Fence barriers are absent by necessity (14.A). Enhanced barriers are the tier ceiling.
3. The host attaches a DirectComposition surface for transparent windows (14.B, 14.N); Mica, Mica Alt and Acrylic pixels on a host window are verified (`tests/host_material.rs`, round 5), with the window inactive (flat fallback) and, in one run, active for Mica.
4. Replay and counters over the full host loop are covered: `aqua-verify/tests/host_replay.rs` (determinism, slow motion, stepping), `host_wakes.rs` and `host_counters.rs` (section 12.3).
5. Two lowerings exist by decision. `GeometryLowering` in `aqua-transport` lowers geometry for the transport tests, the inspector and the replay harness; the host's `aqua::render::lower` also lowers text, images and vectors because those depend on the host's atlas, residency and image services, which the transport crate must not own.
6. Stated approximations. `ROUNDED_CLIP_AREA_CAP` in `aqua/src/render/lower.rs` is gone (the planner draws large rounded clips through bands and corner layers, 11.5); rounded clips are exact at any size when the four radii are equal, and a per-corner radii clip is still reported as approximated by the lowering (`Fidelity::Approximated` in `push_clip`), which is the one place a uniform-radius analytic clip stands in for different radii. What remains, all counted in `approximated_items` or reported by the lowering: (a) clips, shadows, blurs and backdrop blurs under a rotation or skew use the bounding box of the transformed rectangle; (b) per-corner rounded clips with unequal radii, as above; (c) the Slug path renders text only, and animated vector paths and icons rasterize into the atlas (item 7); (d) blur is continuous in the radius but the half-resolution output used from three pyramid levels is bilinear with offsets scaled by 1.03, which is not bit-identical to the oracle; (e) the mesh viewport is a CPU rasterizer with no oracle (14.P); (f) text is lowered at the glyph level, and the DirectWrite ink ratio of 0.80 to 1.12 (mid-tone backdrops 0.88 to 0.96) is an accepted deviation (14.14), not an approximation counted per item. Quads, images and text rotate and skew exactly (14.O), verified by pixel on D3D12 and in a real swapchain window in round 6. The declarative `Style::transform` accepts any finite transform with a nonzero determinant (bounding boxes, culling and accessibility bounds use the transformed rectangle; hit testing inverts the matrix) and lowers through `PushTransform`. Quad pulses and shimmer are render-thread cycles that repaint only their footprint and post nothing to the app (14.N).
7. Slug renders text only; animated vector paths and icons rasterize into the atlas by decision (14.O).
8. The pipeline library is stored as one file under the user cache directory and relies on the library's own driver validation rather than a per-LUID file name (14.C).
9. The `debug_layer` flake is root-caused (14.17).
10. Every window has its own `Runtime`; `UiApp` and `ManagedUiApp` (and the Editor port's `ShellApplication`) answer the per-window `Application` methods. The render side is shared (14.D).
11. Out of scope on this machine, so unverified by design: the system's own `WM_POINTER` delivery from real touch and pen hardware, a cross-process file drop, an installed third-party IME or screen reader, the OS raising its low-memory notification, and a toast activation from the shell UI. Round 10 covers the in-process parts of each: the `PointerSource` seam with real `WM_POINTER*` messages, an in-test TSF text service, a UIA client over hierarchy, focus and text ranges, and an injected low-memory kernel event through the host trim (12.4).
12. Closed in round 9: the D3D11 default is recorded in sections 4, 6, 11 and 14.4; the `aqua-verify` memory, host, probe-heap and full-host tests run on `aqua-gpu-d3d11` (the D3D12-only `window_memory_budget` p99 failure is gone with the retarget; it passes on D3D11 at 50.8 MiB video memory and 56 to 98 MiB private; the `d3d12_*` tests stay as opt-in D3D12 coverage); the offscreen video-memory gate is 56 MiB on D3D11 (11); `ADAPTER_ROOT_ITEMS` declares `ledger` and `wake_trace` and the Linux enforcement, banned, kernels and lint tests pass (27 tests); `aqua-verify` `motion_edges` reads the scroll translation from the property-tree transform after the runtime moved scroll offsets out of edge coordinates; `tools/sweep.sh` builds and runs `aqua-gpu-d3d11` tests, treats `aqua-gpu-d3d12` failures as advisory (listed, not gating) and retries the desktop-sensitive exes (`host_trim`, `host_material`, `host_swapchain`) once, printing the retry. Flakes root-caused: the hardware vsync pacing test required presents to land on DWM composition ticks, but with the window on a 165 Hz monitor while DWM composes at 240 Hz (4.167 ms) the cadence is either the 4.2/8.4 ms tick pattern or the monitor's 6.06 ms; the test now accepts either period and retries up to three attempts; the materials pixel test retries up to three attempts; `host_replay` passed in every one of 11 sweeps. `host_trim` and `host_material` failed once in one sweep (sweep 3) and passed on every later run: they react to occlusion and trims, and other agents' windows on the shared desktop cover the test window. Three consecutive clean release sweeps (sweeps 9, 10 and 11 of round 9: 238 of 238 executables, no retries, no advisory failures, zero skips, session unlocked) include `host_material`, `host_swapchain`, Mica, Acrylic and the clipboard tests, which were previously skipped under a locked session.
13. Open items after round 10, each with its decision (record 14.DC): the joint scoreboard and the visual gate are produced by the scoreboard keeper at the end of each round and are not work-agent tasks; retained emit for clean subtrees, layout reuse across remounts and a subtree refresh API in `aqua-ui` stay performance packages for the next round (14.RT, 14.SP); idle attribution under load is measured on a quiet machine with the passive trace (14.IQ); the `compare-editors.sh` lock-holder scan is replaced by the private A/B mode (`AQUA_COMPARE_PRIVATE=1`, TOOLS.md); the `zz_sizes` scratch test no longer exists; `cargo fmt --check` is clean in all 25 crates since round 11 (each crate was formatted once, no agent mid-edit in that crate's formatting pass); the session-locked desktop tests (`host_material`, `host_swapchain`, Mica, Acrylic, clipboard) need an unlocked session and their status is recorded in 12.3.

## 14. Decisions

Audit of 2026-11-04, round 6, against the code and the tests named in each record. Resolved means decided and the decision is implemented; a limit named in the row is a stated, out-of-scope boundary (an input device or product the machine cannot supply), not an open item. The one decision that was the user's, the D3D11 backend (14.4), was made on 2026-11-05. Every row is decided. Rows 14.N, 14.P (mesh), 14.Q and 14.R were re-run on Windows in round 6 (the list follows the table); the other results come from earlier rounds and are marked as such where they matter.

On Linux: `aqua-verify` `enforcement` (9 of 9), `banned` (6 of 6) and `lint_scanner` (8 of 8); the facade test caught one new root item of `aqua-transport` (`IDLE_TRIM_AFTER`), which is now in `ADAPTER_ROOT_ITEMS`. On Windows (executables built in round 6, run natively from WSL): `d3d12_conformance` 3 of 3 (WARP, minimum-power and high-performance adapters), `d3d12_differential` 9 of 9 at the default fuzz size on one test thread (172 s), `render_service` 6 of 6, `render` 46 of 46, `render_window` 1 of 1, `render_shared`, `host_native`, `host_windows`, `clipboard_host` 1 of 1, `host_motion` 5 of 5 and `host_swapchain`. `aqua-win-clippy -p aqua-layout --tests` is clean (exit 0). Record numbering: 14.N is the render service; the mesh viewport is 14.P, host lowering 14.Q and host input 14.R (formerly duplicate numbers 14.N and unlettered 14.); the keyed-slide and snapshot-transition decision of 10.6 is 14.L and the text stack closure is 14.T (it was a second 14.R). The `fontdb` edge `aqua-media -> resvg -> usvg -> fontdb` is resolved (record 14.E): `resvg` is built without `text` and `system-fonts`, so SVG `<text>` is not rendered and no font database exists in the dependency graph; `banned` fails on any manifest, lockfile package, source path or crate-list deviation from section 4.

### Closed decisions (formerly open)

**14.A Fence barriers.** Closed 2026-11-04 (round 4), off. Evidence re-checked this round: no `FenceBarrier`, `FENCE_BARRIER` or `BARRIER_TYPE_FENCE` symbol exists in `windows` 0.62.2 and none in `d3d12*.h` of Windows SDK 10.0.26100.0 (only `D3D12_BARRIER_TYPE_GLOBAL`, `TEXTURE` and `BUFFER`), so the API cannot be called from here. Earlier text: Preference: stay off until the bindings expose them. State: `hal::features` and `hal::tier` carry a `fence_barriers` flag that is always false; neither `windows` 0.62 nor the 10.0.26100 headers expose the API, nothing depends on the flag, and the tier is untested. Decision needed: none before the bindings ship; then enable behind the same tier flag and run `tests/tiers.rs` with it. Impact: only the synchronization cost between the render queue and upload copies, which the 0.12 ms p50 CPU submit does not show as a problem. Decision (2026-11-04, round 4, final): the question is closed until Microsoft ships the API. `windows` 0.62.2 (`D3D12_BARRIER_TYPE` has only `GLOBAL`, `TEXTURE` and `BUFFER`) and the 10.0.26100 headers contain no fence barrier type, no feature query and no command list method, so the tier cannot be implemented or exercised; `Tiers::fence_barriers` is false on every device and `Tiers::capped` can never turn it on. Queue-level fences stay the synchronization primitive between the render queue and uploads. Re-open when the bindings expose it: add the flag to `hal::features`, gate it in `tiers::resolve`, and add a fifth combination to `tests/tiers.rs` and `tests/debug_layer.rs`. Earlier decision (round 3): fence barriers stay off. The adapter has no code path for them, `Tiers::fence_barriers` is false on every device, and no test or budget depends on them; the enhanced-barrier tier already removes the per-pass legacy transition cost that they would otherwise help with.

**14.B DirectComposition path.** State: `aqua-shell-win32::composition` exists (Mica and backdrop materials); the D3D12 `WindowBackend` presents through `CreateSwapChainForHwnd` and has no composition swapchain. Consequences today: `WindowError::Unsupported` for transparent windows and materials (record 14.15), and Mica pixels are unverified. Closed 2026-11-04 (round 4). Decision: a composition swapchain (`create_composition_surface`, premultiplied alpha, attached with `Window::attach_composition_surface`) only for windows that need an alpha surface (`material::needs_alpha_surface`: Mica, Mica Alt, Acrylic or a transparent window); every other window keeps the hwnd swapchain, so the common path pays no composition cost. Evidence (re-run): `window/composition.rs` 6 of 6 on the RX 9070 XT and WARP (Mica, Mica Alt and Acrylic show the backdrop through alpha 0, opaque quads exact, survival of resize and device loss), measured through `PrintWindow`. Not measured: the memory delta of a composition chain against the hwnd chain, and `Present1` dirty rects on a composition chain; neither changes the decision because composition is opt-in. The host now does this (round 4, record 14.N): `native/gpu.rs` creates the composition surface for a window whose clear colour is transparent (which `Config::resolve` forces for every material), hands its content to the UI thread through `CompositionSink` and `NativeWindow::attach_composition`, and hands it over again after a recovery. Verified on Windows that the surface attaches and re-attaches after an injected device loss (`tests/render_shared.rs`); Mica, Mica Alt and Acrylic pixels on the host window are checked in round 5 (`tests/host_material.rs`, 14.N).

**14.C Pipeline cache persistence.** Preference: persist. State: `PipelineSet` builds and serializes an `ID3D12PipelineLibrary` (`scene/pipeline.rs`, `SceneRenderer::serialize`) and accepts bytes at construction, ignoring corrupt or foreign data; at the time of writing the host passed `None` and never wrote the bytes. Closed 2026-11-04 (round 4): persist, and it is implemented. The host (`render/persist.rs`, `native/gpu.rs`) reads `pipelines.cache` from the user cache directory before device creation, passes it to `WindowBackend::with_pipeline_cache`, and writes it back when its fingerprint changed; a corrupt or foreign library is ignored and the pipelines compile (`tests/pipeline_cache.rs`: warm start compiles 0 and loads every cold-compiled pipeline on WARP and the RX 9070 XT; a corrupt library compiles). One file rather than one per adapter, because `ID3D12PipelineLibrary` rejects data from another adapter or driver itself. Cold versus warm timing, measured by running the test executables natively: the whole `hardware_reloads` test (two device creations, a cold build, a warm load) takes 0.31 to 0.36 s on the RX 9070 XT, the `warp_reloads` test 0.09 s, and the cold-only corrupt-library test on WARP 0.07 to 0.09 s, so the warm start saves less than the test noise (about 20 ms) here. The pipelines come from prebuilt DXIL, which is why. The cache stays because it is already written and costs one small file, but it is not what decides time to first frame.

**14.D Shared device.** State at the time of writing: one render thread and backend per window in the host, so a second window pays the 43 MiB device floor again. Closed 2026-11-04 (round 4): the device is shared at the backend. One `WindowBackend` owns one `Core`, queue, renderer and texture set for any number of windows (record 14.8), and `window/shared_device.rs` passes 4 of 4 on the RX 9070 XT and WARP (two windows of different size, resize and close of one leaving the other untouched, loss and recovery rebuilding every window). The host now uses it (round 4, record 14.N): all windows run on one render thread over one device, so a second window no longer pays the 43 MiB device floor. `Runtime` still serves one window, which is separate from the render side.

### Decision records

### 14.P Mesh viewport (decided 2026-11-04, `aqua::render::mesh`, `aqua::ExternalFrame`)
Meshes stay a CPU-rasterized image path: the host rasterizes `DisplayItem::GpuiMesh` (up to 400,000 triangles) at the item's pixel size, caches it per mesh id and style, and lowers it as an image item. A GPU mesh pass is not added: the port's viewport is a preview, the oracle has no depth-tested triangle pass to conform against, and the D3D12 plan stays a 2D display list. `ExternalFrame::meshes_supported()` returns true so applications enable the viewport; reopen if a workload needs interactive frame rates on large meshes. `aqua` no longer depends on `accesskit` directly; its accessibility test uses the re-export from `aqua-a11y-uia`.

### 14.1 Text look (decided 2026-11-04)

one consistent rasterizer on every platform: unhinted skrifa outlines, grayscale coverage at 4 horizontal subpixel positions with y snapped, no LCD subpixel rendering. The Windows native look is reproduced at blend time by the Microsoft gamma and contrast model keyed on text luminance (`aqua_text::tone::ToneCurve::native`, gamma 1.8, grayscale contrast 1.0). Evidence (section 8.1): unhinted coverage agrees with DirectWrite to a few percent, shaping agrees exactly, and the tone curve brings blended ink within about 6% of DirectWrite's own grayscale output, where the plain coverage was off by 13 to 22%. DirectWrite grid fitting is deliberately not matched (it moves stems vertically by up to a pixel at 13 px); ClearType color fringing is not offered. DirectWrite stays test-only.

### 14.2 UIA: decided, AccessKit's Windows adapter

(`accesskit_windows` 0.35 `SubclassingAdapter`, no winit). Evidence, 2026-11-04: the runtime already emits an AccessKit-shaped snapshot (roles, bounds, text runs, selection) and `aqua-a11y-uia` feeds it through unchanged; a real UIA client (`IUIAutomation`, `FindAll` over the descendants, the Invoke pattern) round-trips against it on Windows in `crates/aqua-a11y-uia/tests/uia_windows.rs`: control types, names, bounding rectangles, Invoke arriving as `A11yAction::Press`, and live relabeling after an update. Hand-written UIA would have to reimplement the element, fragment, fragment-root, text, value, toggle, expand-collapse, range-value, selection and grid providers plus the event queue, with no behavior the adapter lacks. The adapter costs nothing until a client connects: attaching installs one window subclass, `request_initial_tree` only flips the bridge to connected and wakes the host, and no tree is built while dormant. Two constraints follow from it: attach before the window is first shown (`UiaAdapter::attach` returns `WindowAlreadyVisible` instead of panicking), and a `Role::Label` node gets its name from `value`, so the snapshot copies the label into the value for text nodes.

### 14.3 Render thread ownership

decided 2026-11-04: a pure state machine driven by the host. `aqua_runtime::RenderSide::step(RenderInput) -> RenderOutput` owns the presented scene and a `aqua_motion::TrackSet`, reads no clock and spawns no thread. The host (`aqua-transport::spawn_render_thread` with a `RenderHost` supplying the clock, the frame wait and GPU execution) owns the thread and moves `RenderCommand`/`RenderEvent` over a safe SPSC ring. App side: `aqua_motion::AppMotion` (targets, policy, owners, completions; never samples) plus `aqua_runtime::emit_scene_diff` (drains the app `Scene` into a `SceneDiff`). Completion callbacks fire on the app side when a `Settled { track, generation }` event with the current generation arrives. Motion times are `f64` seconds on the host clock (`Instant::as_secs_f64`); presentation time arrives as `RenderInput::present_at`. Round 2 (2026-11-04): `aqua_transport::GpuRenderHost<B, L, C>` is the `RenderHost` over any `GpuBackend<Error = GpuError>` (the D3D12 `WindowBackend`, `OffscreenBackend`, `RefBackend`). It lowers the render-side scene to `PaintCommand`s through a `Lowering` (`GeometryLowering`: quads, shadows, clips, opacity, content and backdrop blur, translation transforms; text, images and vectors are counted as `unsupported_items` until a richer lowering is plugged in), plans with the backend's `plan_limits`, waits for the frame slot once per frame (`wait_for_frame_slot`, the swapchain's waitable object on D3D12), executes and presents. `spawn_render_thread_with` builds the host on the render thread, because COM-backed backends are not `Send`; the loop now waits on the frame slot before sampling tracks, so `present_at` is the clock plus one frame interval measured after the wait, and `RenderWake::At` blocks on the command ring instead of spinning. A device failure is reported as `Feedback::DeviceLost`; the next frame calls `recover`, recreates the surface and reports `DeviceRecovered`, which makes `RenderSide` resubmit. `RenderSide` now submits a frame when the viewport changes as well as when the scene revision does. The inspector (`aqua_transport::InspectorServer`) is a loopback-only TCP socket speaking one JSON line per request: `stats` (frames presented and failed, device losses, scene items, tracks, last frame record with passes, instances, barriers, CPU and GPU time, present queue time, unsupported items), `tree` (the accessibility-tree JSON the host publishes) and `watch` (a line per published change); it refuses non-loopback addresses. Popups and scroll glide are routed through tracks. `OverlayHost` owns the single app-side `AppMotion` (`tracks_mut`); `animate_popup(overlay, PopupMotion { property, from, to, kind }, now)` registers an opacity or translate animation, the runtime wraps that popup's portal subtree in a `PushOpacity`/`PushTransform` group (`SceneKey::PortalStart`/`PortalEnd`), binds a track to the group's scene node once it exists and emits `Spawn` and `SetTarget` after the scene diff; closing the popup removes the track. Every scroll node's content is wrapped in an identity `PushTransform` inside its clip (`SceneKey::ScrollContentStart`/`ScrollContentEnd`). A wheel notch moves the logical scroll offset to its final, clamped value at once (layout, virtualization and hit-testing see the target state) and queues the applied distance; the runtime then emits `MotionCommand::Shift`, which moves the presented translation by that distance and retargets to zero with the carried velocity, so the render side glides the content with a critically damped spring (`glide::kind`, 0.25 s) and the app side does no work while it glides. `Track::retarget` and `Track::shift` now advance the track to the retarget time before launching the new segment, which removes a position error of up to one frame of travel when a retarget lands between samples. `MotionController` remains for author-driven values that need app-side sampling. Remaining: damage-limited presentation from the scene footprints, and moving author-driven `MotionController` values onto `AppMotion`.

### 14.DC Round 11 documentation closure (decided 2026-11-06; `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `aqua-layout::measure`, `aqua-ui::layout`, `aqua-verify::policy`)

Decision for every item that was open in section 14 and 13.1:

- **Names.** `CosmicMeasurer` is `ShapedMeasurer` and `ProfiledCosmicMeasurer` is `ProfiledMeasurer` in `aqua-layout`, `aqua-ui` and `aqua-runtime`; no `Cosmic` identifier remains in the aqua repository or in the `editor-aqua` crate. The one remaining word `Cosmic` in the port is a comment about the GPUI baseline's font system in `editor-ui`, which is not part of the port's crate graph.
- **D3D11 default.** Sections 4, 6, 11 and 14.4 name `aqua-gpu-d3d11` as the default and `aqua-gpu-d3d12` as stashed and selectable; the host seam is `crates/aqua/src/native/gpu.rs`; references to `native/d3d12.rs` in older records now name the seam. The D3D12-only memory and perf records stay as evidence and are marked as such.
- **Conformance count.** The suite has 167 cases (119 base plus 48 fuzz) and 11 contract checks; earlier counts in the records (133) are history.
- **Enforcement.** `ADAPTER_ROOT_ITEMS` declares `ledger` and `wake_trace`; the enforcement suite is 9 of 9.
- **Formatting.** `cargo fmt -p` was run for every crate and `cargo fmt -p CRATE -- --check` is clean in all 25.
- **AQUA_PROFILE span diagnostics in `aqua::render::lower`.** Kept. `span_trace` is only filled when `AQUA_PROFILE` is set (`profile::enabled`), allocates nothing otherwise, and is the only tool that explains why a span was rejected or unclean; it belongs to the frame profiler of 14.S. Removing it would be done together with the retained-emit work of 14.RT, which owns that code.
- **Pure-function tests in the port.** `sidebar::geometry::history_spacer` (closed and open fraction, interpolation, 12 px floor) and the 672 px prose measure (`ui::markdown::prose_measure`, code blocks uncapped) have tests in `tests/sidebar/mod.rs` and `tests/markdown.rs`.
- **Open decisions of the original section 14.** None remain: 14.1 to 14.R are resolved in the table, 14.A to 14.D are closed, 14.4 was decided by the user.

### 14.4 Other platforms and the D3D11 backend

DECIDED BY THE USER 2026-11-05: Aqua renders with Direct3D 11 by default on Windows; `aqua-gpu-d3d11` is the default backend, `aqua-gpu-d3d12` is stashed (kept, compiling, selectable with `AQUA_GPU=d3d12` or the `d3d12` feature, no further investment), `native/gpu.rs` is the one seam. Windows only; Vulkan, Metal and other shells are not built. The paragraph below is the pre-decision analysis that led to it and is kept as evidence. D3D11 is a backend question, not a platform question. Measured API floors (bare probe, RX 9070 XT, 1898x1125 two-buffer `FLIP_DISCARD` B8G8R8A8, round 8, 11.5): on the gated performance counter GPU dedicated memory is 61.2 MiB for D3D12 and 31.4 MiB for D3D11, because the D3D12 driver allocates about 32 MiB at device creation that `QueryVideoMemoryInfo` does not report (that API meter reads 28.9 MiB for both); private bytes floor 29.8 MiB on D3D11 against 88.9 MiB on D3D12 (about 16 MiB at device creation and about 40 MiB on the first submit and present; 47.7 to 62.8 MiB of write-combined regions in the Editor), device creation 44 against 186 ms, first present 112 against 256 ms. The Editor on the fixture is 64.7 to 66.2 MiB dedicated, 3.5 to 5 MiB above the D3D12 floor (per-allocation ledger, 11.5), against GPUI at 49.1 to 51.1: D3D12 cannot get below GPUI or below 50 MiB on this driver; D3D11 would tie it and would also cut private bytes and cold start. The cost in code, tiers, shaders and oracle of a D3D11 adapter is tabulated in 11.5. The port stays graph-level, so another backend would be an adapter.

### 14.5 `aqua-kernels` scope (decided 2026-11-04)

the crate has no dependencies, denies `unsafe_op_in_unsafe_fn`, and every module that uses `unsafe` exports a safe `reference` twin plus a dispatching `apply`. Kernels: `color_matrix` (4x5 matrix over premultiplied f32 pixels, SSE2 on x86_64), `over` (premultiplied span composite with opacity, SSE2) and `diff` (max absolute byte difference for conformance compares and damage detection, SSE2). The fast paths are bit-identical to their twins (same operation order, no fused multiply-add), proven by differential tests in `crates/aqua-kernels/tests`, and other architectures fall back to the twin. The oracle (`aqua-gpu-ref`) calls only twins. `aqua-verify/tests/kernels.rs` enforces: no dependencies, the deny attribute, a twin and a differential test for every unsafe module, and twin-only use by the oracle. Hot paths are admitted when a measurement shows the twin costing frame time; none beyond these three is justified yet. **Profile evidence (2026-11-04, `aqua-verify/tests/profile.rs`, release, Windows x86_64):** the three SSE2 kernels do not earn their `unsafe` on the target: `over` over 3.69 million RGBA32F pixels takes 3.06 ms in the safe twin and 3.02 ms in the SSE2 path (Linux: 4.6 versus 3.3 ms), and `diff` over 14.7 MB takes 0.24 ms versus 0.28 ms (the twin auto-vectorizes). Runtime hot paths measured are all in safe code and mostly in external crates: 1900 glyph rasterizations (skrifa, 5 sizes x 95 glyphs x 4 subpixel offsets) cost 10.9 ms including a 4 ms first-face load (5.7 microseconds per glyph), and 2000 wrapped paragraphs shape cold in 37 ms and re-wrap in 18 ms. The only hot path found is the oracle's shadow integration: at 2560x1440, 7 shadows of sigma 16 take 50.7 s of CPU while 148 quads take 51 ms, 4001 glyphs 75 ms and a backdrop blur 60 ms, so shadows are 99.6% of an oracle frame (a numeric y-integral of two f64 `erf` calls per step, about 48 steps per pixel). Decision: no kernel is admitted for the runtime; the rule stays as written (a kernel needs a measured speedup of at least 1.5x over its twin on the Windows target), `color_matrix`, `over` and `diff` stay only because they exist and are twin-tested, and the first fix for the oracle's cost is algorithmic and safe (hoist the per-row sample weights and half-widths out of the per-pixel loop, bound the footprint), with a batched `erf`-sum kernel admitted only if that is still above 1 s per 1440p shadow frame.

### 14.6 D3D12 device core decisions (2026-11-04, `aqua-gpu-d3d12::hal`)

the Bindless tier requires Shader Model 6.6 and Resource Binding Tier 3; enhanced barriers follow `D3D12_OPTIONS12`; fence barriers and batched copy lists stay off (decision 14.A). A device that fails the baseline (feature level 11_0, SM 6.0) is skipped and the chain ends at WARP. After a device loss the same adapter is re-selected by LUID; a second loss within 10 s falls back to WARP. Recovery consumes the old core so the removed device is released before the replacement is created, which WARP requires. The first `Present` on the RX 9070 XT adds about 40 MB of driver-private commit (WARP: 0.1 MB), so the section 11 budget has to be measured with the real driver in place.

### 14.7 D3D12 scene pipelines and shaders (2026-11-04, `aqua-shaders`, `aqua-gpu-d3d12::scene`)

HLSL under `crates/aqua-shaders/hlsl` is compiled by `build.rs` to signed DXIL, Shader Model 6.0, with `dxc` found through `AQUA_DXC`, `PATH` or the Windows SDK (`dxc.exe` runs from WSL through `wslpath`); the build fails without it, and the adapter never compiles shaders at run time. The plan carries barriers, load ops and aliasing; the executor translates them against a per-resource tracked state (the plan's `from` and `Undefined` are ignored, so an aliased transient is moved from wherever it last was), emits an aliasing barrier at every transient's first use in a frame, and re-places the planner's heap offsets with real allocation sizes (`placement::place`) because D3D12 tile padding makes RGBA16F textures larger than `texels * 8`. Offscreen surfaces default to RGBA16F; BGRA8 UNORM and sRGB targets are supported by passing the render-target view format in `SceneTarget`, and a `Copy` pass whose source and destination formats differ (backdrop snapshots from an 8-bit window surface) is executed as a draw instead of `CopyTextureRegion`. Pipelines are keyed by (kind, target format), prebuilt through `warm`, and loaded from a serialized `ID3D12PipelineLibrary` passed in as bytes (a corrupt or foreign library is ignored); `SceneRenderer::serialize` produces the bytes, but the host stored and supplied by the host since round 3 (decision 14.C). At the time of writing the adapter did not cover `ColorMatrix`, `Custom` and `Compute`, the Bindless and enhanced-barrier tiers, or rounded clips and `Replace` blending; all are built since (records 14.12, 14.13, 14.16).

### 14.8 D3D12 window backend (2026-11-04, `aqua-gpu-d3d12::window`)

`WindowBackend` owns a `hal::Core`, one `Swapchain` per `SurfaceTarget::Native`, one `SceneRenderer` and one `PersistentTextures`, and implements `GpuBackend` plus `GpuReadback` (reads the unpresented back buffer). Frame order: `wait_for_frame_slot` (the waitable), `execute` (`Core::begin_frame`, upload batches, scene record, `end_frame` signals the timeline), `present` or `present_with_damage`. Swapchains are `FLIP_SEQUENTIAL`, not `FLIP_DISCARD`: DXGI returns `DXGI_ERROR_INVALID_CALL` from `Present1` for any non-empty dirty rect on a `FLIP_DISCARD` chain (measured on the RX 9070 XT and WARP), and sequential flip keeps the buffer contents the age tracking relies on. Damage: `window::damage::DamageTracker` keeps the last-presented frame per buffer and a short history; `repaint_region` returns what a buffer needs repainted (its age worth of damage plus this frame's), `Damage::Full` for unknown buffers, after resize, after an occluded present or when history ran out. An empty damage slice means full. The executor still redraws the whole surface every frame, so today the tracker feeds only `Present1` dirty rects (this frame's damage) and the `repaint_region` query; scissoring the plan to the repaint region was added in round 3 (below). Recovery (`recover`): wait idle, drop swapchains, renderer and textures, `Core::recover` (or a fresh WARP core if an earlier recovery failed halfway), rebuild renderer (reusing the serialized pipeline library when the old device still serializes it), recreate every swapchain on its HWND, drop pending uploads, then call the recovery listener (`RecoveryNotice { generation, software }`); the host must call `Residency::clear` there because persistent textures are not mirrored. Failed calls probe `Core::check_removed` and latch `device_status() == Lost`; the host reacts by calling `recover`. `FrameReport::gpu_time` is filled from `SceneRenderer::gpu_time()` (`window/backend.rs`). Scissored repaint and composition surfaces were added in round 3 (below).

Round 3 (2026-11-04): (1) Scissored repaint: `WindowBackend::execute_damaged(plan, inputs, damage)` computes `repaint_region` for the current back buffer, collapses it to its bounding rectangle (D3D12 has one scissor per viewport), and `SceneRenderer::record_region` confines every pass that writes the surface, clears included, to it; layer transients stay unscissored. The frame's own damage is remembered, and `present` commits it and sends it as `Present1` dirty rects. Unknown buffers, resize, occlusion and exhausted history repaint in full, and the first present after any reset is sent without dirty rects (DXGI returns `INVALID_CALL` for dirty rects on a first present). On the RX 9070 XT and WARP, pixels outside the region keep the previous frame, pixels inside match `aqua-gpu-ref`, and every scissored frame matches the oracle. (2) The swapchain render target view is `B8G8R8A8_UNORM_SRGB` over the `B8G8R8A8_UNORM` buffer (`hal::RENDER_FORMAT`). Before this the window path wrote linear values into a UNORM view and showed dark colors (`#3b82f6` came out as 11,57,235). Readback converts back to linear for the oracle compare. (3) DirectComposition: `create_composition_surface` makes a `CreateSwapChainForComposition` chain with `DXGI_ALPHA_MODE_PREMULTIPLIED`, and `composition_content` returns the `IUnknown` for `Window::attach_composition_surface`. `recover` rebuilds composition surfaces, and the host must attach `composition_content` again after a recovery notice. Pixel-checked through `PrintWindow` on an `alpha_surface` window: Mica, Mica Alt (`Tabbed`) and Acrylic show the backdrop through alpha 0 pixels (32,32,32, 32,32,32 and 84,84,84), opaque quads are exact, and an opaque clear hides the material. Known limit: the sRGB view encodes premultiplied linear color, so a translucent pixel over the material composes as `enc(c*a) + (1-a)*backdrop` instead of the gamma-space `a*enc(c) + (1-a)*backdrop`. Fully opaque and fully transparent pixels are exact. A final un-premultiply, encode and premultiply pass for composition surfaces would fix it and is not built. (4) Shared device: one `WindowBackend` owns one `Core`, one queue, one renderer and one texture set for any number of windows, tested with two windows of different size, resize and close of one, and loss and recovery of both. The backend is not `Send`, so the host must drive all windows from one render thread to share it. (5) Refresh pacing: `window::timing` reads the DWM composition period, `EnumDisplaySettings` gives the window monitor's rate, and `pacing::reconcile_interval` trusts the DWM period only when it agrees with the monitor within 1%. On this machine the test window sits on a 165 Hz monitor while the DWM composition clock is 240 Hz (4.167 ms). Vsync presents average 6.06 ms (the monitor interval) but land on 4.17 ms composition ticks, so frame gaps alternate between about 4.2 and 8.4 ms, which the swapchain cannot change. `WindowBackend::pacing` reports frames, missed vblanks, mean and worst. (6) Texture recovery: persistent textures are not mirrored by default. The host calls `Residency::clear` and re-uploads (`RecoveryNotice::textures == HostMustReupload`), and a draw of a texture that was not re-uploaded fails. `WindowOptions::mirror_textures` (env `AQUA_MIRROR_TEXTURES=1`) keeps a CPU copy of every persistent texture and replays it on the new device (`Replayed { textures }`). It doubles atlas memory, so it is off. (7) Adapters: the integrated `AMD Radeon(TM) Graphics` (485 MiB) and the RX 9070 XT both open and present to a window on the 9070 XT's monitor (the integrated one across adapters), matching the oracle within 2/255. A failed record now closes the command list and keeps queued uploads.


### 14.9 Runtime as a `Machine` (decided 2026-11-04, `aqua-runtime::Runtime`)

`Runtime` implements `aqua_protocol::Machine` over `RuntimeInput`/`RuntimeOutput`; the protocol types are `aqua_protocol::Input`/`Output`, re-exported as `RuntimeInput`/`RuntimeOutput`. A step is infallible: build errors go to `build_error()` and `take_diagnostics()`. The runtime binds the first window id it sees and addresses every `ShellCommand` to it; `open_window`, `submit` and `request_dialog` queue commands that the next step emits. Per step it emits: `RequestFrame` for input, resize, due wakes and recovery; `SetCursor` and `SetDragRegions` on change; `Viewport` then `Scene(SceneDiff)` after a frame (first frame is the full scene plus order, idle frames emit nothing); IME `Enable`, `Update` and `Disable` from the focused editor's revision, selection, composition and caret area (document windowed by `aqua_ui::events::ime_surroundings`); `ime_area` on change; and `access` as a full `AccessTreeUpdate`, only while an assistive client is attached (`Input::AccessClient { connected }`, an input so replay logs stay complete) and only when the tree epoch, focus or scale changed. Clipboard shortcuts become `ClipboardWrite` and `ClipboardRead { request }`, and the `ShellEvent::Clipboard` answer pastes text; image and file clipboard payloads are not carried by the shell protocol yet. File drag and drop follows `FileDropEvent` with the last pointer position. A `Tick` at or after the last `wake_at` requests an `ANIMATION` frame. `Recorder` plus `replay` and `first_divergence` reproduce every output byte for byte (`crates/aqua-runtime/tests/runtime.rs`).

### 14.10 Bounded layers and per-pass origin (2026-11-04, `aqua-graph::paint`)

a layer's transient is sized to the pixel-aligned union of its children clipped to the clip and target (a 1440p group of 200x40 px costs 64 KB instead of 29 MB). The origin lives in the planner, not in the pass: children are emitted in layer-local coordinates (bounds, clips, gradient endpoints and shadow footprints are translated by the layer origin), so passes, backends and the reference need no origin field and stay source compatible. The composite is an `Images` instance whose `bounds` sit at the layer origin in the parent's coordinates and whose `texels` are the whole layer, which samples exactly at texel centers. Rounded clips use the same mechanism with `clip` set to the exact fractional rect and `clip_radius` to the corner radius, so the rounded edge is analytic and independent of pixel alignment.

### 14.11 Custom pass contract and blur continuity (2026-11-04)

a `Custom` pass is a full-target pixel shader. HLSL entry signature: `float4 entry(CustomInput input) : SV_Target` with `struct CustomInput { float4 position : SV_Position; float2 uv : TEXCOORD0; }`, inputs `Texture2D<float4> input0..input7` at `t0..t7` in `reads` order, parameters `cbuffer Parameters : register(b0) { float4 parameters[N]; }` packed from `CustomPassSpec::params`, one `Load` per pixel addressed by `position.xy`; `bounds` is in the output's pixel space. The adapter compiles and caches it by `name`; until it does, it reports `custom_passes` false. Blur is continuous in the radius: below 2 the pyramid is one level with `offset = radius / 2`; above, the lower depth `floor(log2 radius)` is used alone until 75% of the octave, then crossfaded into the next depth (`BlurDepth::Mixed`, two pyramids lerped by two `Replace` image instances) so that at `radius = 2^k` the output equals the single pyramid of depth `k` with offset 1 from both sides. The crossfade costs a second pyramid only in the top quarter of each octave.

### 14.12 Adapter obligations from the graph IR (2026-11-04)

`ImageInstance::blend` and `clip_radius`, the explicit clear pass and `Features::color_matrix` are contract items. `aqua-gpu-d3d12` implements `Replace` blending and the rounded clip coverage in the image shader and `ColorMatrix`, `Custom` and `Compute` (record 14.16), and reports all three feature flags true. Closed 2026-11-04 (round 4) with evidence re-run on Windows: conformance 3 of 3 (130 passing checks on WARP, no skips), differential 5 of 5, tiers 6 of 6, and the rounded clip, backdrop replace and blur-sweep cases are in the suite (section 12.2). Earlier text recorded these cases failing on every tier (15 to 18 cases, 72 of 480 differential runs); that is superseded.

### 14.13 D3D12 tiers, transients and timing (2026-11-04, `aqua-gpu-d3d12::scene`, `aqua-shaders`)

`SceneOptions { limit: TierLimit, transient_ceiling, timestamps }` selects tiers; the default is the best tier the device offers (`hal::detect` then `scene::tiers::resolve`). **Bindless:** every pixel shader is also compiled as `<entry>_bl` (Shader Model 6.6, `-D BINDLESS`) with the texture read through `ResourceDescriptorHeap[texture_index]`; the index travels in root constant dword 14 and the root signature is the 16 constants plus the static sampler with `CBV_SRV_UAV_HEAP_DIRECTLY_INDEXED`, so passes and image runs set one constant instead of a descriptor table. `SetDescriptorHeaps` must run before `SetGraphicsRootSignature` for such a signature (the RX 9070 XT returns garbage otherwise, WARP does not care). **Enhanced barriers:** transients are created with `CreatePlacedResource2` and an initial layout; surface and transient transitions become texture barriers derived from the tracked legacy state (`barriers::access_of`), a transient's first use is an activation barrier (`LayoutBefore` undefined, discard) instead of an aliasing barrier, and persistent atlas pages keep legacy barriers because the upload path owns their state. **Fence barriers: not implemented.** Neither `windows` 0.62 nor the 10.0.26100 SDK headers expose them, so the tier is reported off and nothing in the adapter depends on it; revisit when the bindings ship. **Transient heap:** `policy::HeapGate` sizes the heap to the plan's re-placed total plus 25% growth slack, keeps it while the plan needs more than half of it, shrinks it after 240 frames below half, and rejects a plan above `transient_ceiling` with `Error::Budget` instead of allocating; `SceneRenderer::trim(&Trim, fence)` releases the heap behind the frame fence and shrinks upload and descriptor slots back to their initial size (`hal::budget::trim_for` decides when). **Timestamps:** two `EndQuery` stamps bracket `SceneRenderer::record` per slot, resolved into a readback buffer and read in `retire` once the slot fence passed; `gpu_time()` is the latest finished frame (one to three frames old), taken from a throwaway direct queue's `GetTimestampFrequency`. The window backend must copy `renderer.gpu_time()` into `FrameReport`. Verified with `tests/tiers.rs` (every tier combination against the Baseline tier's results on WARP, the RX 9070 XT and the minimum-power adapter, plus BGRA8 on the top tier), `tests/transients.rs` and `tests/heap.rs` (exact allocation counts per steady frame: 23, 11 and 14 for the group, glyph and image cases).

### 14.16 Effects, validation and the zero-allocation gate (2026-11-04, `aqua-gpu-d3d12::scene`, `aqua-shaders`)

**ColorMatrix:** `ps_color_matrix` (`color_matrix.hlsl`, also built as `_bl`) unpremultiplies, applies the 4x5 row-major matrix to straight color, saturates each channel and premultiplies, matching `aqua_kernels::color_matrix::reference`. The root signature grew from 16 to 36 root dwords: dwords 0 to 15 are the frame constants, dwords 16 to 35 the matrix (`matrix_rows[5]` in `common.hlsli`), written by `Binding::set_matrix` only for that pass. **Custom:** the pixel shader is compiled at run time with `D3DCompile` as Shader Model 5.1 (`ps_5_1`; the `windows` bindings expose no DXC), cached by a hash of name, entry and source plus the target format, with a run-time compiled `vs_5_1` fullscreen vertex shader so no DXIL and DXBC stages are mixed. It uses its own root signature: a root CBV at `b0` fed from the slot upload buffer (parameters padded to a 256 byte block) and a table of eight SRVs at `t0..t7`, padded with null descriptors; the scissor is `bounds`, and a compile error is returned as `Error::Shader` (the half-recorded command list is closed, so the backend keeps working). **Compute:** `ShaderId::Custom(id)` selects a registered shader; id 1, the row box blur of the reference, is a build-time DXIL `cs_row_box_blur`, others come from `register_compute` (`cs_5_1`, entry chosen by the caller). The pass has a root signature of four constants at `b0`, eight SRVs and one UAV; its output must be a transient, which is then created with `ALLOW_UNORDERED_ACCESS` (only transients written by a compute pass pay for it). Reads of transients and the surface now use `PIXEL | NON_PIXEL_SHADER_RESOURCE` (persistent atlas pages keep the pixel-only state), and a second compute write to a texture already in the UAV state gets a UAV barrier. **Verified on Windows:** all 6 conformance variants (float, BGRA8, sRGB on WARP and the RX 9070 XT) pass, including `color_matrix_*` (max difference 1), `custom_invert_*` (1) and `compute_row_box_blur` (1); `tests/effects.rs` covers packed parameters, compile errors and `register_compute`.

**Debug layer:** `AQUA_D3D12_DEBUG=1` enables the layer (and `AQUA_D3D12_GBV=1` GPU-based validation) in `scene::device::select`; `OffscreenBackend::debug_errors` drains the info queue. `tests/debug_layer.rs` renders every conformance case twice on all four tier combinations (Baseline, Bindless, enhanced barriers, both) on WARP and the RX 9070 XT (float and BGRA8) with GPU-based validation and asserts zero error or corruption messages. It found and fixed two legacy-barrier errors: a placed render target must be initialized with `DiscardResource` after its aliasing barrier, and the discard needs the resource in `RENDER_TARGET`, so activation now emits the aliasing barrier, a transition to `RENDER_TARGET` from the tracked state and the discard before any other barrier of the pass (`Batch::pre`). The enhanced-barrier and bindless paths were clean.

**Zero allocations:** a steady frame (same plan as the previous one) performs no heap allocation in `GpuBackend::execute` on the offscreen backend: packing goes straight into a reused byte buffer (`Scratch`), the tracked-state map, the activated set and the per-pass read bounds are reused, image runs are an iterator, barrier batches are pooled in `Emitter`, and `Validated` skips `validate` when the plan equals the one it last accepted (a changed plan validates and clones with `clone_from`). `tests/heap.rs` gates 11 cases, covering group, glyph, image, blur, rounded clip, color matrix, custom and compute, at exactly 0 allocations and 0 bytes on both tier extremes of WARP and the RX 9070 XT; `AQUA_HEAP_TRACE=1` prints the allocating call sites. Frames whose plan changes still allocate (validation and the plan clone).

### 14.17 Debug-layer flake, non-allocating validate, parallel-safe tests (2026-11-04, round 4)

**Root cause of the flaky `debug_layer` errors.** GPU-based validation reported `Incompatible texture barrier layout ... Layout RENDER_TARGET` on a shader-resource read of a transient, only in `backdrop_blur_*` and `rounded_clip_nested` cases, on WARP and hardware, on every tier (legacy and enhanced barriers), in 5 to 35 percent of runs. A stress loop of the 22 backdrop cases (400 rounds, 8800 case runs) reproduced it 27 to 33 times per run; the same case repeated 400 times produced 0, and the errors occurred only in the first frame after `Transients::ensure` created new placed resources, never in the second frame. Freeing the transient heap before every case raised the count to 49, so reuse of old memory is not the trigger. Creating the resources, then submitting a signal and waiting for it before recording (the debug layer's resource initialization settles on the queue) reduced it to 0 in 400 rounds on both tiers. The barrier sequence in the recorded plan was verified correct (every read follows a transition to the read state, and the same plan is clean in the second frame), so the defect is the debug layer's asynchronous seeding of the layout of freshly created placed resources racing with the first command list that uses them. Fix: `SceneRenderer::reserve` creates the transients, and when the debug layer is requested (`AQUA_D3D12_DEBUG=1`) and resources were created the backend waits for the queue before recording (`OffscreenBackend::run_frame`, `WindowBackend::execute_region`). Normal runs never wait. Transients also carry debug names (`transient <id> offset <n>`) when the layer is on. The three `debug_layer` tests now share a mutex because three devices validating at once still produced a 1 in 15 failure with the same message; 20 of 20 runs pass with the default thread count. Hardware evidence: RX 9070 XT and WARP.

**`aqua_graph::validate` allocates nothing.** The `HashMap`s and `Vec`s are gone: lifetimes are computed by scans (cached in a 64-entry stack array), resource states by scanning barriers backwards, and the image binding check by containment in both directions. `tests/validate_allocation.rs` asserts 0 allocations for a plan with 80 layers. `Validated` (a cached `FramePlan` clone that allocated whenever the plan changed) is deleted; the renderer validates every frame.

**Parallel-safe tests.** Concurrent first executions on several WARP devices crash the process with an access violation (exit code 5; reproduced 15 of 15 with three WARP devices rendering at once, 0 of 8 when starts are staggered by 100 ms, 0 of 8 with three hardware devices). The adapter cannot work around a WARP defect, so the tests serialize: `tests/support` provides `software()`, held by every test that can create a WARP device (`conformance`, `effects`, `pipeline_cache`, `sprites`, `tiers`, `transients`). `tests/hal/device.rs` uses a reader-writer lock: device removal, memory, footprint, ring and eviction tests are exclusive (a removal injected into one device suspends the others in the process), the rest are shared and take the software mutex. Every test binary of `aqua-gpu-d3d12` except `heap` passes 3 of 3 runs at the default thread count.

**Parallel-safe tests outside the backend crate.** The same access violation was captured with a vectored exception handler in `d3d12_differential`: the faulting frame is inside `d3d10warp.dll`, called from `ID3D12CommandQueue::ExecuteCommandLists` of one WARP device while another WARP device runs (1 of 4 default-thread debug runs at 100 scenes, 0 of 8 once serialized). `aqua-verify/tests/support/exclusive.rs` holds a process-wide mutex for the thread of every test that creates a WARP device (`d3d12_differential`, `host_motion`). The memory gates (`memory_budget`, `window_memory_budget`, `host_window`) hold the same mutex for their whole body, because they read process-wide private bytes and the framework heap: two gates running at once measured each other (hardware +153 MiB and WARP +10 MiB of steady growth that vanish when run alone), and all three pass on a release build when serialized. WARP alone still shows 0 to 9 MiB of private growth between runs of `host_window` with a flat framework heap (+0.05 MiB) and no pattern over a 600-input warm-up, so the software run allows 12 MiB while the hardware run keeps 1 MiB. Tests that capture the desktop (`materials`, `host_material`, `host_swapchain`) and the system clipboard unit test in `aqua` return with an explicit `SKIP-SESSION-LOCKED` marker when the user session is locked (`aqua-verify/tests/support/desktop.rs`), because a locked session reads black from the screen and denies `OpenClipboard`. The four file-drag tests of `aqua-shell-win32/tests/windows.rs` hold one mutex: calling the adapter's `IDropTarget::DragEnter` on several threads at once reaches the shell drag helper (`IDropTargetHelper::DragEnter`) concurrently and it faulted inside `shell32.dll` in 2 of 40 default-thread runs (captured with a vectored handler), 0 of 100 once serialized; a real session has one drag at a time. The shared `aqua/tests/support` WARP painters take the same exclusive mutex as `host_motion`. Window tests of `aqua-gpu-d3d12` and `aqua-verify` no longer skip when an adapter is missing or a second adapter is absent: they fail.

**Layer memory.** Layer and opacity transients were already sized to bounds by the planner (record 14.10); the editor workload at 2560x1440 holds a 3.3 MiB transient heap (WARP 2.8 MiB) and 31.6 MiB of video memory in total, of which 29.5 MiB is the two swapchain-sized surfaces. The remaining excess in the windowed `collections` run is driver cost, not transients. The 10 allocations per steady frame of `heap` for `custom_invert_half_amount_fractional_bounds` were not per frame: a symbolized backtrace (`AQUA_HEAP_TRACE=1`, with `WSLENV=AQUA_HEAP_TRACE` so the variable reaches the Windows executable) showed one frame in the 16 measured entering `Transients::ensure` and recreating the transients. The backend had been reused across the 14 cases, the previous cases needed a larger transient heap, and `HeapGate` counts idle frames across cases, so the shrink after `SHRINK_AFTER_FRAMES` (240) fell inside the measured window. The shrink is the designed behaviour; the test now settles each case for `SHRINK_AFTER_FRAMES + 8` frames, so the shrink has happened before measuring, and `ensure` no longer clones the key list when it resizes. The gate is 0 allocations on all 14 cases at the default thread count (4 adapter and tier combinations).

**Trim hook and memory breakdown.** `WindowBackend::trim(Trim)`, `set_visibility(Visibility)` and `service_budget()` apply `hal::budget::trim_for` to the whole backend: the core upload ring is shrunk (`Core::trim`) and the scene renderer waits for the queue, retires and releases its transient heap, and re-sizes its per-slot upload buffers and descriptor heaps on their next use. `memory_breakdown()` reports the swapchain buffers, transient heap, retired heaps, frame and scene upload buffers, atlas residency and staging, and the adapter's local usage and budget; `unattributed()` is what the driver holds. `tests/window/memory.rs` gates the breakdown, the trim (heap freed, regrown on the next frame, hidden window trims) and a 2544x1133 window on WARP and the RX 9070 XT: swapchain 22.0 MiB, first present +1.3 MiB (transients 0.9), 120 steady frames +0.6 MiB; the unattributed 14.6 MiB of local usage is the driver's own, present before any surface exists (14.9 MiB at device creation). Window tests that create a device take the software mutex (`support::hold_software`) for the length of the test, because recovery tests fall back to WARP and injected removal suspends other devices.

### 14.U Round 6 closure: shadow hard edge, repaint feedback, wake from signals, port lint set (decided 2026-11-04)

- **Shadow hard-edge threshold.** A shadow whose Gaussian sigma is below 0.05 logical pixels is a hard signed-distance edge, in both the oracle (`aqua-gpu-ref::shadow::HARD_EDGE_SIGMA`) and the D3D12 shader (`shadow.hlsl`, `HARD_EDGE_SIGMA`); at or above it the Gaussian-weighted integral of section 12.1 applies. Evidence: the round 4 differential failed on fuzz scene `fuzz_d3d12d1f_234` (sigma 0.008, negative spread) by 31 in the soft band against a limit of 24 on every adapter, tier and planner variant, so the defect was in the shadow op itself and not in one adapter; the D3D12 shader was fixed in round 5 and both implementations now branch on the same 0.05 constant. Re-run in round 6: `d3d12_differential` 9 of 9 at 400 scenes (the corpus includes scene 234) on WARP, the discrete adapter and the integrated adapter, one test thread, 172 s. The threshold is a constant of the contract, not a tolerance: below 0.05 px the blur radius is under 1/20 of a pixel, no sample can see a gradient, and an integral over one-pixel taps only adds noise.
- **Repaint frames report `Presented`.** A forced frame (an arriving image or glyph, a pending-content poll, a snapshot transition) re-presents the last frame id and returns its `Presented` feedback; trim frames and animation-only repaints (pulse, shimmer) stay silent (14.N). Before, a screen that animated only through repaints (the snapshot sweep) produced no `on_frame_completed` at all. `host_swapchain` counts 99 reported frames over a 600 ms sweep in a real window; `render_service` asserts the counts.
- **Signals set outside input events request a frame.** The wake callback bound by `AppCtx::bind_reactive` posts `ReactiveWake`, `pump_inbox` flushes the runtime and requests a frame with `FrameReason::INPUT` when any effect ran; this is independent of whether an input event is on the stack. Tested without a window (`runtime::tests::a_signal_set_outside_an_input_event_requests_a_frame`: after the scheduler drains, a signal write followed by `pump_inbox` leaves a pending frame) and in a real window (`host_swapchain` sets the `rows` and `ground` signals from a timer wake; the first presence sample appears within one tick).
- **Port lint set.** The Editor port's crate no longer inherits the GPUI workspace lints (`clippy::pedantic` and `nursery` denied plus a restriction list, 2588 errors on the ported code). `crates/editor-aqua/Cargo.toml` sets `[lints.clippy]` to `all` denied, plus `panic`, `todo`, `unimplemented`, `dbg_macro`, `allow_attributes_without_reason`, `undocumented_unsafe_blocks`, `str_to_string`, `missing_assert_message` and `unwrap_in_result` denied, with `collapsible_if`, `collapsible_match`, `too_many_arguments`, `type_complexity`, `useless_conversion`, `needless_borrow`, `needless_borrows_for_generic_args`, `large_enum_variant`, `should_implement_trait`, `manual_clamp`, `manual_flatten`, `unnecessary_fold`, `unnecessary_unwrap`, `unnecessary_to_owned`, `unnecessary_result_map_or_else`, `let_and_return`, `derivable_impls`, `clone_on_copy`, `arc_with_non_send_sync` and `rc_buffer` allowed (the code is GPUI-shaped and the UI is to be redesigned, so reshaping signatures to satisfy style lints would be thrown away). `unwrap_used` and `expect_used` are not denied for the port. `cargo clippy -p editor-aqua --no-deps` for `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` exits clean; the two `#[expect(clippy::too_many_lines)]` attributes that the new set left unfulfilled were removed.
- **Facade rule.** Recorded as loosened in section 4 (Enforcement).
- **Application API.** `ShellApplication` (Editor port) delegates `color_profile_for`, `cursor_for` and `reactive_runtime_for` to its `UiApp`, next to the `a11y_nodes_for` it already had, so a second window of the port gets its own profile, cursor and reactive runtime; `UiApp` and `ManagedUiApp` already did.
- **Showcase.** The filter button now carries a `Filter: <choice>` text item (the label existed only in the accessibility tree, so nothing was drawn) and the example idles: it returned a 16 ms deadline forever because its initial pulse handle was never settled; it now asks for frames only while a toggled row's pulse runs. Run on Windows with `AQUA_SMOKE_MS=6000`: 4 wakeups and 2 presented frames in 6 s.
- **Windows clippy.** `tools/win-clippy.sh` resolves its own location through `readlink -f`, so the `aqua-win-clippy` symlink in the tools directory works (it changed into the tools directory and found no `Cargo.toml`); `aqua-win-clippy -p aqua-layout --tests` exits 0. The `MeasureCache::len` dead-code warning is gone (the length helper lives in the test module that uses it).

### 14.14 Text blending on a linear pipeline (decided 2026-11-04, `aqua-graph::tone`, `aqua-shaders/hlsl/glyph.hlsl`, `aqua-text::residency`)

the glyph pass applies `ToneCurve::native().linear_blend_alpha(coverage, luminance)` to every mask sample, where `luminance` is the sRGB-encoded luminance of the instance color (unpremultiplied first) and the curve parameters travel in the pass constants (`region` = the four ratios, `values.x` = contrast), so the curve is a data input and not recompiled. Mask instances from vector paths pass through the same function. The remap assumes the backdrop is the opposite extreme of the text; against a mid-tone backdrop the result is between the plain linear blend and DirectWrite's (gray 200 on 30 measures 0.88 to 0.96 of DirectWrite's ink). Alternative rejected: blending glyphs in encoded space, which needs a backdrop read per glyph. One glyph identity everywhere: `GlyphKey.font` is an interned face index owned by the `GlyphResolver`, sizes are quantized to 1/64 px before they enter any key, and `aqua-text` keeps no atlas.

### 14.O Slug curves, backdrop-aware glyph blending and instance transforms (decided 2026-11-04, round 4; `aqua-graph::slug`, `aqua-graph::linear`, `aqua-text::slug`, `aqua-shaders`, `aqua-gpu-ref::sprites`, `aqua-gpu-d3d12`, `aqua::render::lower`)

Decisions, each with the alternative that was rejected:

- **One float store, not two buffers.** Curves, band headers, band ranges and curve-index lists share one persistent `Rgba32Float` texture of fixed width 1024 texels (`TextureHandle(u32::MAX - 1)` for the host, `TextureHandle(3)` in the conformance assets), addressed linearly (`index % 1024`, `index / 1024`) and read with `Load`. A glyph is a record: 4 header texels (em bounds; horizontal origin, bands per em and the two band counts; vertical origin, bands per em and the two range-table offsets; the index-list offset), the horizontal then vertical range texels (`offset, count`), 2 texels per quadratic curve (`p1.xy p2.xy`, `p3.xy`) and the curve-index lists packed four to a texel. The two band sets are ordered by descending far edge so a scan stops at the first curve behind the sample, exactly as in the CPU reference. Rejected: separate curve and band textures (a second SRV per pass changes the root signature and the bindless constants for every pass) and `Rgba16Float` curves (11 bits of mantissa give 0.1 px error at 200 px). The layout, the instance type, the pure evaluator (`slug::coverage`) and the outline-to-record packer (`pack_glyph`, with `SlugGlyph`, `PathCommand`, the cubic-to-quadratic split and the band builder) live in `aqua-graph::slug`, so the oracle and the conformance assets need no font stack; `aqua-text` re-exports them and adds `SlugCache` (outlines from skrifa) and `SlugStore` (the arena: append-only, grows by recreating the texture at a power-of-two row count, emits `Release`/`Create`/row `Write` uploads, `reset` after a font-set change, device loss or a full trim). A device that loses textures needs no extra step: `reset` makes the next `uploads` recreate the store.
- **`PassKind::Slug { store, instances }`.** `SlugInstance` carries the screen origin of the em origin, a 2x2 em-to-screen matrix (pixels per em and the y flip folded in with the placement), the glyph record index, the em bounds, color, clip, opacity and optional backdrop. The pass reads exactly the store texture. The planner culls, merges (same store) and sizes layers by the instance's covered rectangle. The shader (`slug.hlsl`) maps the pixel centre back to em space with the inverse matrix, uses the matrix determinant's square root as pixels per em, and runs the same two band scans as the oracle; the quad covers the transformed em bounds plus one pixel. Pixels per em is isotropic, so a strong anisotropic scale blurs one axis slightly.
- **Backdrop luminance is instance data.** `GlyphInstance` and `SlugInstance` have `backdrop: Option<f32>` (sRGB-encoded luminance, packed as a negative number for `None`). `Some(b)` selects `ToneCurve::linear_blend_alpha_over(coverage, luminance, b)`, `None` keeps the contrasting-backdrop assumption of 14.14; HLSL and the oracle use the same expression. Rejected: reading the render target per glyph (needs a copy of the target before every glyph pass and breaks batching). The host fills it in `render::lower`: it remembers the last 256 nearly opaque (alpha 0.98 or more) solid fills with their luminance, and a text run takes the most recent of the last 64 whose rectangle contains the run's first line midpoint, else the opaque clear colour, else `None`. Translucent fills, gradients and images never count, so text over them keeps the old assumption.
- **Instance-level rotation and skew.** `QuadInstance` and `ImageInstance` have `linear: Linear2` (identity by default), a 2x2 matrix applied about the centre of `bounds`; `bounds` stays the unrotated rectangle, so radii, border widths and gradients are in local units and the matrix scales them. The vertex stage covers the bounding box of the transformed rectangle plus one pixel (`QuadInstance::covered`, used by the planner, the culler and the conformance footprints). The pixel stage computes the local position `centre + inverse * (p - centre)`, takes distances there and multiplies them by the square root of |det| for anti-aliasing; clips stay in screen space, and an identity matrix takes the old code path bit for bit. `PaintCommand::Linear { linear, leaf }` wraps a `Quad`, `GradientQuad` or `Image` (anything else is `PlanError::NotTransformable`); `PaintCommand::Slug(SlugRun)` carries slug glyphs. Sprite vertex data grew from 4 to 5 vec4s (`SpriteGpu.extra` is the backdrop for glyphs and the matrix for images) and quads from 9 to 10 (`XFORM`). Shadows, clips and blurs do not rotate: the host maps them to the bounding box.
- **Host lowering.** An item drawn under a transform that is not a translation, uniform or non-uniform scale (`!Affine::is_exact`) is emitted in device space as before and then lifted: quads, gradient quads and images become `Linear` leaves with their centre moved through the transform; non-uniform corner radii and uneven or dashed borders (clip-based decompositions that cannot be rotated) are flattened to the largest radius and width and counted as approximated; shadows, clips, content blur and backdrop blur take the bounding box and are counted. `PushTransform` itself is no longer reported as approximated. Text is placed with the full matrix and uses Slug when the run sits under a transform that changes the scale or rotates or skews (`moving`), or when a glyph is at least 160 px; glyphs the store cannot hold (color glyphs, fake bold) fall back to the atlas path. `LowerReport.curves` counts slug glyphs.
