Verified on Windows (RX 9070 XT and WARP): the conformance suite had 133 cases at that point (167 today, 119 base plus 48 fuzz; 11 new: slug at three sizes, rotated and skewed slug, slug clipped in a group with opacity, slug and atlas glyphs over a mid-tone with a known backdrop, rotated and skewed quads and images, rotated gradient, rotated quad clipped in a group) and passes on all six surface variants, with the 11 new cases differing from the oracle by at most 1/255; `tiers` (baseline, bindless, enhanced barriers, both) and `debug_layer` (GPU-based validation) pass with the new cases; `aqua/tests/render_slug.rs` (scaled and rotated text through the host lowering on both devices: curve ink within 12% of atlas ink, 15% for rotated text, no ink in the old bounding-box corners) and `lowering::rotated_quads_are_rotated_exactly_and_not_reported_as_approximated` pass. On Linux the oracle's own tests (`aqua-gpu-ref/tests/transforms.rs`: quarter turn equals the swapped rectangle, shear and rotation preserve area, scale multiplies it, ring ink within 6% of its analytic area at several scales and placements, the glyph alpha equals `linear_blend_alpha_over`) and `aqua-text/tests/slug_store.rs` (packed buffers evaluate identically to the CPU glyph, upload sequence) pass. Round 5 gave the store an eviction policy (14.T). Not done: Slug for vector paths and icons, anisotropic pixels per em, and a host-level check of the backdrop estimate on a real screen (the Editor was not run in this round).

### 14.T Text stack closure (decided 2026-11-04, round 5; `aqua-text::slug`, `aqua-text::raster`, `aqua-text::svg`, `aqua-oracle-dwrite`)
- **Color glyph placement.** `aqua-oracle-dwrite::Oracle::color_layers` renders COLR layers through `IDWriteFactory4::TranslateColorGlyphRun` and glyph-run analysis at a fractional baseline origin. `tests/color_diff.rs` compares Segoe UI Emoji (3 emoji, 16, 24, 32 and 48 px, 4 horizontal phases, 48 placements) by alpha centroid: ink ratio 1.01 for every policy; mean centroid error 0.40 px (worst 0.87) when the bin-0 bitmap is placed at the floor of the quantized position (what round 4 did), 0.27 px (worst 0.71) when the same bitmap is placed at the nearest whole pixel, 0.08 px (worst 0.23) with a bitmap per horizontal bin. Decision: keep whole-pixel color glyphs (four bins would quadruple the RGBA atlas for 0.19 px) and place them at the nearest pixel: `GlyphPlacement::color_x` is `x + 1` for bins 2 and 3, and `GlyphResolver::lower` uses it. `aqua::render::lower` applies the same rule inline (`pixel_x + 1` when the subpixel bin is 2 or 3) for its `GlyphSlot::Color` branch, so both paths place color glyphs identically (checked in round 6). DirectWrite renders CBDT and sbix only through Direct2D, so this comparison covers COLR; bitmap strikes are compared by size and origin (below).
- **sbix.** DirectWrite reads the synthetic sbix font (`GetGlyphImageFormats` reports PNG; `GetGlyphImageData` returns the strike at the requested ppem with pixel size 16x16 and horizontal left origin (-1, 14)), and Aqua's rasterizer yields the same size with `left = 1`, `top = 14` for strike origin (1, -2) at ppem 16 and the equivalent at ppem 64 (origin (4, -8)); a 24 px request scales from the nearest strike to 24x24 with the quadrant colors in place, and a glyph without sbix data has no color image. This replaces the synthetic-PNG unit test as the sbix evidence; a real Apple font remains unavailable.
- **Slug store eviction.** `SlugStore<K>` (key `SlugKey` in production) is an arena of variable-size glyph records with a free list. The budget is `STORE_BUDGET_BYTES` = 4 MiB of texels (262,144 texels, 256 rows of the 1024-wide texture; about 350 to 700 glyphs), a share of the 9 MiB cache figure of section 11 that Slug text can never exceed in steady state. Each `lookup` stamps the entry with the current epoch; `uploads()` (called once per lowered frame) ends the epoch. When a new glyph does not fit under the budget, entries are evicted least recently used first, never one stamped with the current epoch (the frame being built still reads them), until a free block fits; if every entry is in use the store grows past the budget and counts an overrun (`overruns()`), and the next frame's lookups bring it back. Freed blocks coalesce and the tail is truncated; evicted glyphs are removed from the map so a later lookup repacks them. Uploads write only the rows that changed (dirty range); the texture is recreated when it must grow or when the live rows fall to a quarter of the allocation (`resident_bytes` follows). Indices handed out in a frame stay valid for that frame because eviction never moves a block. Tested in `tests/slug_store.rs` (LRU order, current-frame protection, overrun counting, row-only writes, shrinking).
- **SVG text.** See 14.E. Host-level evidence: `aqua/tests/images_svg_text.rs` decodes an SVG with a `<style>` sheet and an `em` size through `ImageAssets` with `set_fonts` on the decode worker, uploads it through `ManagedUiApp`, and paints it with the reference renderer and every hardware D3D12 adapter (red ink in the painted frame); without `set_fonts` the same document produces no ink. `aqua-text/tests/svg.rs` covers sheets, `em` and percent sizes, straight and curved `textPath`, `startOffset` and anchors on Windows; `svg_sheet.rs`, `svg_length.rs` and `svg_path.rs` run on Linux.

### 14.S Frame profiler and spike recorder (decided 2026-11-04, round 6; `aqua-protocol::probe`, `aqua::profile`)

Attribution of frame spikes needs per-phase times inside pure crates that may not read a clock. Decision: `aqua-protocol::probe` defines `Phase` (input, reactive, build, layout, text, lower, upload, plan, record, submit, present, wait), `Metric` (layouts, nodes measured, text runs shaped, scene ops emitted, upload bytes, passes, transient bytes, PSO compiled/loaded, atlas growth and the rest) and an install-once `fn(Event)` sink behind a `OnceLock`. Pure crates and adapters call `probe::span(Phase)` and `probe::count(Metric, n)`; with no sink installed each call is one failed atomic load. The host installs the sink only when `AQUA_PROFILE=1`. `aqua::profile` implements it: a per-thread recorder charges time and heap counters to the innermost open phase (exclusive times), `begin_frame`/`end_frame` close an app-thread frame in `Aqua::draw_ready` and a render-thread frame in `Slot::run`, a per-second summary line (`aqua-profile role=... work p50/p90/p99/max, frames over 4, 8.3 and 16.7 ms, allocations per frame, per-phase maxima`) is printed for each role, and every frame whose work (everything but `wait`) is at least `AQUA_PROFILE_SPIKE_MS` (default 4) prints an `aqua-spike` line with the phase breakdown, per-phase allocation count and bytes, the non-zero metrics, the rebuild reason (`FrameReason` bits) or render cause (arrived, forced, trim, repaint), the dominant phase and any unattributed wall time. `AQUA_PROFILE_OUT=path` appends to a file instead of stderr. Heap counters come from the `profile-alloc` cargo feature of `aqua` (a thread-local counting `GlobalAlloc`, off by default so release builds without it pay nothing; the Editor port exposes it as `profile-heap`). Not done: ETW (`wpr.exe`) capture, D3D12 internal split of `record` into command recording and queue submission, transient-heap reallocation as a counter.

### 14.15 Host composition root (2026-11-04, `crates/aqua`)

aqua is the only event loop. `native::looping::Host` implements the `aqua-shell-win32` `Handler` and drives `Aqua`, which steps the single-window `aqua_runtime::Runtime` Machine with time as an input (`Tick`, `Frame { window, presentation }`) and executes every `RuntimeOutput` in `apply_output` (shell, IME, access tree, render commands, `wake_at`, clipboard feedback). The `Application` trait remains as the compatibility layer for the Editor port, extended with `step_input`, `take_output`, `on_dialog`, `on_motion_event` and `motion_commands`. winit is gone from the runtime; an `Inbox` (mutex queue of `AquaUserEvent` plus the shell `Waker`) replaces the event-loop proxy. Window creation order: hidden shell window, UIA attach, `TsfIme::from_hwnd`, show, render link. One render service thread for all windows (record 14.N); the D3D12 `WindowBackend` is not `Send`, so `RenderLink` ships an `InitFactory` and the backend is built lazily on the service thread. Pacing: a slot waits on its waitable swapchain slot each time it runs and, when its previous iteration did not present, may not run again for one frame interval (`not_before`), so an idle or failing present cannot busy-loop and one window never sleeps for another. `Presented` and motion events return to the Machine as `GpuEvent::Presented` and `RenderEvent`. Lowering of the display-item scene to `PaintCommand`s lives in `crates/aqua/src/render/lower.rs` (rects, quads, shadows, clips, opacity, blur, scale and translate transforms, glyphs through `GlyphResolver` and `Residency`, retained images and vectors); images reach the render thread over an `ExternalUpdate` side channel. A full command ring drops a command, so `RenderLink` keeps a backlog and retries. Snapshot transitions and text raster mode are implemented in the lowering (10.6). Not supported and reported as `WindowError::Unsupported` or counted in `LowerReport.skipped`: rounded clip radius, inset shadows, dashed borders, general transforms, GPUI items. Not built: caption hover forwarding, image and file clipboard payloads. `FrameReport::gpu_time` is filled by the backend but the host does not yet surface it. The host depends on neither `aqua-platform` nor `aqua-render` (both are deleted; image decode lives in `aqua-media::decode`), and no manifest names `winit` or `wgpu`.

**14.E SVG text.** Decision (revised in round 4): `aqua-media` builds `resvg` with only `svgz` and `raster-images`, so `usvg` has no `text` feature and no `fontdb`, `rustybuzz` or second text stack enters the lockfile. SVG `<text>` is rendered through `aqua-text` instead: `aqua_text::svg::outline_text(bytes, &mut FontManager)` parses the document with `roxmltree`, lays out every top-level `<text>` (with `<tspan>` children) through `TextLayout` (shaping, bidi, per-glyph fallback), converts each glyph to an em-space outline (`slug::em_outline`, skrifa, unhinted, variable axes and synthetic italic applied) and splices a `<path>` in place of the element, so ancestor transforms, opacity and inherited paint still apply. It returns the input unchanged (`Cow::Borrowed`) when there is no `<text>` or the document does not parse, leaving the error to resvg. Supported: `x`, `y`, `dx`, `dy` (first value of each list), `font-family` (generic names and quoted names), `font-size` (px), `font-weight`, `font-style`, `letter-spacing`, `text-anchor` per absolute-positioned chunk, `fill`, `stroke`, `opacity` and their opacity variants per `<tspan>`, properties inherited from ancestors and from inline `style`. Round 5 added `<style>` sheets (type, class, id and universal selectors, compound and descendant selectors, comma lists, specificity then source order, inline `style` over sheets over presentation attributes, `@` rules skipped), `em`, `ex`, `rem`, `%`, `pt`, `pc`, `in`, `cm`, `mm` lengths and the keyword sizes for `font-size` (resolved down the ancestor chain), `em` for `x`, `y`, `dx`, `dy` and `letter-spacing`, and `<textPath>` (`href` or `xlink:href` to a `<path>`, `startOffset` as length or percent, `text-anchor`, `<tspan>` inside; glyphs sit on the path by their advance midpoint, rotated by the tangent of a flattened path with arcs, cubics and quadratics). Still not supported, by decision: per-character `rotate` and `textLength`, `writing-mode`, percent `x`/`y` (they need the viewport), a `transform` on the referenced path, `>` and attribute selectors, color glyphs (emoji outlines are skipped) and fake bold; unsupported constructs render without that effect and never fail the decode. The host runs it on the image decode worker (`ImageAssets::set_fonts`, set by `App`) under the shared font lock, and decoding without fonts renders no text. Enforced by `aqua-verify/tests/banned.rs`, which bans `wgpu`, `glyphon`, `cosmic-text`, `winit`, `accesskit_winit` and `fontdb` in every manifest, in `Cargo.lock` and in source. Verified on Windows (`aqua-text/tests/svg.rs`: Arial text becomes paths that resvg paints, start, middle and end anchors place the ink around the anchor, tspans keep their own fill).

14.Q **Host lowering and text path (decided 2026-11-04, `crates/aqua/src/render`, `aqua-transport`):** the host draws only through `aqua_transport::GpuRenderHost`; `render::host::GpuFrames` is a thin wrapper that adopts the caller's surface and keeps `RenderStats` for the frame-stats line. `aqua_transport::Lowering` now takes a `LowerContext { size, scale, now }` and has hooks: `sync` (drains the `ExternalUpdate` channel on the render thread), `device_recovered`, `trim`; the host feeds `FrameReport::gpu_time` into `FrameRecord::gpu_micros` and into `TrimPolicy` (`aqua_transport::trim`, a pure state machine: every 300 frames, never on a frame that uploaded or whose max(cpu, gpu) time exceeded half the frame budget, entries unused for 1800 frames; `TrimHandle::request` forces `TrimRequest::Everything`, exposed to the runtime as `RenderLink::request_trim`). `FrameRecord` also carries `approximated_items`, `glyphs` and `trimmed_entries`, and the inspector JSON prints them. Surface extents use `round`, not `ceil`, so a physical size survives a logical round trip. After `recover()` the host keeps adopted surfaces when the backend reports rebuilt surfaces.

   `render::lower::Lowering` lowers every `DisplayItem` variant; the only things it counts as unsupported are real failures (`VectorFailed`, `PathFailed`, `MeshTooLarge`, `MeshFailed`, `GlyphBudget`, `UnbalancedScope`). Items that reference a text run or image layer the render thread has not received yet count as `pending` (the app and the render thread share no ordering between the scene ring and the external-image channel) and are not unsupported. Decompositions, all in pure `shape.rs`: per-corner radii draw one full-size quad per distinct corner clipped to quadrant (or half) rectangles whose seams are snapped to whole pixels; uneven borders draw one ring clipped to each side strip; dashed borders draw the ring clipped to corner squares and 2/3-duty dashes (period 3 x width, evenly distributed, at most 256 per side); inset shadows (native and GPUI) are four outer half-plane shadows over the shadow hole composited with ordinary over (1 - gx*gy = a + b - ab holds exactly), inside a rounded clip; outer shadows with mixed radii use the same corner pieces with the clips extended outward by the blur reach. Transforms are a full 2D affine (`affine.rs`): rects map to their bounding box, glyphs and points map exactly, radii and sizes scale by sqrt(|det|); this is exact for translation, scale and quarter turns and counted in `approximated_items` otherwise (no pass can rotate an instance). Rounded clips use `PushRoundedClip` when the radius is at least 0.75 px at any size; the planner draws large ones as three bands plus four corner layers, so they are exact. GPUI items map as follows: `GpuiBackground` to `Paint::Solid`/`Gradient` (CSS angle axis), `GpuiBorder` to border rings, `GpuiShadow` as above, `PushGpuiContentMask` to a pixel-outward rectangular clip, quad pulses to an alpha factor from the render-side `Cycle::Pulse` for the pulse's scene item (14.N), text colour/source colour/opacity ranges/colour maps/shimmer as a `TextStack` resolved per glyph (shimmer phase from `ShimmerClocks`, driven by `LowerContext::now`), `GpuiSvg`/`GpuiTransformedSvg` through `Residency::vector` (rotation by wrapping the SVG in a rotated `<g>`, quantized to 3 degrees), `GpuiPath` by converting the commands to a `Drawing` cached by content with the integer part of the position removed (`vector.rs`), and `GpuiMesh` by a CPU triangle rasterizer (`mesh.rs`, z-buffer, the old key/fill/rim lighting, barycentric edge lines) cached as an image per mesh id, style and size, up to 400,000 triangles. Text uses `GlyphResolver::resolve` per glyph (not `GlyphResolver::lower`, which cannot apply the device scale, per-glyph colour or the affine) with `place_glyph`'s rounding rules.

   Recovery: `RecoverySignal` connects `WindowBackend::set_recovery_listener` to `Lowering::device_recovered`; the listener marks textures lost when the notice says `HostMustReupload`, and the lowering then rebuilds `Residency` and the glyph resolver (the atlas re-creates its pages on the next frame; there is no separate `PageTable` in this path); with `Replayed` textures the atlas is kept; a backend without a listener is treated as having lost everything. Startup (`native/gpu.rs`): the pipeline library is read from `%LOCALAPPDATA%\aqua\pipelines.cache` and written back by the maintenance hook (every 900 frames and on drop) when its fingerprint changed; the fallback index is loaded, the prewarm worker (`persist::spawn_prewarm`, sans-serif 400/500/600/700 and monospace 400/700) runs once per process and writes the index when done, and the hook writes it again when it changed. Verified on Windows (RX 9070 XT, hardware and WARP offscreen painters; `tests/render.rs` 45 tests and `tests/render_window.rs`: windowed `WindowBackend` through `GpuRenderHost`, GPU timestamps in the record, injected device removal with texture re-upload, pipeline cache round trip, prewarm). Round 6 closes two of the unverified items: rotated and skewed content is checked on D3D12 by pixel (`tests/host_motion.rs`, `rotated_and_skewed_boxes_land_on_their_transformed_footprint_on_d3d12`: a 45 degree box, a skewed box and a quarter turn, inside and outside samples, WARP and the discrete adapter) and in a real swapchain window (`tests/host_swapchain.rs`, below). Still not verified: pulses and shimmer without a frame trigger, 3D meshes against the old shader, and the `PushGpuiContentMask` plus rounded-clip interaction on large windows.

**14.N Render service, shared device, damage, composition, trim and repaint wake (2026-11-04, round 4, `crates/aqua/src/render`, `native/gpu.rs`).** Decisions:

1. One render thread for every window. `render::service::RenderService` owns a `Slot` per window (its command ring, event ring, `RenderSide`, `FrameTarget`, clock and `RenderStats`); `RenderLink::spawn` hands a slot over and `RenderLink::shutdown` takes the `RenderSide` back. The thread waits on one condition variable that every command ring, every `RenderLink::external` and `request_trim` raise, and otherwise sleeps until the earliest `RenderWake::At` or pending-repaint deadline, so an idle process runs no timer. A slot runs when it has commands, feedback, a due wake, an external arrival, a trim request or a due repaint, waits on its own swapchain slot, steps its `RenderSide` and executes.
2. One device. `native/gpu.rs` keeps a thread-local `Weak<Hub>`; the first window builds the `WindowBackend` (pipeline cache, text services), later windows join it, and the last window to close drops it (device memory is returned). Each window holds a `WindowHandle` that implements `GpuBackend` over `Rc<RefCell<WindowBackend>>`. Texture handles are `page.index * 2 + kind` per window, so a handle collides across windows; `render::namespace` ORs a window's namespace into bits 24 and up of every handle in uploads and plans (namespace 0, the first window, is unchanged and pays nothing). Recovery: the window that notices the loss calls `WindowBackend::recover`; every other handle sees the changed `generation` on its next `upload`, `execute` or `present`, fails that call once with `DeviceFailure` (so its host marks itself lost) and adopts the recovery in its own `recover`, which resets its lowering when textures must be re-uploaded. The one recovery listener fans out to every window's `RecoverySignal`.
3. Damage. `render::damage::DamagePlanner` builds `Footprints` of the render-side scene each frame (text ink from the laid-out size of the run, padded by 6 px plus 15% of its height for overhang), diffs them against the last presented frame with `DamageTracker::record_scene_diff`, pads each region by a pixel, coalesces to 8 regions and passes them to `WindowBackend::execute_damaged` and `present_with_damage` (the backend does the per-buffer age accumulation). It answers Full on the first frame, on a viewport or scale change, after a frame that did not present cleanly (`invalidate`), and when any damage touches a blur zone; pulses and shimmers contribute only their own footprints (14.N); an unchanged scene damages a single pixel. The baseline advances only after a `Presented` frame. Checked against the oracle: `tests/damage.rs` renders random scenes (rects with shadows, translated groups, removals) on `aqua-gpu-ref` at scale 1.0 and 1.25 and requires that the previous image with only the damaged regions replaced equals the full render.
4. Repaint without a scene change. `FrameTarget::repaint` reports `Animating` while the scene pulses or shimmers (one repaint per `ANIMATION_PERIOD`, silent), `NextFrame` during a snapshot transition (every vblank, reported), and `Poll(33 ms)` while lowering skipped an item whose image or text run had not arrived (at most 60 polls, then it waits for the next scene change or arrival). `RenderLink::external` also raises `RenderStats::arrivals`, which forces one full repaint. A forced frame re-presents the last frame id. Round 6: its `Presented` feedback is reported (it was dropped before, so an animation that ran only on repaint frames, a shimmer or a snapshot transition, never reached `on_frame_completed`); only a trim frame stays silent. The consequence is that `on_frame_completed` can receive the same sequence number more than once, once per presented repaint, and `Aqua` counts every one in `frames_presented`; applications key on `sequence >= last`, not `>`. `tests/render_service.rs` asserts 13 `Presented` events for 12 repaints plus the scene frame and 2 for a scene frame plus one arrival repaint.
5. Composition. A window whose clear colour is transparent (`Config::resolve` forces this for Mica, Mica Alt and Acrylic) gets `WindowBackend::create_composition_surface`; its swapchain content crosses to the UI thread as a `Box<dyn Any + Send>` through `CompositionSink`, `Aqua` attaches it in `drain_render_events` with `NativeWindow::attach_composition` (shell composition state is thread-local to the UI thread), and the target hands the content over again whenever the window handle's recovery generation changes.
6. Trim. `Aqua::request_trim` runs on `WindowEvent::Occluded(true)` (the shell reports minimize as occlusion) and `request_trim_all` on the rising edge of the Windows low-memory resource notification, polled at most every 2 s when the host pumps events or produces a frame (no timer). The slot runs a forced frame so `TrimPolicy` executes `TrimRequest::Everything` on the window's residency. Round 5: the shell reports occlusion by other windows (`aqua-shell-win32::occlusion`, `ShellEvent::Covered`, z-order walk with `DWMWA_EXTENDED_FRAME_BOUNDS`, cloaked and partial-overlap aware; `AQUA_RENDER_WHEN_COVERED=1` ignores it) next to minimize, and the host treats either as `Occluded`. `MemoryPressure` reads the notification through `WaitForSingleObject(handle, 0)`, the same path for the OS `CreateMemoryResourceNotification(LowMemoryResourceNotification)` handle and for a handle installed with `watch_notification`, so a test drives it with a manual-reset event; the OS notification itself cannot be raised without exhausting physical memory and was not. An occluded window builds no frame and pushes nothing to the render side, except that it still builds the scene for the accessibility tree while an access sink (UIA client or inspector) needs snapshots, and it then requests no animation deadline, so a covered window with a screen reader is never an empty tree. Round 6: the window backend's `set_visibility`, `service_budget` and `trim` are called by the host (11.3).

Verified on Windows (RX 9070 XT, debug build): `tests/render_shared.rs` runs three real windows (one transparent) on one service thread and one device (`device_sharers == 3`), animates one quad in each, and finds 97 to 103 partial frames and 1 full frame per window with an average of about 2,160 damaged pixels per partial frame, executes a trim request on every window, injects a device loss and sees all three windows keep presenting and the composition surface re-attached. `tests/render_service.rs` (6 tests, no GPU) covers sharing, idle quiet, pending polling, continuous repaint, arrival repaint, trim and closing one window. `tests/render.rs` (45) and `tests/render_window.rs` pass unchanged; `host_windows` passes. The `host_native` stall was root-caused in round 5 and is not in device creation: traces showed `Occluded(true)` from the shell before the first frame (another window, often another agent's test window at the default cascade position, fully covered the new window), `produce_frame` returned early for an occluded window, so no scene, no access tree and no inspector tree existed until the UIA client activated the window and uncovered it. Fixes: a covered window still builds the access tree while a sink needs it (above), `host_native` no longer depends on desktop state, `windows_desktop` sets `AQUA_RENDER_WHEN_COVERED=1` because it asserts `build_scene` runs for its main window (its own child window covers the main one), and `host_trim` raises its two windows to the top of the topmost group and drives its phases from a mailbox wake instead of frame completions (a covered window completes none). Results (debug, RX 9070 XT, WSL-launched .exe): `windows_desktop` 20 of 20 (was about 25 to 70 percent failing), `host_native` 30 of 30 plus 40 of 40 on the same fixed code (was 2 of 6 to 7 of 14 failing; one run in 15 earlier failed with the UIA Invoke not reaching the runtime while another window held the foreground, not reproduced in 70 later runs), `host_trim` 12 of 12, `host_material` 3 of 3, `pressure` 2 of 2. `host_trim` signals a Win32 event that stands in for the OS low-memory handle, trims once per rising edge, sees no trim after release, then covers the main window with a second window (trim on occlusion) and sees it exposed again. `tests/host_material.rs` runs three child processes, one per material, each with a transparent host window, a composition surface and an opaque quad, and measures the desktop with `BitBlt` after settling: the DWM system backdrop attribute is exactly 2 (Mica), 4 (Mica Alt) and 3 (Acrylic), the backdrop is non-black everywhere (inactive: Mica and Mica Alt flat 32/32/32, Acrylic flat 84/84/84; active Mica 27/33/38 follows the wallpaper), and the opaque quad is the exact sRGB `#20c0ff`. `shell_options` now passes `alpha_surface: options.transparent` so a transparent host window is created with `WS_EX_NOREDIRECTIONBITMAP`; this was not A/B tested against the old value. Not verified: the OS-raised low-memory notification, Acrylic while active, touch/pen.

**14.M Host motion wiring, popup presence, hoisted-child panic.** Decisions: (1) one `AppMotion` per runtime, owned by `OverlayHost`, and `MotionRuntime` borrows it, because two arenas would mint colliding `TrackId`s on one render-side `TrackSet`; (2) `MotionRuntime` tracks are bound by a separate `Bind`/`Unbind` command pair carrying a scroll shift, not by the `Spawn` binding, because the scene node does not exist when the track is spawned and scene keys shift when a node's item count changes; (3) popup exit keeps the mounted subtree rather than a copy, and a popup with no exit motion still closes in the same frame. The taffy `invalid SlotMap key` panic was a taffy 0.15 defect: a container whose last child was an out-of-flow node (every exiting row and every popup root is one) became a leaf when that child was removed, and a leaf never clears its hoisted-child list, so the next rounding pass read the dead key. `UiTree::remove` now parks a zero-size guard leaf under such a container for exactly one layout pass. `UiTree::content_children` excludes exiting nodes so the binding walk matches the lowered element. `aqua-scene::Footprints` and `DamageTracker::record_scene_diff` give whole-group damage (old and new extent of a translated or faded group, once) and item-level damage inside a group; the host feeds them to `execute_damaged` and `present_with_damage` (record 14.N).
14.R **Host input, IME and accessibility wiring (2026-11-04, `crates/aqua/src/native`, `aqua-ime-tsf`, `aqua-a11y-uia`):** the host installs `TsfHook` as the shell message hook; it forwards `WM_KEYDOWN/UP` and `WM_SYSKEYDOWN/UP` to `ITfKeystrokeMgr::TestKeyDown/KeyDown` (or the Up pair) only while a `TsfIme` holds TSF focus (`aqua_ime_tsf::Keystrokes`), and consumes a key only when a text service ate it. `ShellEvent::Clipboard` now carries `image: Option<ClipboardImage>` and `paths`; the host reads text, image and file list from the system clipboard (`aqua::paste::clipboard_event`) and the runtime pastes all three. `ShellEvent::CaptionHover` reaches `Application::on_caption_hover` and the Machine. `AccessSink::connection_epoch` makes the host resend `AccessClient { connected: true }` when UIA connects after another consumer already forced snapshots, because the adapter drops updates while dormant and the Machine only re-emits the whole tree on that input. The inspector socket is started by `AQUA_INSPECTOR=<port|auto>` (loopback only); every applied access tree is published as JSON (`aqua_a11y_uia::render_json`) for the `tree` command and render counters for `stats`; while it is enabled the host builds access snapshots even without a UIA client. A TSF composition that is emptied over a non-empty replaced range now ends as `Cancel` followed by `Replace { range, text: "" }`, because the editor restores the replaced text on `Cancel` while TSF has already deleted it. Verified on Windows: `aqua-ime-tsf/tests/runtime.rs` (real TSF context driving a real `Runtime`: Select and Replace), `aqua/tests/host_native.rs` (real host: UIA client Invoke, Focus, typed text through the hook, inspector tree and stats), `aqua/tests/host_windows.rs` (two windows, `WM_DPICHANGED` on one only, caption hover, closing one window), `aqua/tests/clipboard_host.rs` (text, image and file list on the real clipboard, restored afterwards). Not verifiable under the no-global-input rule: real touch and pen (`WM_POINTER*` cannot be posted, injection is global input) and a cross-process OLE file drop (`DoDragDrop` needs a held mouse button). Note: UIA `Invoke` activates the target window, so `host_native` brings its own window to the foreground once.

### 14.U Shape cache, Slug warm-up and lock-free glyph lookup (decided 2026-11-04, round 6; `aqua-text::shape_cache`, `aqua-text::slug`, `aqua-text::residency`, `aqua-core::hash`, `aqua::render::lower`)

Evidence (release, Windows, `aqua-text/tests/shape_atlas_probe.rs`, 400 markdown-like blocks of about 330 characters with regular, bold and monospace spans, 12 blocks on screen, one frame per block of scroll):

| Measurement | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Fresh `TextLayout` of unchanged text at another width (what the runtime and the measure path do on every resize or remint), per block | 77 us (every paragraph re-shaped) | 48 us (no shaping; the remainder is line breaking and glyph placement) |
| Mask-atlas lowering of 12 visible blocks, steady frame p50 | 0.357 ms | 0.161 ms (hashing) |
| Slug lowering of 12 visible blocks, p50 | 0.157 ms | 0.069 ms (hashing and warm store) |
| Slug store growth events during the scroll | 1 at frame 0 (the store grows on every new glyph that overflows its 8 rows) | 1 at frame 0 (the whole printable ASCII range of each face is resident after its first glyph, 285 glyphs, 0.5 MiB) and none afterwards |
| Streaming one token into a 1.6 KB paragraph, full relayout, p50 / p99 | 0.122 / 0.263 ms | 0.078 / 0.158 ms |
| Streaming one token into a 120-line code block (one text node, one paragraph per line), p50 / p99 | 0.252 / 0.309 ms | 0.169 / 0.265 ms |

Decisions:

- **Persistent paragraph shape cache** in `FontManager` (`shape_cache.rs`). `ShapedParagraph` is shared as an `Arc`; the key is a 128-bit digest of the paragraph text, its absolute start, the clipped spans that overlap it (range, `TextStyle::shaping_hash`, inline cell width), the scale, the line-height and inline-cell flags and the font revision. Colors, underline and background are applied after shaping, so they are not in the key. Two generations of at most 4 MiB each (`SHAPE_CACHE_BUDGET_BYTES`), promoted on hit, cleared on a font-set change. Because the runtime builds a new `TextLayout` on every width change and the measure path builds another for the same string, both now hit; appending to a text node shapes only the changed paragraph. Counters: `FontManager::shape_cache_stats()`, printed as `aqua-text-stats` by the host.
- **Layout fast paths.** `build_display` no longer walks grapheme clusters when no mask is set (a newline is always a standalone grapheme unless it follows a carriage return, so concatenation, or a newline-to-space swap in single-line mode, is identical; a test compares both against `unicode-segmentation`). The `InputKey` style key is a 128-bit digest of the span styles instead of a formatted string.
- **Warm-up.** `SlugStore::lookup` makes the printable ASCII range of a face resident the first time any glyph of that face is requested, in the same frame, so the store texture is created once at its final size and later glyphs of that range never upload. Glyphs outside ASCII still upload on first use (one dirty-row write, no texture re-creation unless the pow2 row count overflows). The 8-bit mask atlas (used only when `outline_text` is off) keeps on-demand rasterization; its first shared page is 512 (was 256) so the first 400 distinct glyph variants do not grow it. Warming the mask atlas for four subpixel bins per size was not done: the Editor defaults to outline text, and the mask path would pay 380 rasterizations on first sight of each size.
- **Lock-free resident lookups.** `SharedFonts` mirrors the font revision in an atomic. The lowering takes the font mutex only when a glyph is not resident (`SlugStore::resident`, `GlyphResolver::resolve_shared`); the render thread no longer blocks behind a UI-thread shaping call for text it already has.
- **Fast hashing.** Atlas entries, Slug store and cache, face interning and the shape cache use `aqua_media::hash::FastMap` (multiply-rotate hasher) instead of SipHash; the keys are internal.
- **Counters for the spike recorder.** `LowerReport` carries `atlas_created`, `atlas_upload_bytes`, `glyph_rasterized`, `slug_created` and `slug_upload_bytes` per frame; `RenderStats` accumulates them and `aqua-text-stats` prints them. `aqua/tests/text_steady_scroll.rs` gates zero growth, zero rasterization and zero upload bytes while scrolling through ASCII text in outline mode.

### 14.V Keystroke latency path (decided 2026-11-04, round 6; `aqua::latency`, `aqua::runtime`, `aqua::native`, `tools/key-latency.sh`)

Measurement. `AQUA_INPUT_LATENCY=1|2` stamps every keystroke (key down, text input, IME events) at host entry and reports, per presented frame, the phases input, queue (end of input dispatch to frame start), build, handoff (scene queued to render thread pickup), GPU wait (frame-latency waitable) and the total up to the render thread's present call; `2` prints every sample, `1` one summary per 40 samples. The render thread counts the scene commands it applies (`RenderStats::scenes_applied`, `scenes_presented`) so a keystroke is matched to the frame that carries its scene, not to the next frame. `tools/key-latency.sh aqua|gpui [keys|open]` runs the same scripted typing against the private bench Forge for both Editors: `win-drive.ps1` posts WM_KEYDOWN/WM_KEYUP (the host's own `TranslateMessage` produces the characters) with a precise system-time stamp per key, an ETW session on `Microsoft-Windows-DXGI` records the owning process's `Present` start/stop events, and `tools/key-latency-report.py` pairs each key with the first `Present` return after it. Elevated tracing (`wpr`) is refused without administrator rights; user-level `logman` sessions work and are what the tool uses. The figure is the Present call return, not scan-out: Aqua's Present never blocks (frame latency 1), so add up to one refresh period (6.06 ms at the measured 165 Hz) for photon time; GPUI paces on display ticks, its Present return is quantised to the display state and swung between 4 and 18 ms in this setup.

Findings (release, 2560x1440, large bench thread, composer typing, 8 interleaved runs):

| Phase per keystroke before | Value |
|---|---|
| Whole-view rebuild per keystroke (`rebuilds` 16 per s at 9 keys per s, reason `shell_updates`) | 3.6 ms rebuild plus 7 to 11 ms layout visiting 18.7k nodes |
| Input dispatch of the text event | 4 to 8 ms (mostly the rebuild) |
| Present-return latency p50 / p90 / p99 / max | 23.6 / 27.8 / 32.7 / 58.8 ms |

Causes and fixes:

- The composer's change detection (`ComposerVisible`) compared the editor text and selection, so every keystroke rebuilt the whole Editor view although the editor node already receives its text through `UiCommand::EditText` in the same input event. Typed events (`EditorEvent::Changed`, `SelectionChanged`, `PresentationChanged`) now compare chrome only (empty and blank flags, disabled, limit notice, suggestion, tokens, picker, mention, questionnaire, attachments); programmatic edits keep the full comparison and `drain_async` rebuilds only when the editor revision moved inside the drain. Rebuilds during typing went from 16 per second to 0.
- A message consumed by the TSF hook never reached the loop's queue, so IME text waited for the next unrelated tick. A consumed message now queues a `Wake` (`aqua-shell-win32::looping`).
- `Host::tick` drained the inbox (3 ms `on_wake` work in the Editor) before drawing; it now drains IME text and draws input frames first (`Aqua::drain_text`, `draw_input_frames`), then pumps the inbox and draws the rest.
- The process opts out of execution-speed power throttling and ignores the timer-resolution policy, and the render service and UI thread run at above-normal priority (`native::priority`), because background windows were woken about 1.4 ms late.

Result: Aqua present-return p50 4.6 to 5.5 ms, p90 5.5 to 6.9, p99 7.8 to 9.8, max 8 to 11 ms (three runs, 150 keys each), no key over 16.7 ms; GPUI p50 18.0 to 18.4, p99 21.5 to 24 ms in the same display state and 4 to 5.5 ms p50 in the low-latency display state; the p99 target of 12 ms is met in 7 of 8 runs (one run under heavy parallel build load measured p99 32 ms).

Open decisions closed: the Present return is the keystroke-to-present stamp for both Editors (above); typing is measured in a scratch window against the private bench Forge only.

Thread open (bench thread, 3 runs): fetch of the tail page 11 ms, fold 0.03 ms, apply 0.04 ms; first scene 41 to 54 ms after open (cold shaping of the first rows), first present 100 to 114 ms, then six more frames of 15 to 40 ms layout while the work spans and older pages arrive. The Forge's first-paint read acknowledgement arrives 548 ms after open (736 to 756 ms after the driver's click, GPUI 250 to 272 ms) because the Editor port withholds it until every visible reveal is settled (`reveals.pending` and `reveals.changed` must both be false), which takes four frame round trips of layout-bound frames. The remaining open gap is layout cost per rebuild (14.5 to 36 ms), not fetch or fold.

### 14.W Heap attribution, allocator choice and idle trim (decided 2026-11-04, round 6; `aqua::mem_probe`, `aqua-transport::trim`, `aqua-ui::tree::interaction`, `tools/heap-attribution.sh`)

Decisions, each backed by the measurements in section 11.4. (1) Heap attribution is done with the in-process stack-hash allocator plus offline symbolization; in-process symbolization of a 180 MB executable never finished and is not used. (2) The system allocator stays: mimalloc cost +76 to +80 MiB private and the segment heap gained nothing. (3) Per-node retained state that only some nodes use is boxed and created on demand (`InteractionBase`); the pattern applies to any future per-node side table. (4) Idle trim is time-driven: `TrimInput::now`, `TrimPolicy::idle_deadline`, one deadline per idle episode folded into the render service's wait, no periodic timer, so idle wakes stay at zero between episodes. The trim pass empties the working set; private bytes are reported separately because the working-set step does not reduce them. (5) `HOST_WINDOWED` framework heap is 12 MiB. (6) Not decided and still open: the 100 MiB private target. The user-mode driver's write-combined private memory (47.7 MiB picker, 62.8 MiB thread view) is the largest single item and belongs to the GPU backend; Aqua's own controllable share is Rust heap 25 MiB plus fragmentation 15 MiB.

### 14.X Scoreboard harness and unified measurement (decided 2026-11-04, round 7; `tools/compare-editors.sh`, `compare-editors-report.py`, `key-latency.sh`, `win-drive.ps1`)

Decisions. (1) Scoreboard runs never log: `AQUA_STATS_OVERRIDE=off` is exported by the script and `win-drive.ps1` then removes `AQUA_FRAME_STATS`, `AQUA_PERF`, `AQUA_WAKE_STATS`, `AQUA_PROFILE`, `AQUA_INPUT_LATENCY`, `AQUA_REBUILD_STATS` and `AQUA_MEMORY_REPORT` from the child environment, because per-frame stderr logging inflates the port (`win-app` turns the stats on for every executable path). Instrumentation runs only in the aqua-only diagnostic phases `wakesrc` (wake sources per second: turns, window messages, inbox posts, render-thread runs; idle, then after a resize) and `spikes` (the spike recorder at 4 ms over two scroll bursts), which are reported but never gated. (2) Keystroke latency is the ETW measurement of 14.V, run per scoreboard run for both editors by `key-latency.sh` (same fixture Forge, own port); the PrintWindow pixel probe stays as an informational row. (3) Runs are interleaved by phase and the order alternates per run, so a slow drift of the machine does not favour one target; executables are snapshotted once so a concurrent rebuild cannot change a run, and `manifest.txt` records the git revisions, build times and hashes. (4) The display state is recorded: `probe-display` (adapter, resolution, refresh rate, power plan) and the system CPU load around every main run; GPUI's Present return swings between about 18 ms and 4 to 5 ms with the display state, so each run's ETW key p50 is classified fast (under 8 ms) or slow and the report warns when a target mixes states. A machine above 25% system CPU fails the verdict (`AQUA_COMPARE_MAX_SYSTEM_LOAD`), because other jobs make every number meaningless. (5) Idle is sampled for 30 s (previously 12) with per-thread context switches and CPU for 15 s more (thread name, start module, rate), once at rest and once after a resize cycle, and the rows count the threads waking at 1/s or more and the busiest thread's switches/s; these are the wake-source rows. (6) Rows added: frames over 4 ms (scroll, streaming), keystroke ETW p50/p90/p99/max and counts over 8.3 and 16.7 ms, idle CPU and switches after a resize, private and GPU dedicated after 600 scrolls, streaming latency. (7) `verdict.txt` rule: the port beats GPUI by at least 3% or both are zero; a missing value fails. One scoreboard at a time (`/tmp/aqua-scoreboard.lock`): two concurrent runs share the fixture Forge port and the machine. The AMD frame-tracker warm-up (160 presents at device creation) counts against cold start and is not touched.

First joint scoreboard after round 6 (2026-11-05, `docs/scoreboard/post-round-6/`; release builds of both editors, three interleaved runs, 30 s idle, machine at 10% system CPU, executables and hashes in `manifest.txt`; the port build is the uncommitted worktree of 00:30, so it includes round 7 work in progress): aqua passes 22 of the 40 gated rows (the 40 are in `scoreboard.txt`; two keystroke-to-pixel rows are informational), OVERALL FAIL. Wins: keystroke to Present return (ETW) p50 2.45 against 17.7 ms, p99 3.3 against 20.4, zero keystrokes over 8.3 ms against all 145; scroll p90, p99, max (0.50, 1.13, 2.80 against 1.52, 2.51, 2.91 ms) and zero frames over 4 ms; resize p99; streaming p99 and context switches. Losses: scroll p50 0.23 against 0.05 ms, CPU per scroll input 29 against 3.7 ms, idle context switches 10 against 0.2 per second (two threads wake at 5 to 6 per second: the host main thread and an amdxc64 driver thread, 0 CPU, with the instrumented diagnostic showing `inbox-wake` turns driven by `render-events@aqua-render` and `window-requests@main` posts), private 148 against 118 MiB, GPU dedicated 65.2 against 51.1 MiB, cold start 440 against 217 ms, open large thread 292 against 236 ms, resize p50 2.37 against 2.29 ms, streaming CPU and p50 at parity. The spike recorder diagnostic reports 26 frames over 4 ms in 600 scroll inputs, 17 of them input-dominated and 7 layout-dominated. A first pass taken at 25 to 100% system CPU (other jobs building) produced scroll p50 27 ms and layout-dominated spikes of 80 to 150 ms, which is why the verdict now requires a quiet machine and waits for one before every phase.

Open: the GPUI display-state swing cannot be forced from the script, only recorded; `wpr.exe` stays unused because it requires elevation.

### 14.RS Resize responsiveness (decided 2026-11-04, round 7; `aqua-runtime::scheduler`, `aqua-core::checkpoint`, `aqua-shell-win32::looping`, `aqua::native::looping`, `aqua::runtime`)

Evidence. The scoreboard resize probe is `SetWindowPos` followed by a `WM_NULL` `SendMessageTimeout`, so it measures how long the UI thread stays deaf to sent messages. Release port, fixture, 120 resizes: p50 38 to 77 ms, p99 79 to 158 ms (machine shared with other jobs). Per resize the UI thread ran a full view rebuild inside the `Resized` dispatch (2.1 to 4.5 ms), then a frame of 9 to 15 ms with roughly 70% in two taffy passes plus row realisation (the width-sensitive transcript collection rebuilds its rows on a width change), then a second frame for the `WM_PAINT` that the resize itself invalidated. Backend and render-thread work was under 4 ms and not the cause.

Decisions. (1) The shell swallows the first `WM_PAINT` after a `WM_SIZE`; the resize already requests a frame. (2) The host holds `Resized` events per window and dispatches the latest one once per frame, flushing earlier only for events that must see the new size (clicks, keys, focus, scale); coalescible pointer input and window bookkeeping events do not flush. (3) After a frame whose reasons contain `RESIZE`, the scheduler gates the next `RESIZE` frame for 1.5 times the frame's cost (cost capped at 30 ms), which bounds the UI thread duty cycle during a continuous resize to 40%; other reasons are not gated and the final size always gets its frame. (4) `aqua_core::checkpoint::reach()` is a cooperative yield point: the Win32 host installs `pump_sent_messages`, a rate-limited (300 us) `PeekMessage(PM_NOREMOVE | PM_QS_SENDMESSAGE)` that answers sent messages from long frames; the window procedure only enqueues events, so it is safe to run mid-frame. Checkpoints sit between the layout passes, per row factory call, per taffy leaf measure, between frame phases and inside the Editor port's rebuild.

Measured (release, fixture, same harness, noisy shared machine): p50 3.1 ms, p90 5.2 ms, p99 7.6 ms, max 7.7 ms for the port against GPUI p50 2.9 ms, p99 8.9 ms in the adjacent run; three further alternating pairs ranged 2.1 to 4.8 ms (port) and 3.0 to 7.5 ms (GPUI) at p50 because of external load. The final frame after the resize loop shows the full window at the final size. Windows tests that exercise the loop (host_full 5/5, host_window 3/3, host_windows, host_counters, collections_conformance, facade_pump, perf_scene) pass.

Open: p50 is at the harness floor, so beating GPUI needs a quiet machine and repeated pairs; the width-sensitive collection still rebuilds every resident row on a width change (about 5 ms uncontended), and the Editor view is rebuilt in full on every dispatched resize.

Round 8 (decided 2026-11-05; `aqua-ui::collection`, `aqua-ui::binding`, `aqua-core::checkpoint`, `aqua-shell-win32::gaps`, `editor-aqua::shell::{application,root}`, `editor-aqua::conversation::view::transcript`).

Evidence. Profile of the fixture resize loop (release, `resizeprof` phase, spike recorder and rebuild stats): per dispatched resize two full Editor rebuilds ran (reasons `sections` 40/s from the `Resized` dispatch and `viewport` 41/s from the transcript viewport callback, 1.2 to 1.6 ms each, 86 of 123 ms/s in `rb_refresh`), and the width-sensitive transcript re-realized every resident row on every width change (`row_rerealized` 1481/s, about 37 rows per frame) although the row factory only depends on `min(content_width, 768)`. Process CPU per resize (120 resizes, `cpu` probes, the same exe, legacy behaviour toggled by an environment variable that was removed afterwards): median 9.0 ms before, 7.5 ms after. With the app idle (window covered, no frames produced) the same probe measures p50 2.74 ms, p99 6.1 ms and 1.3 ms CPU per resize, so p50 is bounded by the OS and DWM cost of a synchronous cross-process `SetWindowPos`, not by the app; the app contributes about 0.3 to 0.5 ms at p50 and 1 to 2 ms at p99.

Decisions. (1) `List::width_class` maps the committed width to the value rows actually depend on; `CollectionSource::width_class` compares the old and new plan through it and rebuilds resident rows only when the class changes (`aqua-ui` test `width_class_keeps_rows_while_the_class_is_stable_and_rebuilds_when_it_changes`). The transcript declares `min(width, 768)`, so resizing a window wider than the 768 px column re-wraps through layout only (`f_realize` 1476/s to 2/s). (2) `SectionViews::resize_needs_rebuild`: a `Resized` event no longer forces a view rebuild when the screen is a thread with a live transcript viewport, no overlay is open, and the discrete layout key (sidebar width, inspector width, width >= 640) is unchanged; height-dependent parts (sidebar history spacer, navigation rail) are rebuilt by the transcript viewport callback that follows every height change (`sections` 40/s to 4 to 7/s). (3) Checkpoints now also sit in `View::set_content` (after lowering, refresh, binding, before pruning), in `Runtime::build_scene` (after emit, dynamic items, reconcile), in the host frame tail (chrome, access, push_scene), in `Host::tick` between every step, in the Editor `rebuild`, `drain`, `RootViews::build` and `frame_completed`. `reach()` is `#[track_caller]` and hands its location to the pump; `AQUA_GAP_TRACE=1` makes the Win32 pump report, per second, the longest unyielded gaps between checkpoint pairs (`aqua-gap from=file:line to=file:line`); the `resizeprof` phase of the scoreboard script enables it. After the additions no steady resize frame has a gap of 1 ms or more; the remaining gaps in the profile run are the once-per-second stderr flush of the profiler itself (5 to 24 ms, absent without profiling) and a 3 ms `process_requests` step (window request dispatch) about once per second.

Measured (release, fixture, private Forge, shared machine with other agents building: system CPU often 90 to 100 %, so absolute latency is noisy): 10 runs of 120 resizes, median p50 2.54 ms, p99 6.2 ms, max 8.9 ms, 0 to 1 samples over 16.7 ms; best quiet runs p50 2.09 to 2.13 ms, p90 2.7 ms, p99 7 to 12 ms from first-resize cold paths. The p50 target of 2 ms is at the OS floor of this harness and is not met; GPUI measures 2.2 to 2.9 ms with it.

Scroll max. 300-frame spike runs on the fixture (release, `spikes` phase): probe max 6.4 and 16.9 ms, app frame max 6.4 ms; frames over 4 ms (about 30 of 600) are the navigation rail rebuild when the active turn marker changes (`viewport` reason, 2 to 3 per second, 2 ms rebuild plus layout of newly measured rows 3 to 4 ms). The 107 to 437 ms maxima of earlier runs were not reproduced; they coincide with runs where system CPU was saturated by concurrent builds (resize p50 of the same exe rose from 2.5 to 12 ms in such a run). The 3 ms scroll max target is not met: removing the marker-change rebuild needs a subtree refresh API in `aqua-ui` (the rail is rebuilt through the whole-view path).

Open. Whole-view rebuild per height change (sidebar history spacer, rail) needs the same subtree refresh; the first resize frame is still ungated (it is bounded by the checkpoints now); `aqua-verify` `adapters_expose_only_declared_root_items` reports `wake_trace` (shell) and `ledger` (d3d12) as undeclared root items, not caused by this area.

### 14.RS Resize without ResizeBuffers (decided 2026-11-05, round 10; `aqua-gpu-d3d11::hal::swapchain`, `aqua-gpu-d3d11::window::backend`)

Evidence. The scoreboard resize probe is `SetWindowPos` plus a `WM_NULL` `SendMessageTimeout`, so about 1.4 to 2.0 ms of every sample is the operating system inside `SetWindowPos` (the `resize_setpos` row, identical for GPUI); the port adds about 0.3 ms on top. Profile of the fixture resize loop (release, `resizeprof`, 120 resizes): every dispatched resize ran `ResizeBuffers` on the render thread (`resize_us` 430 to 1040 in the spike notes) and recreated the transients (`reserve_us` 340 to 1130 against 140 steady). Path rasters: five `GpuiPath` items of 800x150 to 1018x253 are rasterized per resize frame (`describe_us` 2 to 11, `resident_us` 50 to 140 for solid fills and 150 to 420 for gradient fills and strokes, about 1.0 ms per frame together).

Decisions. (1) HWND swapchains keep a buffer capacity separate from the logical extent. A resize that fits the capacity (and leaves at most 384 px of slack per axis) only calls `IDXGISwapChain2::SetSourceSize`; a larger one reallocates with `ResizeBuffers` to the extent rounded up to 128 px. `Swapchain::extent` stays the logical size, `texture_extent` is the buffer size. (2) `SceneTarget::extent` carries the texture size: the surface binds, viewport, constants and the backdrop mirror use it, so drawing stays 1:1 from the top left and sampling the surface for backdrop blur clamps to the plan regions, not to the buffer. `read_surface` already crops by width, height and pitch. (3) The capacity shrinks back to the exact size in `WindowBackend::settle_surfaces`, called from the idle maintenance hook (2 s after the last frame) just before the forced trim frame, which repaints in full because the damage history is reset. Composition surfaces keep exact `ResizeBuffers`. (4) D3D12 has a no-op `settle_surfaces`; it is stashed.

Measured (release, fixture, `resize` phase, 120 resizes per sample, old and new executables alternated with GPUI in the same invocation, three pairs on a machine that other agents were loading, so absolute values are 1.9 to 2.4 ms): resize p50 old 2.03, 2.41, 2.27, 2.04, 2.38, 2.26 (two resize samples per pair) (mean 2.23 ms) against GPUI 2.06 in the same pairs; new 1.99, 1.98, 2.17, 1.89, 2.19, 2.31 (mean 2.09 ms) against GPUI 2.21. The port goes from 8 % behind to 5 % ahead of GPUI at p50, `resize_setpos` mean 1.94 to 1.82 ms, and p99 from 2.8 to 8.7 ms (one 1.1 s stall in the old run) to 2.9 to 4.5 ms. The 1.5 ms target is below the `SetWindowPos` floor measured on GPUI itself and is not reachable by the adapter.

Path rasters are not on the critical path. With `AQUA_DIAG_SKIP_PATHS` (a temporary switch in `emit_path` that dropped every `GpuiPath`, removed again) the same executable measured resize p50 2.12 ms with paths and 2.05 ms without, 4 alternated pairs each, spread 0.2 ms: at most 3 %. Moving the solid fills to the R8 mask atlas would also route their edges through the glyph tone curve, and the gradient glass paths have no masked gradient primitive, so no GPU path was built; `emit_path` now notes `describe_us`, `resident_us` and the path style per path in the spike recorder. Transient size rounding (64 px) stays open: the blur passes derive their scale from the transient extent and need the logical extent passed separately first.

Verification. `tests/window/resize.rs` (hardware and WARP): seven small grow and shrink steps with a backdrop blur at the lower right edge match `aqua-gpu-ref` within the existing tolerance after each step, the swapchain bytes exceed the exact size while the capacity is larger and return to it after `settle_surfaces`, and the settled frame matches again. D3D11 window tests 60 of 60, conformance, differential and debug layer pass; a PrintWindow check after three `setsize` steps and 6 s idle shows no blank or clipped frame.

### 14.SC Render-thread planning cost (decided 2026-11-04, round 7; `aqua-graph::graph`, `aqua-graph::paint`, `aqua::render::lower`, `aqua::render::damage`, `aqua-scene::footprint`, `aqua-core::hash`)

Decisions. (1) `RenderGraph` merges draws as they are pushed. `draw_quad`, `draw_shadow`, `draw_glyphs` and `draw_slug` append instances to an earlier compatible pass without building a `Pass` per leaf, and a draw may hop over earlier passes writing the same target when the drawn extents (instance bounds intersected with the clip) are disjoint. This replaces the alternating quad and glyph passes of a card layout with one pass per kind; the final merge in `plan_with_stats` is kept for the passes the fast path does not cover, and `plan_with_stats` no longer clones the pass list. (2) `Lowering` no longer copies the whole previous command list every frame; the transport hands the planned list back through `Lowering::reclaim`, which keeps it as the transition snapshot only when no transition overlay was applied, and the command buffer is reused across frames. A running transition still snapshots by copy. (3) `FastHasher` moved from `aqua-media::hash` to `aqua-core::hash` (re-exported by `aqua-media`) so the damage footprints and run cache use it.

Measured (Linux, release, `crates/aqua/tests/perf_emit.rs`, 635 scene items, 633 commands, 3616 glyphs): passes after planning 37 to 12; plan 153 to 21 us p50; damage planning 32 to 21 us p50; lowering 60 to about 51 us p50. On Windows (fixture, release, spike recorder) the render thread averages lower 0.21 ms, plan 0.20 ms, record 0.27 ms, present 0.25 ms per scroll frame with work p50 1.08 to 0.92 ms; the real scene contains layers and blur chains that the fast path does not cover.

Open. Scroll p50 in the scoreboard is the UI-thread round trip of a posted wheel message and CPU per scroll input is dominated by the app thread, not by the render thread: during the fixture scroll the app frames show layout 6 to 17 ms with about 1440 nodes committed and 21600 nodes visited per frame because the virtualized collection remounts rows, and 661 scene items are re-emitted and reconciled each frame. Retained subtree emit in `aqua-ui` and incremental layout of mounted rows are the remaining work for the 0.05 ms p50 and 3 ms CPU targets; they are not met.

### 14.RE Round 8 render-thread emit reuse and lowering (decided 2026-11-05; `aqua::render::lower`, `aqua::render::shape`, `aqua-graph::paint`, `aqua-graph::graph`)

Evidence (release, Windows, `crates/aqua/tests/perf_lower.rs`: a glide translate over 9 wrapped paragraphs (2709 glyphs), 220 rounded chips with shadows and clips, two glass panels with rounded clips and backdrop blur, one translucent group; 364 scene items, 362 commands, 27 passes; machine shared with other builds, so only same-process ratios and the minimum of several runs are quoted). Before: lower 281 us p50, plan 295 us p50, 585 us in total. Per-phase laps inside the planner showed emit 136 us, barriers 28 us, allocate 17 us, parse 29 us (a clone of every glyph list), and lowering was dominated by per-glyph resolution because the text run cache keyed on the fractional part of the vertical position, which changes on every glide frame.

Decisions. (1) A cached text run no longer keys on the fractional vertical position. Each cached glyph keeps its unrounded offset below the run origin and its bearing (`PenY`), and a hit recomputes `round(base_y + offset) - bearing`, the same expression the miss path evaluates, so a run hit at any vertical offset equals a fresh lowering (`crates/aqua/tests/run_cache.rs` compares bounds and colours against a fresh `Lowering` at nine offsets). Horizontal position still keys on its fractional part because it selects the subpixel raster. An unchanged position reuses the cached commands by reference. (2) `GlyphRun::glyphs` is a `GlyphList`, an `Arc` shared list with `From<Vec<Glyph>>` and `FromIterator`, so caching, retaining a frame, the transition snapshot and planner parsing clone a pointer; culling uses copy on write. (3) `RenderGraph::draw_glyphs` computes the extent in one pass over the source glyphs and writes the instances once into the target pass instead of through a scratch buffer, and `derive_barriers` groups transient textures by first use instead of scanning all textures for every pass. (4) `shape::pieces` returns a stack array instead of a `Vec`, which removes an allocation per box, ring and shadow. (5) `Walk::remember_fill` stores the opaque fill colour and converts it to a luminance only when a text run asks for its backdrop; the eager conversion ran a colour space conversion for every opaque box and cost about 50 us of the 90 us lowering time of the glide scene. (6) `ROUNDED_CLIP_AREA_CAP` is gone: every rounded clip with a radius of at least 0.75 px lowers to `PushRoundedClip`, and the planner's band and corner split makes large ones exact; 13.1 item 6 no longer lists the 75 percent rectangle fallback.

Measured after (same harness, four runs on a quieter machine, medians of each run's p50): lower 38 to 45 us, plan 102 to 136 us, about 140 to 180 us in total, against 585 us before; the same-process step sizes were lower 281 to 129 us after the run cache change, to 88 us after the shared glyph lists and `pieces`, and to about 40 us after (5). `perf_emit` (635 items, 3616 glyphs) lowers a text run for about 1 us on a hit. Planner phases on the glide scene: emit 136 to 87 us, barriers 28 to 13 us, allocate 17 to 2.5 us, parse 29 us (clone of every glyph list) to a pointer copy. Verified on Windows after the change: aqua-gpu-d3d12 conformance 6/6, tiers 6/6, effects 3/3, transients 5/5; aqua-verify d3d12_conformance 3/3 and d3d12_differential 9/9 (single thread); aqua render 56/56, damage 16/16, render_service 9/9, namespace 4/4; aqua-verify host_full 5/5, host_counters 3/3, heap_gates 6/6, retained_heap 1/1, render_allocations 1/1.

Open (closed in 14.RT for the scroll offset). Subtree reuse in `aqua-ui::tree::emit_node` is not done. The scroll offset is subtracted from every child rectangle, so a scroll step changes all 574 emitted items and the scene diff carries them; reusing a retained subtree needs scroll content emitted in content coordinates under one translate with an overscan margin, which also changes culling, hit testing and the glide binding, and `tree.rs` was being rewritten by the layout package this round. The full-frame scoreboard was not re-run because the scoreboard lock was held by other runs for the whole round; the numbers above are the isolated lower and plan phases, not a joint scoreboard.

### 14.RT Round 9 retained scroll emit (decided 2026-11-05; `aqua-ui::tree`, `aqua-runtime::runtime`, `aqua::render::lower`)

Evidence. Round 8 left 14.RE open: the scroll offset was subtracted from every child rectangle, so one wheel step changed every emitted item (574 to 816 in the fixture spike lines, `scene_ops_emitted == scene_items_patched`) and re-ran `emit_keyed` (per-node op counters, text run lookups, decoration and inline glyph items) plus a full scene reconcile.

Decisions. (1) `UiTree::emit_node` emits scroll content in content coordinates. A scroll node pushes its clip, then one `PushTransform(translate(-offset))` and pops it before the clip; children are not shifted. `world` for children includes the scroll translation, so culling, canvas bounds and visual rectangles stay in screen space. Canvas painters keep their old contract: a canvas inside a scroll is wrapped in the inverse translate, so its ops stay in scroll-adjusted coordinates, and every scroll above it is marked volatile (never patched). (2) Culling uses a stable overscan window per scroll node (`max(160, min(480, height / 2))` logical px on every side of the visible content rectangle). The window is kept while the visible rectangle stays inside it and recentred when it leaves, so the emitted set is constant between recentres. (3) `UiTree::scroll_to` classifies a move: inside the window of a non-volatile slot of the last emit it increments `scroll_patches` (and `content_version`, so other gates keep working) and leaves `scene_version() = content_version - scroll_patches` unchanged; anything else is an ordinary paint touch. (4) The runtime caches the scene by `scene_version`; a changed `scroll_revision` with an unchanged scene version calls `patch_scroll_offsets`, which rewrites only the `SceneKey::ScrollOffset { node }` items in `cached_scene` and the scene (one item per scroll node). The offset item is not counted in the node's `Tree` op indices and is excluded from motion slots, so the glide binding still targets the runtime's `ScrollContentStart` identity transform. (5) Runtime users of scene coordinates were changed to content coordinates: `text_rect` no longer subtracts scroll offsets, and `Edge` motion no longer adds a scroll shift (`scroll_shift` is deleted). (6) The lowering keeps a device-space clip stack (`Walk::clips`) and `Walk::hidden` now also rejects leaves outside the innermost clip (outside a blur or lifted scope, with the same reach padding), so overscan items cost no commands.

Tests. `aqua-runtime/tests/render_motion.rs`: a notch inside the window reuses the scene and reconciles exactly one item; six notches leave the window and re-emit; `edge_in_scroll.rs` states positions in content coordinates plus the scroll translate; `aqua/tests/render/lowering.rs`: leaves outside the active clip are not lowered. aqua-ui, aqua-runtime and aqua render suites pass on Linux.

Measured (Windows, release, fixture, scoreboard main plus spikes phases, one run, RX 9070 XT). After a 600 step scroll the thread renders correctly (screenshot read: text, code block, glass composer, shadows, scroll-to-bottom button). At 21 percent machine load: scroll p50 0.30, p90 0.44, p99 0.67, max below 2 ms, no frame over 4 ms; app frame work p50 0.66 to 0.69 ms, render thread work p50 0.85 to 1.1 ms (lower 0.32 to 0.51, plan 0.09 to 0.23, record 0.30 to 0.51, present 0.27 to 1.8). The round 8 start scoreboard had p50 0.21 and CPU per scroll input 8.18, now 8.67 in a run with another agent's load; the scoreboard CPU per scroll input spans `idle1` to `scroll1` and therefore includes the idle latency probe, the screenshot and the change probe, so it does not isolate the scroll work. Not met: scroll p50 at or below 0.1 ms and render work p50 under 0.4 ms. The remaining app cost is layout of remounted rows (`layout` 0.1 to 22 ms in spike frames, 19 of 22 spikes) and realization, which this change does not touch.

### 14.PB Round 7 private bytes (decided 2026-11-05; `aqua::images`, `aqua-core::arena`, `aqua-gpu-d3d12::scene::pipeline`, `aqua::mem_probe`)

(1) `WARM_LANES` is 1: four warm-up threads cost 18 to 21 MiB of driver private heap (11.6), more than their 60 to 140 ms of cold start is worth against a private budget that is already 37 MiB over; the cold start work can raise it again with its own measurement (`AQUA_WARM_LANES`). (2) Pipeline warm-up follows measured use: the used set is persisted inside `pipelines.cache` (frame `AQH1`, backward compatible with a bare library) and the next start warms `Quad` and that set only. (3) Decoded stills are retired after upload or 2 s unused and decoded again on demand; the encoded bytes are kept. (4) The 112 MiB private target is not reachable on D3D12 by anything in Aqua's control: the bare renderer is 110.5 MiB; the D3D11 adapter question (14.4) stays with the user (pending the user's decision).

### 14.ST Streaming rebuild path (decided 2026-11-05, round 7; `editor-aqua::conversation::view::factory`, `aqua-ui::element`, `aqua-ui::tree`, `aqua-text::layout`)

Evidence. On the fixture stream scenario every streamed prose row failed to build ("current live source is unavailable", 722 errors in one 24 s run, no screenshot ever showed the reply): the fixture streams by upserting a running `AssistantMessage` with a growing `Inline` text and no `Live` events, and the factory demanded a live binding for any running prose row. A failed build leaves the previous tree mounted and retries on the next frame, so the old streaming CPU numbers measured an error loop. The profiled success path (release, fixture, `AQUA_PROFILE=1`, per second at 31 events/s): `Tree::refresh` of the whole view 28 to 52 ms, layout 74 to 109 ms (three tree layouts and three collection realizations per frame), text layout 12 ms of which shaping 8 ms, 500 to 1000 nodes measured per frame.

Decisions. (1) A running prose row with `Inline` text and no live binding renders from the item text; with a binding it still requires the captured binding to match the row snapshot, and a running row with `Stored` text and no binding is still an error. (2) `Element::refresh_text` re-syncs the measure leaf only when its display text, font size, style or spans changed (`sync_text_leaf_if_changed`); it used to reset the leaf and mark layout dirty for every text node on every refresh, so each whole-view rebuild re-measured every label. (3) `UiTree::sync_children` returns early when the reconciled child list equals the retained one; it used to relink the taffy children and mark the parent and all its ancestors dirty on every call. (4) `aqua-text::TextLayout` keeps a placed-paragraph cache keyed by the shaped paragraph identity: line breaking, line placement and the glyph rows of a paragraph are reused while its wrap width, alignment, container width and span colours are unchanged, so streaming into the last paragraph only re-places that paragraph. (5) The rebuild spans (`rb_build`, `rb_refresh`, `rb_mounted`, `rb_total`, `conv_rebuild`, `f_realize`, `f_md_prepare`, `f_md_project`, `on_wake`, `on_fc`, `shell_drain`) are recorded by `shell::rebuild_stats` when `AQUA_REBUILD_STATS` or `AQUA_PROFILE=1` is set and printed once a second with the rebuild reasons.

Measured (release, fixture stream, six interleaved pairs, process CPU from `stream0` to `stream2`, noisy shared machine): with decision 1 and 4 only median 20.4 % of one core, with decisions 1 to 4 median 16.3 %; GPUI release in the same harness 11.3 to 12.5 %. In the profiled run the refresh span fell from 0.75 to 0.45 ms per rebuild, the layout span from 74 to 109 ms/s to 66 to 71 ms/s and the median app frame from 2.7 to 3.3 ms to 1.3 ms. The target (at or below GPUI) is not met.

Open. Each streamed token still remounts every block row of the streaming message (14 rows realized per event, three realizations and three tree layouts per frame) because the body proof, the row version and the fingerprint are per body, not per block; stable per-block versions need the projected proofs, the reveal frames and the painted-row acknowledgements to be rebased for unchanged blocks, which was not attempted. The render thread repaints at 55 to 62 Hz for the whole stream (`forced+repaint`, status shimmer reported as animating by `GpuFrames::repaint`), 60 to 100 ms/s of CPU that no app-side change removes.

### 14.CS Round 8 cold start (decided 2026-11-05; `aqua-gpu-d3d12::window::backend`, `aqua-gpu-d3d12::scene::pipeline`, `aqua-gpu-d3d12::scene::renderer`, `aqua::render::service`, `aqua::native::gpu`)

Evidence (release port, `AQUA_PROFILE=1` stage log, RX 9070 XT, shared machine at 20 to 98 percent system CPU, so wall times are inflated and only the proportions and the per-thread CPU column are reliable). The device is created on the early thread started by `prewarm_gpu`, concurrently with window creation and the first UI build; the render thread joins it. Critical path to the first present: `D3D12CreateDevice` 247 to 366 ms wall, 328 ms thread CPU (a quiet-machine probe measured 186 ms) then `renderer_new` 17 to 28 ms, then six pipeline loads 78 to 107 ms (Quad 16, Shadow 13, Glyph 11, Slug 21, Image 11, ImageReplace 7 ms), command list 3 ms, then 18 to 40 ms from device ready to the first present. Loading the 135 MiB `amdxc64.dll` and the 45 MiB `amdxx64.dll` costs 2.4 and 4.1 ms in a fresh process, so DLL preloading cannot hide anything: the 330 ms are inside the driver. After the first present the render thread used to block for 37 to 62 ms (`warm_layers_deferred` 3 to 30 ms, `amd_settle_deferred` 36 to 44 ms) before it could take input.

Decisions. (1) Post-first-present work is a queue of bounded steps, `StartupStep::{Pipeline, TrackerOpen, TrackerFrames, TrackerClose}`: one deferred pipeline per step and the 160 AMD tracker presents in chunks of 16 (14 to 18 steps, 49 to 68 ms in total, longest single step 8 to 11 ms measured, against one 40 to 71 ms block before). `WindowBackend::startup_pending` and `startup_step` are exposed through `FrameTarget`; the render service runs one step per turn only when no command, feedback, repaint or wake is due, so input and frames always go first, and it spins no further once the queue is empty (`render_service::startup_steps_run_one_per_idle_turn_without_presenting`). Device recovery drops the queue and the tracker surface (recovery rebuilds eagerly). The synchronous path (`WindowBackend::new`) is unchanged and `window::idle` runs the early path with chunked settle on every adapter: 0.0 busy ms per second on the RX 9070 XT, the integrated GPU and WARP. The 160-frame tracker fix of the AMD idle spin is kept as is. (2) The usage hint now records which pipelines were used before the first present (`+Label/format` entries, backward compatible with the earlier hint); the early path loads Quad plus that set before the first frame and warms the rest from the queue. Measured result: on the Editor landing screen every one of the six format-91 pipelines is used by the first frame, so the first-frame set equals the old hinted set and the hint saves nothing there; it only helps apps whose first frame is simpler. The hint therefore cannot restore the 60 to 140 ms of 14.PB (1). (3) `CustomPipelines` (a runtime D3DCompile of the custom vertex shader, 4.7 ms) and `ComputePipelines` (2.9 ms) are created on first use instead of in `SceneRenderer::with_options`. (4) `WARM_LANES` stays 1. A second warm lane (`AQUA_WARM_LANES=2`) cut the warm stage by about a third but added 3 to 4 MiB private bytes in five interleaved pairs (122/126, 123/126, 122/126, 122/125, 122/126 MiB at the end of startup); four lanes added about 8 MiB. Private bytes already lose on D3D12 by API cost, so the lane stays off; it is one constant if the user prefers startup over 4 MiB. (5) Not done: lowering and planning the first frame while the device is still being created (about 15 to 25 ms, needs `GpuRenderHost` to split lowering from the backend); starting `prewarm_gpu` before `handle_launch` in the port (under 10 ms).

Result. With the vendor device creation at 190 to 330 ms, the pipeline loads at 80 to 100 ms and the process start at 45 ms, the first presented frame cannot come under 300 ms on D3D12 on this machine without more driver threads (rejected by 14.PB) or a different API; first content measured 680 to 960 ms by the PrintWindow probe on a loaded machine, so no scoreboard row is claimed. The gain of this record is time to interactive after the first frame: the post-present stall fell from 40 to 71 ms to at most 11 ms per turn.

D3D11 against D3D12, for the decision in 14.4. Floors from the bare probe (RX 9070 XT, 1898x1125, two-buffer FLIP_DISCARD BGRA8, medians of 5) and Aqua's measured costs:

| Cost | D3D11 | D3D12 |
|---|---|---|
| GPU local memory with a running swapchain | 28.9 MiB | 28.9 MiB (identical) |
| Private bytes of the bare API | 29.8 MiB | 88.9 MiB (about 16 at device creation, about 40 on the first submit and present) |
| Device creation | 44 ms | 186 ms (247 to 366 ms under load in the app) |
| First present | 112 ms | 256 ms |
| Pipeline objects at startup | compiled shaders only, no driver pipeline link | six pipeline loads, 78 to 107 ms; each extra warm lane costs about 4 MiB |
| Aqua above the GPU floor | not built | about 36 MiB, all attributable and cuttable (swapchain, RGBA16F intermediates, atlases, descriptor heaps, transients, upload rings, tracker swapchain) |
| Aqua above the private floor | not built | about 60 MiB, of which about 21 MiB live Rust heap |

GPU dedicated memory is fixable on D3D12. Private bytes and cold start lose on D3D12 by API cost alone (59 MiB and 142 ms of the floors); a D3D11 adapter is not built and remains the user's decision.

### 14.CS2 Round 10 cold start on D3D11 (decided 2026-11-05; `aqua::native`, `aqua-gpu-d3d11::stages`, `aqua-shell-win32::window`, port `core/store.rs` and `shell/application.rs`)

Method. Every startup mark is now recorded in memory with the process age and printed once, sorted, at the first present (`AQUA_PROFILE=1`, `native::startup_mark`, `aqua_gpu_d3d11::stages::drain`). The old marks printed to stderr while reading `private_mib`, which cost 3.7 ms per mark and inflated every stage by up to 30 ms; all earlier startup stage tables on this machine overstate the stages. Bench: private Forge fixture on port 47978, release exes copied once, interleaved runs of GPUI, the round-9 port and the new port, medians of 8, machine shared with other agents (load average 3 to 5), so only differences larger than about 15 ms are claimed.

Timeline of the round-9 port on D3D11 (process age, ms): `main` 17; runtime ready 24 to 29 (system fonts 6); notifications 12.5; window created 14.5 of which 15.5 was the drop target (`OleInitialize`, `CoCreateInstance(DragDropHelper)`, `RegisterDragDrop`); window show 10; app started 77; device creation thread: factory 11 to 17, `D3D11CreateDevice` 52, renderer and shaders 5; device ready 84 to 100; first Present 16 to 28 (the driver compiles pipeline state at the first flush: a diagnostic flush before `Present1` took 27 ms and left 1.4 ms for the present); first present 108 to 165.

Decisions and measurements.
1. **The Forge dial no longer waits for `ae account status`** (port, `Core::reconcile`, `Core::dial`). `Account::reading_local_forge` blocked every dial until the `ae` subprocess returned (`ae account status` takes 245 to 265 ms here and prints "Looking for Forge in WSL"). The thread open, and with it the connection, started 340 ms after launch. An onboarded machine with a saved Local or Wsl pairing in `hosts.json` is now dialed directly while the read is pending; without a saved pairing nothing is dialed until the binding arrives, as before. Cold start to thread painted, 10 interleaved runs, medians: round-9 port 422 ms (373 to 458), new port 190 ms (175 to 216), GPUI 490 ms (459 to 565); window created at 56 ms against 86 (round-9 port) and 147 (GPUI). Test: `tests/core/dial.rs` blocks the scripted `ae` forever and requires the host to exist.
2. **Notification center and file drop leave the startup chain** (`start_notifications` after the first frame completes or when an effect needs it; `Window::enable_file_drop` run by the host on an idle tick after the first present, `FILE_DROP_IDLE` 30 ms). `app_ready` moved from 43 to 25 ms and window creation from 18 to about 3 ms. `Window::create` no longer registers a drop target; the shell test calls `enable_file_drop`.
