3. **First frame richness.** With decision 1 the first presented frame already contains the thread rows (non-dominant pixel fraction 0.165 against GPUI 0.186; the round-9 port presented a chrome-only frame at 0.087 and showed the thread 230 ms later). The probe therefore reports a second figure, `rich_content_ms` (first screenshot with at least 15 percent non-dominant pixels, the scoreboard row "cold start to a frame with 15% non-dominant pixels"), next to `first_content_ms` (6 percent). Medians of 10: GPUI 219 (6 percent) and 220 (15 percent); round-9 port 182 for its lighter frame and 431 for the same richness; new port 204 and 204. The new port is 7 percent ahead of GPUI on both figures; its earlier 182 to 190 ms came from showing less. A screenshot after the thread opened shows the same glass, history footer, code card and checklist as GPUI.
4. **Measured and rejected.** (a) Preloading the vendor driver (`LoadLibraryW` of `amdxx64.dll` on a helper thread at `main`): the load takes 1.2 ms on a warm cache and `D3D11CreateDevice` stayed at 52 ms, so the cost is driver initialisation, not file access; a cold-cache gain cannot be measured here without evicting the standby list, so no prefetch code was added. (b) Pipeline warm-up draws on the early device thread (five kinds, 8x8 target, flush): the first Present fell from 28 to 10 ms but the early thread grew by 32 ms; first present medians 223 against 238 ms in one A/B and first content 219 against 206 ms in another, so the effect is below the noise and the code was removed. (c) Delaying the thread open until after the first frame to move the pipeline compile off the critical path: the first app frame is produced by `draw_ready` after the inbox is pumped, so a wake-based release opens the thread before the first frame; a correct version needs an `Application` hook after frame submission and was not built because the render thread is idle for only 16 ms in that window. (d) `SINGLETHREADED`, a private heap and Segment Heap remain rejected (11.5).

Remaining cost on the first rich frame (loaded machine, process age): device ready 90 to 100, first scene with the thread rows ready 95 to 190 (scene build 28 to 100 ms for 49 realized rows, owned by the open-thread work), render lowering 5, first Present 25 to 30 (driver pipeline compile). The first `Present` cost belongs to the device and is the next lever once the scene build is under 50 ms. Not done: system font discovery at 6 ms in `Aqua::new` (parallel start would need the text services to accept a late manager), delay-loading `uiautomationcore`, `d3dcompiler_47` and `mmdevapi` (static imports of the exe; no measurement of their load share), a smaller exe.

### 14.GC Colour space and blending follow the GPUI Editor (decided 2026-11-05; `aqua-core::color`, `aqua-graph::tone`, `aqua-shaders`, `aqua-gpu-ref`, `aqua-gpu-d3d11`, `aqua-gpu-d3d12`, `aqua-transport::gpu_host`, `aqua::render::lower`, `aqua-media::vector`)

Decision (the user's): the GPUI Editor is the visual reference, so Aqua adopts GPUI's colour model exactly (section 6.8). Aqua blended in linear light on an sRGB render-target view; GPUI blends sRGB-encoded values on a plain UNORM target. Translucent white over near-black therefore came out much lighter in Aqua: the composer glass read 62 against GPUI's 30.

Changes:
- `Oklch::to_gpu` is premultiplied sRGB-encoded; `Oklch::to_linear` became `to_srgba` (straight encoded). The quad shader's Oklab gradient clamps linear sRGB and encodes before premultiplying, and the oracle does the same through `to_gpu`.
- Swapchains on both adapters create, view and read back `B8G8R8A8_UNORM` (`hal::RENDER_FORMAT`; `BACK_BUFFER_FORMAT` is gone). `SurfaceFormat::Bgra8UnormSrgb`, the typeless offscreen resource and `bgra8_srgb_rows_to_linear_rgba8` are removed, and readback is no longer decoded.
- Text: `ToneCurve::linear_blend_alpha` and `linear_blend_alpha_over` are removed; the shaders apply `tone_alpha` (the GPUI correction) and blend in encoded space. The tone is per frame, `FramePlan::text_tone`, set by the lowering from the window's `TextRasterMode` and carried through `aqua_transport::Lowered::text_tone` into the plan. The backdrop machinery of 14.O is removed with it: `GlyphInstance::backdrop`, `SlugInstance::backdrop`, `paint::Glyph::backdrop`, the `EXTRA` glyph input, the host's memory of opaque fills and the clear luminance, and two words of the text run cache signature.
- Images, emoji and vector rasters: atlas data is premultiplied encoded RGBA8. Raster images and colour glyphs already arrived that way and were sampled as if linear (washed out); SVG and vector rasters no longer convert to linear. The unused `DecodedImage::to_premultiplied_linear_*` converters and `f32_to_f16_bits` are deleted. Snapshot-transition tints are straight encoded.
- Conformance: the sRGB surface variants are dropped, the cases that tested known backdrops became `slug_on_midtone`, `glyph_atlas_on_midtone` and their `exact_tone` twins, the Oklab gradient analytic test now expects encoded values, and the new case `gpui_translucent_white_over_near_black` plus the contract check `translucent_white_matches_gpui` pin white at 0.05 and 0.20 over `#111111` to GPUI's 8-bit straight blend (29 and 65) within 2. Tolerances are unchanged.

Superseded: 14.14 in full, the backdrop half of 14.O, and the composition-surface limit of 14.8 round 3 item (3): a composition swapchain now receives the encoded premultiplied pixels DWM expects.

Verified on Windows, 2026-11-05 (RX 9070 XT, the 2-CU iGPU, WARP): D3D11 conformance passes all 6 variants (float and BGRA8 on each adapter), 145 cases and 11 contract checks, worst interior 1, soft 4, edge 4; D3D12 conformance passes all 4 variants with the same worst errors; D3D11 `tests/window` 58 of 58, `differential`, `debug_layer`, `heap`, `half`, `sprite`, `lifetimes`, `ledger` pass. Release Editor port against the release GPUI Editor on the bench fixture thread (PrintWindow, physical pixels, 5x5 means):

| Point | GPUI | Aqua before | Aqua after |
|---|---|---|---|
| Composer glass interior | 30 | 62 | 30 |
| Composer glass top sheen | 40 | 85 | 40 |
| Composer tab chip | 30 | 49 | 30 |
| Code card | 30 | 63 | 30 |
| Code card copy button | 37 | 79 | 37 |
| Hovered sidebar row | 38 | hover not drawn | 38 |
| Send button | 28, 59, 134 | 48, 77, 179 | 28, 59, 134 |
| Opaque backgrounds (title bar, sidebar, transcript) | 9, 17, 17 | 9, 17, 17 | 9, 17, 17 |

Text ink (sum of coverage above the background, GPUI = 1.00) was 1.08 to 1.15 before because the port drew Outline text with the native curve; after the change it is 0.99 to 1.00 for the code block, breadcrumb, sidebar titles, panel labels and composer placeholder. Emoji now carry GPUI's saturation (heart 247, 63, 95 against GPUI 244, 48, 46; before 252, 139, 158); the remaining difference is glyph design (Aqua draws the COLRv1 gradients of Segoe UI Emoji, GPUI's DirectWrite path the flat layers).

Not reproduced: GPUI's sRGB gradient interpolation mode (unused by both editors), its half-code gradient dither, and its ClearType subpixel text for `PlatformDefault` (Aqua is grayscale by 14.1).

### 14.IW Idle wakes (decided 2026-11-05, round 8; `aqua-shell-win32::occlusion`, `aqua-shell-win32::wake_trace`, `aqua-shell-win32::looping`)

Measured first, with the shell-side wake ledger (`AQUA_WAKE_STATS=1`: `aqua-shell-wakes` once per second, silent when nothing wakes): every message the loop retrieves, every message a window procedure receives (`sent:` when it arrives from another thread), every message that reaches any window of the UI thread through a `WH_CALLWNDPROC` hook (`hook:<class>:<message>`), and the result of every `MsgWaitForMultipleObjectsEx` (timer, waitable, input available with the pending `QS_` bits from `GetQueueStatus`). The pure formatting is `wake_trace::Tally` (`tests/wake_trace.rs`).

- **Main thread, 5.3 to 11 context switches/s with an empty `aqua-wakes` log.** The loop turned over only on `loop:queue-send`: sent messages that reach no window procedure. The cause was `occlusion::watch`: one out-of-context `SetWinEventHook` over the whole range `EVENT_SYSTEM_FOREGROUND..EVENT_OBJECT_UNCLOAKED` for all processes, installed at window creation and never removed. That range contains every accessibility event any process raises (name, value, state and selection changes, reorders), so the UI thread woke at the rate of unrelated desktop traffic, 100 to 400 per second while another application was busy (collections example, quiet machine 5 to 11/s, loaded machine 100 to 1100/s). WARP did not change it (15/s), the AMD driver thread is not the sender. Fix: `occlusion::Watch`, a pure state machine with `Level::Off | Narrow | Full`. Activity (a queued event or a due deadline) installs the `Full` set: system-wide foreground, minimize start and end, destroy, show and hide, cloak and uncloak, as five narrow ranges instead of one wide one. Location changes are not watched at any level; while the set is `Full` and the loop is active, `Watch::refresh_due` re-derives coverage at most every 250 ms on turns that happen anyway (a watched location event is the flood: 100 to 470 wakes/s during any other process's activity, found by A/B with the hooks disabled, 13 main-thread switches/s against 235 to 850). After 1.5 s without activity the hooks are removed (`Off`) when no window is covered or minimized, and reduced to `Narrow` when one is: system-wide foreground and minimize events plus destroy, hide and cloak events scoped by process id to the processes that own the windows above ours (`occluder_processes`, rebuilt after every recomputation). `looping::watch_occlusion` steps the machine at the start and the end of every `turn`, and `run` folds `Watch::next_check` into the timer so the removal is one scheduled wake per idle episode. Coverage is recomputed (`DIRTY`) whenever hooks are installed, before the queued events are handled, so the first input after idle sees fresh coverage. Rejected: skipping hooks for covered windows (a covered window whose last frames were skipped would stay stale after the occluder closes), `WINEVENT_SKIPOWNPROCESS` (a second window of the same application can be the occluder). Known limit: an occluder that is moved away while the window is covered and idle is noticed on the next input or frame request, not when it moves. Measured on the Editor port (release, D3D11 default build, open `bench-large` thread, `idleattr`, 15 s window after 10 s idle, whole-process context switches/s from the harness's per-thread counters): 0.1 (max 1) in two consecutive runs; the landing screen (`idleland`) 0.1 (max 1); a passive 70 s trace (`AQUA_WAKE_STATS=1`, no probe steps) shows 0 `aqua-wakes` lines and 0.1 to 0.6 shell wakes/s after startup. Before the change, same harness on the D3D12 build: main thread 5.3/s in every run (Editor port) and 5 to 12/s (collections), plus 100 to 1100/s whenever another process was busy. On the collections example (D3D12, quiet machine) the main thread went from 5.3, 11.1 and 12.0 to 1.4 and 1.5 (one loaded run 12). The `wakes` and `threads` probe steps themselves wake the target (`hook:AquaShell32:geticon` appears only while they run), which is why scoreboard rows taken during probes read higher than the passive trace. `tests/occlusion_watch.rs` covers the state machine and the refresh rate.
- **Landing screen and open thread.** The 163 renders/s `forced+repaint` loop seen on the landing screen in round 7 does not reproduce on the current tree (`idleland`, 15 s window after 10 s: no `aqua-render-runs` and no `aqua-wakes` line after startup); the open `bench-large` thread passes `tools/idle-gate.sh` (0 lines in 10 s). Wakes after startup on both screens come from the harness (`threads`, `sample` and WMI queries post `WM_GETICON` and friends: the `hook:AquaShell32:geticon` lines appear exactly when those steps run) and from the Forge reconnect (`messages@forge-net`, about 0.4/s averaged over a window that includes startup).
- **The AMD `amdxc64` thread (5.4 context switches/s, 0 ms CPU).** Attributed on the RX 9070 XT with a bare D3D12 probe (device, 1898x1125 flip swapchain, 200 clear-only presents, 60 s idle): no such thread. In Aqua it is created at the first frame that draws and its start time equals the first content time (collections: thread 1138 ms, first content 1147 ms; Editor port: thread 417 to 480 ms, first content 476 to 534 ms). With the settle warm-up disabled the same thread spins (1079 ms CPU/s, 116 ctx/s); with 160 or 3000 warm-up presents it settles at 5.4/s and stays there for the life of the process, so it is the Radeon Anti-Lag frame tracker after retirement, not a separate heartbeat, and more warm-up frames do not remove it. `AQUA_TIER=baseline` and one PSO compile lane do not change it. The swapchain already uses the frame-latency waitable. It cannot be avoided from the application on D3D12; it does not exist on the D3D11 path (`amdxx64.dll` shows 0.3/s).

### 14.IQ Round 9 idle attribution and quiet wake paths (decided 2026-11-05; `aqua-shell-win32::looping`, `aqua-shell-win32::occlusion`, `aqua-shell-win32::timing`, `aqua-shell-win32::wake_trace`, `tools/win-drive.ps1`)

Evidence first. On a machine at 5 to 20 percent load the D3D11 default idles at 0.1 switches/s and 0.00 percent CPU (`idle threads waking` 0); the 629 switches/s of the round-8 start scoreboard no longer reproduce. ETW ReadyThread stacks were not possible: `wpr.exe` and kernel logger sessions need elevation and the session is not elevated, so attribution is in-process.

- **Probe fix.** `probe-wakes` read `Win32_PerfRawData_PerfProc_Thread` and `Get-Counter`, which return "data in one of the performance counter samples is not valid" whenever a thread exits during the window, so the aqua idle rows came out `n/a`. `win-drive.ps1` now reads per-thread `ContextSwitches` and CPU from `NtQuerySystemInformation(SystemProcessInformation)` (one buffer per second, no WMI, nothing posted to the app) and prints a per-second series (`probe-ctxseries`, with `start_unix_ms`, process hard faults and page faults) and the top threads by name. The `threads` probe resolves names and modules after the window, not inside it.
- **Burst source.** The remaining main-thread cost was single seconds of 200 to 430 context switches with no loop wake behind them (`AQUA_WAKE_STATS=burst`: counters only, no `WH_CALLWNDPROC` hook, a line only for seconds with 30 or more wakes or a step slower than 2 ms, each line stamped `unix_ms` for alignment with `probe-ctxseries`). They coincided with `slow:pump:5..9ms` after an `EVENT_SYSTEM_FOREGROUND` from another process: `occlusion::pump` re-created every narrowed hook and walked the z-order calling `DwmGetWindowAttribute` for every window above the target, hidden ones included (`slow:occluders:12ms` in one run). Fixes: hooks are reconciled against the desired set (`occlusion::reconcile`, only stale keys unhooked and only missing keys hooked) and the narrowed occluder hooks filter by thread as well as process; coverage and occluder discovery test visibility, style and a `GetWindowRect` overlap first and only then ask DWM. After the change three burst-mode runs and the 3-run gated scoreboard show no idle second above 5 switches.
- **One wake signal.** `Waker::wake` posts `WM_WAKE` only when none is pending (`AtomicBool`, cleared when the message is dispatched and when a window is destroyed); a thousand wakes before the loop turns deliver one `woken` (`tests/wake_dedup.rs`). The drop-target path uses the same latch.
- **Timer leeway.** `timing::plan` keeps deadlines within 100 ms on the high-resolution timer and sends farther ones to a second, ordinary waitable timer with a tolerable delay of a tenth of the distance, at most 250 ms (`SetWaitableTimerEx`), so the kernel can coalesce them with other wakes.
- **What is left at zero.** `idle busiest thread` ties GPUI at 0.10/s (`forge-net` heartbeat on both sides, an application thread); the gate demands a win or a tie at zero. `slow:` labels remain available in burst mode for future attribution.

### 14.OT Large-thread open and cold shaping (decided 2026-11-05, round 8; `aqua-text::font`, `aqua-text::shape`, `editor-aqua::conversation::view::transcript`)
- **Measured critical path (fixture `bench-large`, release, `AQUA_OPEN_TIMING=1`, private fixture Forge, quiet machine).** The read acknowledgement is consumed in `frame_completed`, which the host dispatches on `RenderEvent::Presented`. Two frames are on the path: the first realization of the visible rows (scene build 18 ms of which 7.7 ms realizing 49 rows) and a second frame after the reveal sync (`sync_contents` changes the visible height and text opacity of each freshly mounted row, so the row is repainted before it is `ready`). Typical internal timeline: page applied 13 ms, first content scene 43 ms, Presented 47 ms, second scene 72 ms, Presented 81 ms, acknowledgement 85 to 93 ms. About 26 ms of that is scheduler gaps between the frames and 4 to 10 ms is render-thread time per frame. The probe `since_click` also contains 140 ms of harness (`click` posts move, down and up with 80 ms and 60 ms sleeps) and about 40 ms of Forge round trip, so the internal acknowledgement time is what the Editor controls.
- **Lazy tail layout.** The transcript list mounts rows up to 1600 px beyond the viewport on both sides. While `Conversation::landing` is set (from `open_thread` until `landed()` or the 1.5 s expiry; the thread view is invisible in that time) the overscan is 320 px, then it grows to 1600 px when landing ends. `AQUA_LANDING_OVERSCAN_PX` overrides the landing value for A/B runs. Measured, same executable, interleaved, 4 runs each, internal acknowledgement and probe `since_click`: landing overscan 1600 px gives 125, 141 and 128 ms and 318, 324 and 314 ms; 320 px gives 90, 93, 83 and 108 ms and 278, 278, 259 and 320 ms (medians 127 to 92 ms and 316 to 278 ms). 120 px and 0 px give 88 to 105 ms, no further gain, so 320 px stays. The extra rows are realized on the first frame after landing, outside the acknowledgement path.
- **Read-evidence semantics.** The acknowledgement still requires, for the thread's boundary item, a text row that is inside the transcript clip, `revealed`, whose body is prepared against the current item sequence, whose row version still matches, and whose reveal state is `ready`, observed on a presented frame. Only the visible boundary row gates it: neither overscan rows, nor projections of off-screen bodies, nor `Conversation::landed()` take part (`read::evidence` and `read::capture` never look at other rows; `landed()` uses the visible rows only). That is the weakest evidence that still means "the user can read the newest content": GPUI acknowledges at paint of the same row. Rejected: acknowledging at scene build without a Present (it would report content as read that a lost frame never showed) and acknowledging before the reveal sync (the row can still change size).
- **Shape plans.** `FontEntry` keeps `Arc<harfrust::ShapePlan>` per (direction, script, feature set) and `shape_run` passes it through `ShapeOptions::plan` instead of letting HarfRust compile a plan per run; a buffer with a language (never set today) bypasses the cache. Reuse is limited to 64 plans per face. Measured with a counting allocator (`aqua-text/tests/shape_plan.rs`, 38 distinct one-line paragraphs after the first, debug build): 83 allocations and 57 KB per layout before, 67 and 46 KB after. The earlier figure of about 1000 allocations and 0.7 to 1 MB per run is not in `aqua-text`: shaping a run costs 50 to 80 allocations once the face is warm, and the rest is the UI layer (markdown presenter and text node preparation), outside this record.
- **Cold shaping.** `PrewarmJob::build` (the `aqua-prewarm` thread started at launch) now also shapes a pangram with the face's default plan, which compiles the plan and touches the GSUB, GPOS and cmap pages before the first layout. First layout of the default sans style: 164 allocations and 76 KB cold, 52 and 18 KB after prewarm (test `prewarmed_entries_pay_no_plan_cost_on_first_layout`); `tests/shape_plan.rs::reused_shape_plans_leave_glyph_positions_unchanged` checks that cached plans give bit-identical glyph positions.
- **Not done.** (1) A single-frame acknowledgement needs the reveal sync to happen at row preparation instead of after layout (`ui::markdown::reveal`, `conversation::view::reveal`), which would remove one Present round trip, about 35 ms. (2) Frame scheduling gaps of about 26 ms between the first and second content frame belong to the host scheduler. (3) With a loaded machine the render thread plan phase measured 22 to 26 ms per frame in one run (0.3 ms in the post-round-6 run), which dominates every Present-gated step; it is outside this record. The probe `since_click` target of 220 ms is not met: 259 to 320 ms against GPUI 245 in the doc; the floor from the harness is about 180 ms, so the internal acknowledgement would need to fall below 60 ms.

### 14.AR Animated repaint cost during streaming (decided 2026-11-05, round 8; `aqua::render::host`, `aqua::render::damage`, `aqua::render::lower`, `aqua::render::cull`, `aqua::render::service`, `aqua-scene::footprint`, `aqua-motion::cycle`, `tools/compare-editors.sh`)

Evidence (release, Windows, RX 9070 XT, fixture `bench-stream`, 24 s of streaming, spike recorder at threshold 0 through the new aqua-only phase `AQUA_COMPARE_PHASES=streamprof` of `compare-editors.sh`, which runs the stream scenario with `AQUA_STATS_OVERRIDE=profilewakes`: `AQUA_PROFILE` and the wake ledger together; machine shared with other agents, so numbers are same-session). Before: the render thread ran 86 to 97 loop turns and about 65 presented frames per second, 55 of them animation repaints (`forced+repaint`), at 62 to 74 ms of CPU per second. Every animated frame lowered 515 to 527 commands in 50 to 55 passes (lower 0.16, plan 0.18, record 0.13, present 0.37 ms) because every animated frame planned as `Plan::Full`. Per-frame notes (`plan=`, `cull=`, `changed=`, `walk_kinds=`) identified four causes:

1. `diff` returned `Plan::Full` whenever the damage touched a backdrop blur zone. The composer glass is within reach of the status line, so the pulse and shimmer rect forced a full frame every 15 ms.
2. Text scope push and pop items (`PushGpuiTextColor`, `PushGpuiTextOpacity`, `PushGpuiSourceTextColor`, text colour maps, shimmer, quad pulse) were `Kind::Structural` in `aqua-scene::footprint`, so every streaming update that recreated one invalidated the whole frame. The `Full` entered the swapchain damage history, so the animation frame that followed had no cull region either (`cull=None` in 90 percent of the forced frames).
3. The shimmer sweep keeps repainting at 15 ms during its 1.5 s delay although the phase is constant, and the lowering walk emitted text, svg and path items that lie outside the repaint region (text 2.6 to 4.5 us per item, svg 1.2 us).
4. An animation frame that follows a scroll glide frame repaints the union of both damages (two back buffers), so its cost is that of the glide frame, at 15 ms cadence, in between content frames that arrive every 33 ms.

Decisions. (1) Blur zones widen the damage instead of forcing `Full`. A zone is the blur bounds grown by `4 * radius + 16` px; every zone that intersects the damage is added to it, repeatedly until no further zone joins, and the plan is `Full` only when the widened bounding box exceeds 75 percent of the frame. The backdrop blur then reads freshly drawn content over its whole reach, which is why the whole zone and not only the changed pixels is repainted. `crates/aqua-gpu-d3d12/tests/window/scissor.rs::{hardware,warp}_repaint_across_a_backdrop_blur_matches_the_full_reference` repaints 17 frames through a blur with `[PATCH, zone]` and compares the whole surface with `aqua-gpu-ref`; `crates/aqua/tests/damage.rs` checks the planner side and a differential run of the scene diff around a blur (seeds 21 to 27, without 24, see Open). (2) The text scope and pulse items are group items in `aqua-scene::footprint`, so a changed scope damages the extent of what it wraps (new and old) instead of the frame (`restyling_a_text_scope_damages_what_it_wraps_and_not_the_whole_frame`). (3) The planner keeps `SceneTraits` per scene revision and, when the revision, viewport and the lowering's `pending` count are unchanged since the committed plan, plans from the animated rects only (about 3 us instead of 20 to 90 us). (4) `Lowering` culls while it walks: boxes, shadows, text, images, svg and paths whose device bounds (plus shadow reach, text overhang and svg rotation slack) fall outside the repaint region are not emitted; fill memory, pulse state and pending counts are still updated, and the cached glyph pages of a skipped run are touched so idle trim does not evict them. Nothing is skipped inside an open blur layer, under a lifted transform, or while a transition is pending. `cull::cull` then removes clip and opacity scopes left empty. `culling_while_walking_matches_culling_the_finished_commands` proves the walk emits what culling the finished list would have. (5) `Cycle::holds_for` and `ShimmerClocks::hold` report how long every active sweep stays at its starting phase; `LowerReport::still_micros` carries the minimum, and `Repaint::Still(d)` makes the render service wait `d` instead of repainting every 15 ms (visually identical, the phase does not change; only possible when no quad pulse is active). (6) `Repaint::Heavy`: when the repaint region of the last frame covers more than 20 percent of the frame, the next animation tick is scheduled 33 ms after it instead of 15 ms, so it coalesces with the content frame that is arriving anyway. This is a visible change: the sweep advances at about 30 Hz instead of 60 Hz while a large repaint is in progress (computed from `color::shimmer`: a glyph colour changes by about 11 instead of 5.5 levels per step at the status shimmer's 3 s duration and 50 percent spread); it applies only while content is already changing at that rate.

Measured after (same harness, release; two runs, the second after the final cleanup): streaming render thread 62 to 74 down to 31 and 34 ms of CPU per second (target below 40), 65 down to 35 presented frames per second, animation repaints from 55 to about 3 per second. An animated frame with a small repaint region (the pulse or shimmer alone) lowers 82 commands in 3 passes: lower 0.06, plan 0.01, record 0.03 ms, 0.10 ms outside Present1 (target 0.3), present 0.25 ms. The remaining render-thread cost is the content frames themselves: the conversation follows the stream with a scroll glide, so every content frame repaints the whole 1202 by 1030 px viewport (440 commands, 50 passes, lower 0.13, plan 0.12, record 0.22, present 0.23 ms, 33 per second); an animation frame right after one repaints the same union and costs the same, which (6) now makes rare (75 of 840 frames).

Open. (1) Content frames during the glide are inherently full-viewport; cutting them needs fewer glide steps or a scroll blit in the app or the backend, not planner work. (2) `walk_us` is 45 to 75 us for 310 items with most culled, dominated by `GpuiBackground` (16 items, 6 to 8 us) and the scope items (190, 7 us); skipping whole groups with the extents `Footprints` already computes would remove them but needs the lowering to see node ids and a skip table. (3) The differential run of seed 24 (no blur) misses a one pixel row at x 134 to 138 (`DIFFBOX 134,70`), also with plan reuse switched off, so it predates this record; seeds 21 to 23 and 25 to 27 pass. (4) Occlusion was ruled out as the cause of the missing cull region: `WindowStats.occluded` is 0 in the fixture runs, so `DamageTracker::reset` on `PresentStatus::Occluded` is untouched. (5) Two instrumentation additions remain under `AQUA_PROFILE` only: the spike notes `damage_us`, `plan=`, `cull=`, `changed=`, `walk_kinds=` and `window=`.


### 14.DH D3D11 host feature parity (decided 2026-11-05, round 9; `aqua-gpu-d3d11::window`, `aqua-gpu-d3d11::hal`, `aqua::native::gpu`)

Audit of the D3D12 host features against the D3D11 path, each verified on the RX 9070 XT, the integrated adapter and WARP by the `aqua-gpu-d3d11` window suite (58 of 58 pass, three consecutive full runs after the change; one earlier run failed `composition::hardware_mica_alt_composes_premultiplied_alpha` and passed in isolation and in the two reruns, a desktop-composition timing flake), conformance (6), differential (5) and debug layer (3).

Already present and covered by tests: composition surfaces for transparent windows (`composition`, premultiplied alpha, Mica, Mica Alt, Acrylic, resize and device loss on a composition surface), buffer-age damage and present rectangles with scissored repaint (`damage`, `scissor`), trim and visibility-driven release (`memory`), device-lost recovery with the hardware, reselect and WARP chain and texture replay or reupload (`backend`, `recovery_textures`, `shared_device`), early device on a helper thread with chunked startup (`startup`, `idle`), vblank pacing (`pacing`, `refresh`).

AMD frame-tracker settle: not ported. The D3D12 spin was measured as an `amdxc64.dll` thread polling after the third presented frame. On D3D11 the idle test presents 10 frames plus three resizes of 5 frames and then measures every non-main thread for 3 s on every adapter; it passes the 50 ms/s limit, and the probe reports 0.00 ms/s process CPU in the same state. No 160-frame warm-up swapchain is created on D3D11, so it adds nothing to cold start.

Pipeline persistence: shaders are build-time DXBC (`aqua-shaders`), so there is no compile cost to cache. The persisted blob is the used-label hint (`scene::hint`); the probe reports `compiled: 11, loaded: 0, late: 2` on a cold start and the hint drives the early prewarm. Nothing more to persist.

`D3D11_CREATE_DEVICE_SINGLETHREADED`: implemented as `DeviceOptions::single_threaded` (`AQUA_D3D11_SINGLETHREADED=1`), default off. The early device is built on a helper thread and joined by the render thread, which is the only user afterwards, so the flag is legal. Measured with the probe (600 frames at 1898x1125, release, three alternating runs each): frame CPU 0.60, 0.55, 0.60 ms with the flag against 0.47, 0.52, 0.60 ms without; device creation 72 to 101 ms against 64 to 75 ms; private bytes 39.7 to 39.9 MiB in both. No gain on this driver within noise, so it stays opt-in.

Round 10 re-measurement on the Editor (release port exe, bench fixture on a private Forge, phases main and stream, `AQUA_D3D11_SINGLETHREADED` passed through `WSLENV`, one run per variant per round, order alternated; the machine was shared with other agents' builds and scoreboards at 20 to 28% load, so a third flag-off run aborted on the quiet-machine rule and the comparison is 2 runs off against 3 on). Scroll p50 0.12 and 0.15 ms off, 0.14, 0.10 and 0.18 on; p99 0.62 and 0.69 off, 0.90, 0.70 and 0.81 on; max 0.81 and 0.95 off, 1.41, 1.05 and 1.38 on. Cold start to first content 203 and 197 ms off, 193, 200 and 184 on. Streaming CPU 9.5 and 13.2% off, 9.0 and 9.8% on; streaming p99 0.63 and 0.74 ms off, 0.45 and 0.38 on. Private bytes and GPU dedicated are bimodal in both arms (GPU dedicated 45.9 or 53.9 MiB, private 116.5 to 126.2 MiB after startup) and the mode does not follow the flag (off 45.9 and 53.9, on 53.9, 53.9 and 45.9), so the flag has no memory effect; the 8 MiB step is the persistent-texture creation bump recorded in 14.GD. The flag helps streaming latency slightly and worsens the scroll tail slightly, both inside the run-to-run spread, so the default stays off. Round 10 sweeps of `host_full`, `host_counters`, `render_allocations`, `host_replay` and `motion_edges` (release, D3D11, Windows, `--test-threads=1`): three of three clean sweeps on the tree at 17:2x, then, after other packages landed, `host_full`, `host_counters`, `host_replay` and `motion_edges` clean three of three again while `render_allocations` (`glide_frames_on_the_render_side_do_not_allocate`) fails three of three with one heap event on the worst glide frame, a regression from the uncommitted render-side edits of that round (not from the host-feature work), open until its owner fixes it. The measurement is not repeated on a quiet machine; a gain would have to be larger than this spread to justify the change.

### 14.GD D3D11 dedicated GPU memory: meters, ledger, 8-bit intermediates, idle release (decided 2026-11-05, round 9; `aqua-gpu-d3d11::lifetimes`, `aqua-gpu-d3d11::scene::transients`, `aqua::render::host`, `aqua::native::gpu`)

Which meter gates. `compare-editors.sh` gates the per-process counter `\GPU Process Memory(pid_N*)\Dedicated Usage` (`gpumem` step of `win-drive.ps1`). `IDXGIAdapter3::QueryVideoMemoryInfo` (`MemoryBreakdown::local_usage`) is the in-process view of the same VidMm accounting. Measured on the D3D11 default, release port, bench fixture, RX 9070 XT, same instant: counter 45.9 MiB, DXGI 45.4 MiB (0.5 MiB apart, the counter being the larger). The counter gates; DXGI is for attribution inside the process. The 61 to 65 MiB figures of rounds 7 and 8 were the D3D12 adapter.

Ledger (`aqua-gpu-d3d11::ledger`, already present) at the settled state, 23.0 MiB of Aqua allocations: swapchain 16.3 (2 buffers, BGRA8, the floor), image atlas 4.9 (six RGBA8 pages; the round-7 attribution in section 11 names five of them as the composer glass paths at about 1 MiB each), Slug store 1.0, transients 0.6, instance buffer 0.25. DXGI reads 45.4, so about 21.3 MiB is driver memory: 12.6 to 13.8 is the bare device plus swapchain floor measured by an Aqua-free window test (30.06 MiB with a 1898x1125 window), and about 2 MiB per three further shader programs (the first blur frames add 2.25 MiB for 0.23 MiB of transients). A 4 MiB `UpdateSubresource` adds exactly 4.0 MiB and leaves no staging behind, so uploads are not hidden cost.

Decisions. (1) Layer, blur and filter transients that the planner emits as `Rgba16Float` are stored as `Bgra8Unorm` when the target is the BGRA8 swapchain (`lifetimes::Precision::EightBit`, chosen per frame in `Renderer::record` from the target format and always for windows); compute-written transients keep their format because BGRA8 has no guaranteed typed UAV. This is GPUI's own intermediate precision (6.8) and also lets the backdrop seed copy use `CopySubresourceRegion` (same format as the surface). The transient peak halves (2.09 to 1.04 MiB on the fixture) and every pixel shader now sees one render-target format instead of two. Conformance on the 8-bit variants (WARP, RX 9070 XT, the integrated GPU), differential, debug layer and the 58 window tests pass unchanged; the float-surface variants are untouched. 64 px rounding of transient extents was not done: layers are already sized to their clipped bounds and the pool reuses equal shapes, so rounding would only enlarge them. (2) Transients were released only by frame-count aging, which never runs at rest, so an idle window kept its glass layers forever. The render service's wall-clock idle trim (`IDLE_TRIM_AFTER`, 2 s) now also calls `GpuFrames::with_idle_release`, installed by `native::gpu` as `Trim { shrink_upload_ring, free_transients, evict_idle_resources }` on the backend after the forced frame (after, not before, because the forced frame itself needs its layers). `AQUA_LEDGER=1` prints the ledger and DXGI usage before and after that release; with `AQUA_MEMORY_REPORT=1` the report runs after it as well. Measured: transient textures go from 16 live (0.62 MiB) to 0 at the idle release. DXGI usage does not drop for 0.6 MiB (the D3D11 driver returns freed textures lazily), so this is hygiene for hidden or idle windows, not a scoreboard gain.

Result on the scoreboard rows (main phase, one run, same session as GPUI): GPU dedicated after startup 45.9 against GPUI 49.1 (pass); after 600 scrolls 51.9 against 51.1 (fail by 0.8 MiB). The growth is attributed with `AQUA_LEDGER=1` on a stats-off run (the memory-report mode hides it): DXGI usage goes from 47.3 to 49.3 MiB between the idle release at boot and the one after the scroll while the live ledger is unchanged (six RGBA8 atlas pages, 4.876 MiB) and the transients are 0 and 0.6 MiB. What changed is the created counters: `persistent_rgba8` 16 to 21 and the Slug store 2 to 3, that is, atlas pages and the store texture are destroyed and recreated while the scene scrolls (images and glass paths entering and leaving), and the D3D11 driver does not hand the destroyed allocations back (the same happened for the 0.6 MiB of transients, whose release changes neither meter). GPUI grows by 2.0 MiB over the same scroll (49.1 to 51.1), so the churn costs the same order on both. Boot readings vary by up to 4 MiB between runs (45.9 and 49.9 on the same exe), so one run per row is not enough to claim 3 percent; the scoreboard needs three runs on a quiet machine for this row.

Round 11 attribution for private bytes after the scroll (memory-report run, one exe, stage lines `executed_N`): the step of +2.6 MiB private and +2.0 MiB write-combined between the idle trim and the next scroll was the Slug store. `TrimRequest::Everything` and a font revision change called `SlugStore::reset`, which forgot the GPU texture, so the next upload emitted a `Create` for the same handle at the same size; the old texture went to the persistent pool for ten seconds (the pool check ran before the replaced entry was retired, so `reused` stayed 0) and the driver did not return it (`slug_created` 2 and the Slug store is 1.0 MiB). The fix has two parts. `SlugStore::clear` drops the CPU texels and entries but keeps the GPU texture, which is then rewritten in place (no `Create` or `Release`; the shrink rule no longer fires on an empty store); the full `reset` stays for device recovery. The D3D11 persistent store now reuses an entry in place when a `Create` repeats the shape of the handle's current texture. Re-measurement: `slug_created` stays 1 over the same scroll and the +2.6 MiB step is gone. Per-page ledger labels now separate `persistent_*_small` from `persistent_*_page` (over 512 KiB). Measured and not changed: the 5.9 MiB of RGBA8 pages are five 1000x250 path rasters, and four of the five are gradient fills or strokes, so they cannot move to the R8 mask atlas (the glyph shader applies the text tone curve to coverage, which would change anti-aliasing, and gradients need colour); only the solid ones (about 1.8 MiB) could, and that needs a plain-mask draw path in the protocol. Rounding page extents to 64 px so the pool could match them was tried and showed no change (the pages are created one frame before the old ones are released, so the pool never sees both), and was removed. The remaining startup churn (ten page creations, five live) comes from path rasters re-made at the initial window resize.

Not done and why: moving the composer glass paths to the R8 mask atlas (`emit_path` always uses `VectorMode::Color`; solid fills would save about 3.6 MiB) needs a mask draw path with a per-instance colour in the lowering and a conformance case, and no measurement yet shows the atlas pages are what the scroll adds; no atlas page-policy or Slug store change was made. The next lever, backed by the created counters above, is to stop destroying and recreating persistent textures: pool released textures by (extent, format) in `PersistentTextures` for a wall-clock interval, and grow the Slug store without a release-and-create. It needs a ledger-instrumented before and after on the scoreboard.

### 14.SC Main-thread cost of streaming (decided 2026-11-05, round 9; `editor-aqua::shell::live_throttle`, `shell::host`, `shell::application`, `shell::root`, `conversation::view::factory`, `tools/compare-editors.sh`)
- **Evidence (release, Windows, fixture `bench-stream`, 24 s, `AQUA_COMPARE_PHASES=streamprof`, which now runs `threads stream_threads 8` instead of `wakes` and aggregates every `Tm` timing as a per-second span in `rebuild_stats`).** The scenario is 723 upserts of one running assistant message every 33 ms (`HostEvent::Item`), not `HostEvent::Live` deltas. Per second before: 43 to 45 whole-view rebuilds (`rb_total` 27 to 31 ms: `rb_refresh` 21, `rb_build` 8), 49 to 54 frames (`ext`, which holds layout, 31 to 35 ms), `on_fc` 12 to 14 ms, `on_wake` 28 to 31 ms; main thread 78 to 88 ms CPU/s, `aqua-render` 19 to 43, the AMD driver thread 6, `forge-net` 4. `f_realize` is 3.7 ms/s (140 realizations), so re-realizing rows is not the cost; the whole-tree rebuild and refresh behind each upsert is. Rebuild reasons: `shell_updates` 31 to 34 per second, the rest `after_layout` and `reveals`.
- **Decision 1: stream updates rebuild at most every 66 ms (`LiveThrottle`).** A watched `HostEvent::Live` or a running assistant-message `HostEvent::Item` is applied to the conversation model at once (`Conversation::core_event`), but the view rebuild it asks for runs on the first event after a quiet interval and then at most once per 66 ms; a deferred update requests an animation deadline, and `externals` posts the wake mailbox when it is due so `Shell::drain` reports it. Any other rebuild satisfies a pending deferral (`flushed`). Terminal item updates, tool items and every other event stay immediate. The reveal releases words every 150 ms, so the visible cadence is unchanged; fades step at the rebuild cadence (about 15 Hz instead of 30 Hz). Rejected: advancing fades in place in `sync_contents` (each row wraps its text in a clip box whose height is baked at realization, so a line reveal needs a re-realization anyway, and realization is not where the time goes).
- **Decision 2: `RowFactory::after_layout` separates repaint from rebuild** (`AfterLayout { repaint, rebuild }`; `sync_after_layout` still returns the union). Notes `al_shimmer`, `al_compaction`, `al_reveal`, `al_stored`, `al_motion` name the cause of an after-layout rebuild. Measured cause: `al_motion` (`RowMotion::animating`: the work header phrase rolls its elapsed time for 0.66 s after each change), 9 per second throughout the stream, not the status shimmer. Rebuilds caused by motion are paced to 33 ms (`MOTION_REBUILD_INTERVAL`). This did not change the rebuild count (19 to 20 per second) because the throttled stream rebuilds already bound it.
- **Measured after (same harness, interleaved with the old executable, same session, machine shared with other agents).** Rebuilds 43 to 45 down to 19 to 20 per second; `rb_total` 27.5 to 28.4 down to 13.8 to 13.9 ms/s; `ext` 30 to 32 down to 22 ms/s; `on_fc` 12.4 to 13.2 down to 7.8 to 8.3; `on_wake` 28 to 29 down to 17. Main thread CPU 72 and 84 (old) against 60 and 70 (new) in the paired runs of the final executable, 78 against 49 and 51 for the first throttled build. Gated row `streaming CPU` (three-run medians, old and new alternating, private fixture port): old 11.16 and 11.27, new 8.01 and 8.49; the metric moves by 70 percent with machine load between sessions (round-8 start 16.8, 9.5 to 10.9 on a quieter hour), so only the interleaved pairs count. The target of 10 percent or lower holds in both pairs.
- **Cost.** `streaming latency p99` (SendMessage round trip with the UI thread) rose from 0.20 to 0.21 ms to 0.33 to 0.34 ms (p50 unchanged at 0.06 to 0.07); with probe gaps of 23 and 37 ms in profile mode 0.24 to 0.27 against 0.41 to 0.46, so it is not probe aliasing. GPUI measured 3.0 ms in round 8. Maximum span per second per phase is unchanged (`on_wake` 1.1, `rb_total` 0.9 to 1.0 ms), so the likely cause is fewer and fatter bursts (rebuild, layout and frame completion in one turn); not resolved.
- **Not done.** (1) Scroll blit or retained layer for glide frames: the render thread spends 27 to 43 ms/s (about 50 frames per second, lower 0.14, plan 0.11, record 0.18, present 0.20 ms) with most frames repainting the whole viewport; that needs backend support in `aqua-gpu-d3d11` and was not attempted. (2) A subtree refresh in `aqua-ui` would remove most of `rb_refresh` (0.47 ms per rebuild). (3) The visual gate was taken on the Editor port only (the streamed frame, composer glass, shadows and text equal the pre-change executable); no renderer code changed, and the GPUI side-by-side was not repeated. (4) The fixture scoreboard lock was held for hours by other agents, so these runs used a private copy of `compare-editors.sh` with its own lock, fixture root and port 47971; the gated scoreboard itself was not re-run. Tests: `tests/shell/live_throttle.rs` (4) and `tests/shell/host.rs` (4) pass on Windows.

### 14.LP Legacy stack purge audit (decided 2026-11-05, round 10; `Cargo.toml`, `aqua-core`, `aqua-text`, `aqua-verify::policy`, `editor-aqua-port` manifests)

- **Audit.** Every case-insensitive hit for `wgpu`, `winit`, `glyphon`, `cosmic` and `fontdb` in the aqua repository (excluding `target` and `Cargo.lock`) was read. `Cargo.lock` and every manifest are clean (`aqua-verify` `banned`, `editor_port_manifest_declares_no_banned_crate`, `editor_port_closure_reaches_no_banned_crate`). Real dependencies: none. Remaining hits are enforcement data (`policy.rs` ban lists, `banned.rs` source tokens), history in this document and in the two early design documents (`GUI_FRAMEWORK_DESIGN.md`, `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, kept as superseded history), and the type names `ShapedMeasurer` and `ProfiledMeasurer` in `aqua-ui` and `aqua-layout` (identifiers of aqua's own measurer, no `cosmic-text` code; a rename touches `aqua-ui`, `aqua-runtime` and the port and is left to the owners of those crates).
- **Reworded.** Doc comments in `aqua-core` (`lib.rs`, `event.rs`) no longer name the removed libraries; the `aqua-text` test `backgrounds_follow_pinned_reference_painter_for_clusters_bidi_and_wraps` and the `aqua-verify` `lint_scanner` fixtures use neutral names (`banned_gpu`, `banned_window`); `aqua/tests/facade_pump.rs` header likewise.
- **Editor port.** `editor-aqua` (the port crate) reaches no banned crate in its dependency graph with or without the `qualification` feature (`cargo tree` shows none; `port-win-build --features qualification` builds). `editor-aqua-port/Cargo.lock` still lists `wgpu`, `cosmic-text` and `fontdb`: they are the GPUI reference Editor's own dependencies (`gpui_platform` with `cosmic-text`, and the `windows-wgpu` feature of the `editor` crate), reachable only through the optional `source-parity` feature (`editor-ui` and GPUI) and by the GPUI `editor` binary that the scoreboard measures as the baseline. They are not Aqua dependencies and are kept: removing them would change the baseline the port is measured against. The `editor` crate's `windows-wgpu` feature and the `editor-ui` markdown view comment belong to the GPUI editor and were not edited.
- **Enforcement.** `ADAPTER_ROOT_ITEMS` now declares `pool` (`aqua-gpu-d3d11`) and `pointer_source` (`aqua-shell-win32`); the enforcement suite passes 9 of 9 on Linux.
- **Formatting.** `cargo fmt` was applied to `aqua-core` and `aqua-text`; the other crates are left to the integrator once no agent is mid-edit (counts in 13.1 item 13).
- **Not done.** No round 10 scoreboard or visual gate was run by this package; row 12 and 13.1 quote `docs/scoreboard/round-9-end`.

### 14.SP Round 10 CPU per scroll input (decided 2026-11-05; `aqua-ui::element`, `aqua-ui::tree`, `aqua-ui::binding`, `aqua-ui::collection_runtime`, `aqua-ui::events`, `editor-aqua::conversation::view`, `editor-aqua::ui`)

Evidence. Fixture scroll, release port, `AQUA_PROFILE=1` spans: the app thread re-emitted the whole scene on every glide frame (`bf.emit_keyed` 202 of 202 frames, 57 ms per second, plus scene reconcile 11, text runs 6, dynamic items 14) although round 9 retained scroll emit exists. Cause, found with a temporary trace at the point that marks a scroll slot volatile: every `Canvas` painter inside scrolled content marks all scroll ancestors volatile (it is called with scroll-cancelled screen coordinates), and the transcript rows carry one `on_paint` probe canvas per row that only reports its bounds and returns no items (`painted`, body observer, observed row), plus pure icon, glass and code-card canvases. One such canvas in the emit window disables scroll patching. Per-thread CPU during 600 wheel inputs: main 173 ms/s, render 149 ms/s, driver 29 ms/s (6125 ms process CPU, 9.6 ms per input, 4.3 frames per input because the glide renders every refresh).

Decisions. (1) `Canvas::on_observe(|tree, node| ...)` registers a bounds observer that is not a painter: `UiTree::run_observers` calls it once per build frame in tree order whenever `content_version` changed since the last run (layout, scroll patch, style), after layout and before emission, and never marks a scroll volatile. `UiTree::observe_clip(node)` gives the viewport intersected with the clipping ancestors for visibility reporting. The transcript row probes and the media-frame visibility canvas moved from `on_paint` and `on_paint_context` to `on_observe`. (2) `Canvas::scroll_stable()` declares a legacy painter whose items depend only on its bounds: it is called with its content-space rectangle, is not wrapped in the inverse scroll translate and does not make scroll ancestors volatile. Used for icons, glyphs, glass layers, code card layers, callouts and stats cells; painters that read screen positions (probes, backdrop capture, anything storing the rectangle) must not use it. (3) Realized-window hysteresis (`collection_runtime::REALIZED_WINDOW_HYSTERESIS`, 0.25 of the pixel overscan, only when `overscan_pixels` is set): the mounted window is kept while it covers the needed window and stays inside needed plus twice the hysteresis on each side independently; when one edge leaves that band it is reset to needed plus the hysteresis, so the leading edge advances in chunks and the trailing edge trims in chunks. The first window is exact. (4) `MountedReveals::sync` skips rows that are ready and idle while neither the layout version nor the structure epoch changed (`settled_at`), and `events::hit_node` prunes subtrees whose clip rectangle does not contain the point and computes visual rectangles only for clipping nodes.

Measured (Windows, release, RX 9070 XT, fixture, 600 wheel inputs, `scrollthreads` and `spikes` diagnostic phases, one run each, shared machine). Volatile traces: 86 samples per run before (all probes, ops 0) and 0 after flagging the last pure painter (code card layers). `emit_keyed` frames per second 202 of 202 to 9 of 190; app thread CPU 173 to 87 ms/s; render thread 149 to 127 ms/s; driver thread 29 to 37 ms/s; scoreboard main phase, two runs after (the same method as the 9.48 to 9.61 ms baseline): CPU per scroll input 5.78 and 5.73 ms, scroll p50 0.091 and 0.092 ms (baseline 0.09 to 0.10), p90 0.178 and 0.169 ms (0.31 to 0.33), p99 0.302 and 0.279 ms (0.46), max 1.02 ms (0.60 to 0.75), nothing over 4 ms; `fc.reveals` 22.3 to 1.5 ms/s; `wheel.hit` 14.5 to 2.8 ms/s. Harness (`aqua-runtime/tests/collection_scroll_build.rs`, 600 steps of 70 px, 413 nodes, ignored report): hysteresis halves structural events (306 to 179) and lowers the total from about 86 to 70-96 ms but raises the worst structural frame from 0.66 to 1.3-1.8 ms (larger batches), which stays under the scroll p99 gate by a wide margin. Tests: `aqua-runtime/tests/scroll_patching.rs` (legacy painter forces re-emit, scroll-stable painter and observer keep patching, observer sees each scroll, observers quiet without change), `aqua-ui/tests/collection_runtime.rs::realized_window_holds_until_the_needed_window_escapes_then_expands_ahead`.

Not met. CPU per input is 5.7 ms against the 3.5 ms target and p50 0.09 ms against 0.05 ms: the render thread spends 0.73 ms p50 per glide frame (lower 0.25, plan 0.23, record 0.12, present 0.23) on 165 frames per second even though the scene diff is one item, and the glide renders 4.3 frames per input where GPUI renders one. Reducing either is outside this package (render-thread retained lowering, glide cadence in the port).

### 14.PF Startup private bytes and D3D11 device flags (decided 2026-11-06, round 11; `aqua-gpu-d3d11::hal::adapter`, `aqua-gpu-d3d11::hal::engine`, `aqua::native::gpu`)

Per-stage private bytes (release port, `AQUA_MEMORY_REPORT=1`, `AQUA_PROFILE=1`): the D3D11 device adds about 16 MiB, the eight startup shaders about 12 MiB together (Quad 0, Shadow 0.8, Glyph 0.5, Slug 2.6, Image 1.2, ImageReplace 2.0, BlurDown 1.1, BlurUp 2.3; the bytecode is 1 to 9 KB each, so the cost is driver per object, not bytecode size, and DXBC optimisation flags cannot change it), and the second frame adds about 18 MiB (13 MiB write-combined). Lazy BlurDown, BlurUp and ImageReplace save nothing: the persisted first-frame hint (`+BlurDown +BlurUp +ImageReplace`) shows the bench thread draws all three on its first frame, and shaders already build from that hint only.

`D3D11_CREATE_DEVICE_PREVENT_INTERNAL_THREADING_OPTIMIZATIONS` is now an explicit `DeviceOptions::prevent_internal_threading` (env `AQUA_D3D11_PREVENT_THREADING=1`), default off. A/B on the bench fixture (private runs, phase main, 4 runs per arm interleaved, `WSLENV` pass-through): private after startup 97.0 to 97.8 MiB off, 90.6 to 91.6 on (-6.5 MiB); after 600 scrolls 97.8 to 99.0 off, 91.8 to 94.9 on; scroll p50 0.11 0.11 0.15 0.11 ms off against 0.11 0.17 0.14 0.18 on; cold start to first content 208 230 223 232 ms off against 295 219 229 245 on; GPU dedicated unchanged (38.9 MiB). GPUI on the same machine and fixture: private 115.5 and 119.0 MiB, GPU dedicated 49.1 and 51.1 MiB. The default build already sits 18 MiB below GPUI, so the flag buys a margin that is not needed and costs scroll p50 and cold start where Aqua is closest to losing; it stays off. `AQUA_D3D11_SINGLETHREADED` together with the flag measured 102 to 108 MiB in the win-app method against 101 to 105 for the flag alone, so it adds nothing.

### 14.IT Idle OS threads: attribution and lazy toast notifier (decided 2026-11-06, round 11; `aqua-shell-win32::notifications`, `aqua-shell-win32::drop_target`, `aqua-shell-win32::occlusion`)

Attribution by bisecting a bare test process (per-thread `QueryThreadCycleTime` sampled every 50 ms, new threads described by start module through `NtQueryInformationThread`). A DXGI factory creates no thread. `D3D11CreateDevice` on the RX 9070 XT creates 16 `amdxx64` driver threads and 3 `ntdll` thread-pool workers. `OleInitialize` and `CoCreateInstance(CLSID_DragDropHelper)` create none; `RegisterDragDrop` creates one `combase` RPC worker and two pool workers (ole32 marshals the `IDropTarget` so other processes can reach it). `NotificationCenter::new` created another `combase` worker and four pool workers (`RoInitialize` plus `ToastNotificationManager::CreateToastNotifierWithId`). UIA attach creates none (`AQUA_EXP_NO_UIA` changes nothing). The Editor port therefore started two RPC workers and about nine pool workers before any content was visible, which matches the `combase` and `ntdll` threads in the scoreboard idle breakdown.

Those threads do not wake periodically for ever: pool workers retire on 30 s idle timeouts, so a registration produces wakes at about +30 s, +60 s, +63 s (RPC worker), +67 s and +134 s, and a pair near +300 s, then nothing (400 s bare-process trace). Sampling the Editor for 90 s after the thread opened: before this change one `combase` thread woke twice and three pool threads once each beyond the always-present pool and `mswsock` threads (11 to 12 wakes in 90 s from those threads); after it, the `combase` thread and two pool threads no longer wake (2 wakes), 4 interleaved before and after pairs (two with `AQUA_STATS_OVERRIDE=off`; a third pair lost its after-run to a machine stall).

Decision: the toast notifier is created lazily. `NotificationCenter::new` keeps the registry identity, protocol registration, receiver window and explicit AppUserModelID (no threads); the apartment and the notifier are created on the first `show`, and `withdraw` initialises them only when something is active in this process or a toast has ever been shown by this identity (`EverShown` DWORD under the AppUserModelId key), so stale toasts from an earlier session are still cleared. Test: `aqua-shell-win32/tests/notification_threads.rs` asserts that creating the centre and withdrawing before any toast starts no thread.

Not done, with the evidence: (1) The OLE drop target stays registered. External file drags with hover, move and leave events are specified in section 7 and cannot be received without `RegisterDragDrop`; `DragAcceptFiles` (WM_DROPFILES) would remove the three threads but loses hover feedback, and registering on first input, first activation or first desktop mouse capture would only make the benchmark pass while real sessions pay the same wakes. The GPUI Editor registers the same drop target, so both applications carry this transient. (2) Moving the occlusion hooks to a dedicated thread. The hooks are already off while visible and, while covered, scoped to the occluding windows' process and thread plus system foreground and minimise events. A relocated hook thread receives the same events, so process-wide context switches do not change; only the main thread's share moves. The wakes seen in the covered private runs came from other processes' window activity (`winevent:0x8001/2/3`) and from sent `WM_GETICON`/`WM_NCHITTEST` queries, not from idle application work. (3) A quiet 60 s zero-wake window could not be demonstrated: the machine load was 30 to 50 % from other agents throughout, and private-root runs showed 5 to 12 switches/s on the main and `forge-net` threads that the official scoreboard (quiet machine) does not show (0.07/s in round 10). That residual needs a re-run on a quiet machine with `AQUA_WAKE_STATS` attribution before further work.

### 14.SG Round 11 scroll glide cadence and scroll p50 (decided 2026-11-06; `aqua::render::cadence`, `aqua::render::host`, `aqua::render::link`, `aqua::runtime`, `aqua-protocol::window`, `aqua-gpu-d3d11::window::backend`, `aqua-gpu-d3d11::hal::swapchain`, port `shell::wheel`)

Evidence. Fixture scroll, release port, 600 wheel inputs at a 16 ms post gap on the 165 Hz RX 9070 XT (`AQUA_PROFILE=1` phase summaries and process CPU from `cpu` probes, settled 30 s after open): the render thread ran 165 frames per second (lower 0.13, plan 0.19, record 0.30, present 0.10 ms, so about 0.75 ms per frame) and the app thread 205 frames per second (0.25 ms, of which input 0.20), 3.9 render frames per wheel input. Process CPU per input was 5.1 to 5.5 ms; user time was two thirds of it and kernel time (present, waits) the rest, so the cost scales with presented frames. The synthetic lowering benchmark (`perf_lower`) is 7 us lower plus 35 us plan for 90 commands and is not representative of the 600-command real scene.

Decision. While a wheel glide is active the port asks the window to present every second vsync (`WindowRequest::SetGlideCadence`, sent from `WheelGlides::cadence_change` on the active edge only). The render thread owns the decision: `Cadence` keeps the median of the last nine presented-to-presented gaps (full-rate presents only, 2 to 20 ms) and allows half rate only when that period is below 7.5 ms (displays above about 133 Hz) and a glide is flagged. The half rate is `Present1(2, ...)` on the flip-model swapchain (`PresentMode::HalfVsync`, per surface), not a timer, so the frame latency waitable releases every second vsync and frames stay phase-locked to the display; a 60 or 120 Hz display never halves. D3D12 ignores the request. Measured wait phase went from 5.2 to 11.3 ms per render frame, render frames 165 to 83 per second, app frames 205 to 119 per second.

Measured (same fixture and method, base and new exes interleaved, machine shared with other jobs): CPU per scroll input 5.10, 5.10, 5.52 ms before and 3.12, 3.18, 3.12 ms after the final build (earlier builds of the same change: 2.94, 2.97, 3.18; under heavy load the base read 4.6 to 7.2 ms against 3.1 to 3.2 for the new exe), against the GPUI release editor at 3.7 ms on the scoreboard. Scroll p90 0.21 to 0.23 ms and p99 0.36 ms are unchanged.

Not met: scroll p50 stays 0.12 to 0.14 ms against GPUI 0.06 to 0.08 ms. The probe is the `SendMessageTimeout(WM_NULL)` round trip. QPC traces in the app (wake return, wndproc entry and exit, loop marks, wheel dispatch) show the `WM_NULL` wndproc itself takes 0.4 us, the wheel dispatch 4 us, the reply path 17 us, and the wait between send and wndproc entry is 61 us when the UI thread is parked or just woke and 115 us when it is inside the turn (queue drain with inbox pump about 135 us, tick about 130 us), because sent messages are only serviced at message API calls and `checkpoint::reach` pumps (300 us spacing). Three experiments, all measured interleaved against the previous build, did not move it: a `PeekMessage(PM_QS_SENDMESSAGE)` between queue items and before the tick (0.121 to 0.138 against 0.128 to 0.160 ms), a checkpoint spacing of 100 us (0.122 and 0.142 against 0.131 and 0.145 ms), and a spacing of 40 us with extra checkpoints in the port wheel offer, which made the probe read 4.1 ms constant and CPU per input rise to 3.5 to 4.5 ms and was reverted without finding the cause. All three were reverted. The remaining gap is the thread-state dependent wait inside the turn plus about 50 us of fixed wake cost, not wheel handling.

Not done: retained plan and record replay for unchanged glyph runs (per-glyph instance materialisation in `draw_glyphs`, about 0.2 ms plan and 0.3 ms record per frame, needs a run-relative instance format across `aqua-graph` and the D3D11 backend) and app frame coalescing for input frames (119 app frames against 83 render frames).


### 14.VC Monitor vblank pacing across idle periods (2026-11-06 to 2026-11-07, round 12)

The current worktree's D3D11 monitor clock fell back to the swapchain wait after three waits returned in under 1 ms, even when idle intervals separated those waits. A diagnostic captured 49 waits above 25 ms: 7764.62 ms in the swapchain wait versus 0.02 ms in the monitor clock, with a maximum swapchain wait of 260.79 ms. Resetting the fast-wait streak after one monitor interval of idle time removes that false fallback. Three rapid nonblocking waits still disable the clock, and a blocking wait still resets the streak.

The presentation-aware slot API forwards the current hint through the host and native wrapper. Only full-rate Vsync uses the monitor clock; immediate and half-rate frames retain their swapchain wait. A present consumes its clocked result once, and monitor or factory reinitialization resets the guard. Clocked frames use non-tearing `Present1(0, 0)`. The clock follows the window's monitor and retries unavailable outputs after one second. This change packages the existing monitor-clock work with these corrections; the original debug-panel checkout is untouched, and unrelated debug and idle-trim changes are excluded from the PR.

Validation: all 80 render tests, six transport tests and ten pacing tests pass on Windows. The regressions cover sparse fast waits, rapid consecutive fast waits, a blocking wait resetting the streak, selecting the mode before acquisition, and forwarding the current hint. Windows library clippy passes across the protocol, transport, D3D11 backend and host with warnings denied; the D3D12 host configuration passes its Windows library check. Formatting and diff checks pass. The corrected release build's focused trace matched all 145 inputs at p50 1.46 ms, p99 2.22 ms and maximum 2.31 ms, with none above 8.3 ms. Focused traces and source manifests are tracked in `docs/scoreboard/round-12/`.

Completed joint scoreboard: `docs/scoreboard/round-12/joint/` records three interleaved release runs, alternating editor order, using one frozen Editor source and a frozen current Aqua source with the reconciliation, frame-slot, timing-span and monitor-clock fixes. The source manifest identifies the uncommitted baseline and applied changes; the harness's shared-checkout git row is explicitly overridden. All runs completed without retries or foreground changes and passed the quiet-machine check. Aqua passes 30 of 40 performance rows under the existing 3% rule; the overall verdict is FAIL. Median key-present p50 is 1.56 versus GPUI 17.68 ms, p99 2.15 versus 20.34 ms, and maximum 2.40 versus 20.37 ms. Every one of 435 evaluated inputs matched, with none above 8.3 ms. CPU per scroll input is 0.83 versus 4.12 ms; scroll p99 is 0.36 versus 2.70 ms.

Ten performance rows remain open: scroll p50; busiest idle-thread context switches (0.10 versus 0.10 per second); private memory after startup and scrolling; dedicated GPU memory after startup and scrolling; the two first-content capture rows; resize p50; and streaming p50. GPU startup memory is numerically lower at 47.9 versus 49.1 MiB but misses the required 3% margin. Private memory after scrolling is 130.3 versus 118.1 MiB; the absolute 142 MiB windowed budget still passes. The previous joint attempt stopped at key validation, and the first corrected attempt was intentionally stopped after one main scenario per editor to fix presentation modes; neither is an additional completed scoreboard.

The visual gate fails only its checked jump-to-latest regions: the button is absent from Aqua at depths 1 through 4, producing over 95% difference against a 30% region limit. The worst whole-image difference is 14.32%, below the 18% limit. All action checks change their frames, including turn navigation and image lightboxes. The depth-1 screenshot pair and the full pixel-diff report are tracked under `docs/scoreboard/round-12/joint/visual/`.

The Editor follow-up, draft PR [516](https://github.com/artisanstreet/editor/pull/516), fixes the omitted control refresh when leaving tail-follow mode. Its regression fails before the fix and passes after; all three Windows root tests pass on the committed Editor master base. The release visual rerun is recorded under `docs/scoreboard/round-12/jump-latest/`. The button appears at every checked position, and notch1 plus depths 1–3 pass with 0.2%, 1.9%, 2.8% and 2.9% changed pixels. Depth4 still fails at 96.6%: its transcript is approximately 127 physical pixels higher in Aqua, so GPUI has text behind the glass button while Aqua has empty space. The unchanged visual gate remains FAIL, with the worst whole-image difference 15.366% against the 18% limit. All functional action checks pass and neither editor changes the foreground window. This follow-up has no new performance scoreboard; the preceding 30/40 result remains the measured joint result.

Disabling window redirection, using composition for opaque windows, removing the queue limit with composition, forcing the surface mirror, and disabling GPU timing queries did not remove the latency tail. Those experiments were rejected; their hashes and traces are recorded in `docs/scoreboard/round-12/rejected-variants.json`. Repository CI still stops before tests because its workflow requests the removed `aqua-macros` package on both platforms; the local validation above ran the actual affected packages.
